# Hive Desktop — Specification (v0.2)

Hive Desktop (Hive for short) is a desktop app for AI-assisted ("vibe") coding. It looks and feels like a streamlined VS Code, but its centre of gravity is the **agent session**, not the text editor. It organises code into Workspaces and Projects, runs CLI agent sessions (Claude Code and Codex, up to twelve agents per active project by default, up to 24 with Settings → Advanced → Agents per project), and manages the skills, MCP servers and shared notes those sessions use.

This document is the source of truth for product decisions. Update it when decisions change.

---

## 1. Goals and non-goals

**Goals**
- Open or create a Workspace; list its Projects in a left-hand pane.
- Run Claude Code and Codex sessions per active Project in embedded terminals, several at once; each agent chooses its provider, so a project can mix them.
- Show per-session stats: tokens, cache use, compactions, estimated re-cache cost on resume.
- Review and edit the agent's memory for a project.
- Manage Hive skills and MCP servers at workspace ("global") scope with project-level opt-outs.
- Per-project model, effort and permission mode, with global defaults.
- Indicate session state (working / waiting / finished) with optional chime and desktop notifications.
- Review changes (git diff) made by the agent.
- Expose an Agent API (HTTP + built-in MCP server) so agents can interact with Hive.
- Minimise and close to the system tray. Ship as a Windows installer.

**Non-goals (for now)**
- A full code editor. Monaco is used for notes, memory, skills and diffs.
- Providers beyond Claude Code and Codex. The provider framework (§10) is built for more; each needs its own adapter.
- Moving a conversation between providers. Work moves through a handover (**Hand Over to…**, §6.2).
- Automatic session restore. Resuming is always a user action.
- Cloud sync, accounts, multi-user. A remote skill repository is a future idea.

---

## 2. Technology

| Area | Choice |
|---|---|
| Shell | Electron, TypeScript throughout, built with electron-vite |
| UI | React + zustand, VS Code-style layout, `@vscode/codicons` |
| Terminal | `xterm.js` with its WebGL renderer (DOM fallback) + `node-pty` prebuilt (`@lydell/node-pty`, Windows ConPTY) |
| Editor / diff | Monaco (bundled locally, no CDN) |
| File watching | `chokidar` (the workspace's `.hive`), Node's `fs.watch` (the workspace root, the Files tab) |
| File access (main) | Electron's `original-fs`: `.asar` files in projects are plain files, never opened as archives (#246) |
| Docs rendering | `marked` |
| Packaging | `electron-builder` (NSIS installer) |
| Tests | Vitest (unit, in CI) and Playwright `_electron` end-to-end suites (`tests/e2e`, local: they need the CLIs) |
| Checks | TypeScript, oxlint (`.oxlintrc.json`; typescript-eslint doesn't support TypeScript 7 yet), GitHub Actions on every push to `main` and on pull requests |

Process model: the **main process** owns the filesystem, ptys, hook server, Agent API and agent adapters. The **renderer** is UI only and talks to main through a typed preload bridge (`contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, no `nodeIntegration`).

**Windows** (like VS Code): one Hive process, one window per workspace (or the welcome page). Settings, the tray, updates, the Agent API and file locks are shared; each window has its own workspace service. **File → New Window** (`window.new`, Ctrl+K Ctrl+N; VS Code's Ctrl+Shift+N is New Session in Hive). Open Workspace (or Create Workspace) replaces the current window's workspace; if its agents are running, the quit dialog's *Switch workspace?* variant asks first (per **Confirm on quit**, like quitting and closing a window), then they are stopped. **File → Close Workspace** does the same (*Close this workspace?*) and stops every agent of every project in it, keeping the window open (its tooltip says so). Cancel leaves everything running; a workspace already open in another window brings that window forward instead, so one project is never in two windows. Closing a window that isn't the last stops its workspace's agents after the quit dialog's window variant (*Close this window?*, no "when agents finish"); the last window keeps the old behaviour (tray, or quit). **File → Close Window** (`window.close`, Ctrl+Shift+W, just above Exit; also in the palette) is the window's X: it calls `window:close`, which goes through the same close handler. Quitting covers every window; unsaved files are asked about in each window that has them. With several windows open, File → Exit reads **Exit Hive (all windows)** (`Command.liveLabel`, from the window count main sends as `windows-changed` and `window:count`), and the quit dialog's title says it closes all N windows, its text that it stops the sessions in every workspace, and it lists the agents under their workspace's name (`QuitSession.workspace`), grouped by its full path (`workspacePath`, case-insensitive) so two workspaces with the same folder name stay apart: each of those also shows the fewest parent folders that tell them apart (`distinguishingParents`, `shared/folderLabels.ts`), and every heading has the full path on hover; a **Close this window only** row in its body (not the footer, which has no room beside Quit when agents finish; the footer wraps rather than overflow) answers quit choice `'window'`, which closes just the window showing the dialog through the Close Window path (`requestCloseWindow`), asking only about what that dialog didn't cover: from the main dialog nothing more (it listed the agents and dealt with the unsaved files); from the unsaved-files question asked first in another window, "Close this window?" about that window's agents, per Confirm on quit. It doesn't save "Don't ask again". A workspace can't be opened inside, or around, one another window shows (a project must belong to one workspace), nor by another name (an 8.3 short name, a junction) for one already open: Hive compares workspaces by real path (#455); an Agent API workspace name that two open workspaces share is refused (409) rather than guessed, and one named by its path (`X-Hive-Workspace`, `?workspace=`) is found by place, under any of its names (`findWorkspace`, `placeMatcher`, #494). Stopping a window's agents also cancels agent starts still being prepared, and turning a provider off lists its agents in every window. The open windows (workspace, bounds) are saved in `config.json` `windows` and reopened at start when *Reopen last workspace* is on; `lastWorkspace` stays the last focused window's for older versions. Events about a project, and its terminals' output, go to the window showing it; app-wide events (settings, updates, plan usage) to every window. **Always on Top**, per window (`main/pin.ts`): the title bar's pin (left of the window buttons, lit while on), **View → Always on Top** (`view.alwaysOnTop`, checked while on), the palette and Ctrl+Alt+O all call `window:setAlwaysOnTop` (`BrowserWindow.setAlwaysOnTop(true)`: above other apps, below system UI; no higher levels). It is remembered by workspace in `config.json` `alwaysOnTop` (lowercased paths), never in `.hive` (a desk preference, not the workspace's): a window takes its workspace's pin when the workspace opens in it, at start too, and is off on the welcome page and for a workspace never pinned.

Robustness and safety rules:

- **Hive's JSON files** (`project.json`, `sessions.json`, `config.json`…) are written to a temporary file and renamed into place, each write with its own temporary file, so a crash or two writes at once never leave a partial file. The files Hive keeps (`config.json`, `workspace.json`, `project.json`, `sessions.json`) also get a `.bak` copy of the last good version with every save; a file that can't be read is set aside as `<file>.corrupt-<time>`, the `.bak` restored and the user told (or, with no good copy, defaults used and the user told), so a damaged file never silently turns into lost settings or session records. Read-modify-write changes to one project's `project.json` or `sessions.json` are serialised, so changes made at the same moment (two agents finishing together) are all kept.
- **Session IDs** from the renderer, the Agent API or the file system are checked (letters, digits, `-`, `_`) before they are used in a file name.
- **Starting an agent** is reserved before anything else happens, so two starts at once (a double click, or the UI and the Agent API) start it only once; the other is refused.
- **The window** only uses the clipboard; every other permission a page can ask for is refused, and a build's CSP allows no network connections. IPC calls are accepted only from Hive's own top-level page (not an embedded preview). Paths from the window are checked against the workspace by their real location too (following links and junctions), for `file:read`/`file:write`, `hive-img:` and shared notes.
- **Files the renderer may open** outside the workspace are limited to what Hive shows from each provider's config folder: Claude Code's user `CLAUDE.md` and auto-memory files and Codex's user `AGENTS.md` (editable), and `SKILL.md` files (read-only), never credentials or settings.
- **The UI** catches errors per view: a failure in one tab or view shows a message there (**Try Again**, **Open Logs**) instead of blanking the window, and running sessions are unaffected.

Primary platform is Windows 11. Code avoids Windows-only assumptions where cheap.

---

## 3. Concepts

- **Workspace** — a folder that contains Projects. One workspace is open per window. Hive remembers recent workspaces: the 12 last opened (`config.json` `recentWorkspaces`), in File → Open Recent and on the welcome page (#144). Each entry says whether its folder is there (checked whenever the list is shown, and when the welcome page's window comes back to the front; one that isn't is greyed, "not found", and kept, since a drive may come back) and whether another window has it open (choosing it brings that window forward). An entry can be removed (its ✕, or right-click → Remove from Recent), comparing paths without case; **Clear Recently Opened** (File → Open Recent, and the palette) asks, then keeps only the workspaces open in a window. Opening a missing one says it can't be found and offers Remove from Recent. Every change (opened, removed, cleared, a workspace closed) is sent to every window (`recent-changed`), which asks for its own view of the list (`workspace:recent`). The welcome page fits a narrow window (#265): its two columns stack when there's no room for both, and an entry's path shortens first, then its name if that alone is too wide (the tooltip has the path).
- **Moved workspaces** (#146, `main/workspaceMove.ts`, paths in `shared/movePaths.ts`). Hive records where the workspace and each project were last opened (`lastPath` in workspace.json and project.json; recorded the first time a folder is opened with it). When a workspace opens and the folder differs (compared as places, by real path: the same folder by another name, an 8.3 short name or a junction, hasn't moved, #455), Hive works out in the background what still points to the old folder; if anything does, a banner says **This workspace was moved from …** (or that projects moved, when only a project folder was renamed or moved in) with **Repair…** (also `workspace.repairMove` in the palette while it's needed); if nothing does, the new folder is simply recorded. Repair… shows the plan (`workspace:movePlan`) and, on **Repair**, does it (`workspace:moveRepair`), refusing while any agent of the workspace (the Assistant included) runs: **worktrees**: each agent worktree is looked for moved along inside its project or the workspace, moved along in the worktrees folder beside the workspace (`<workspace>.worktrees`), then where it was; the first folder with a `.git` file wins; `git worktree repair <paths>` runs in the project, the result is checked (git lists it and its links are right both ways) and only then is `agents[].worktree.path` updated (`mutateProjectConfig`); one not found is listed with **Recreate worktree** (or **Create a new worktree** when its branch is gone, see below; made at its moved-along place, after the repairs above), **Locate…** (a folder picker; the folder must have a `.git` file; the plan is worked out again with it) or **Remove the agent's worktree link** (`worktree` dropped from the agent; nothing deleted); one still at its old place while the project's old folder exists too (a copy) is left to the original; **sessions**: `cwd`s under the old project folder or an old worktree path move to the new ones (`mutateSessions`); **per-path data**: for the project, and for each worktree now at a new place, kept in project.json (`pendingCopies`, written in the same locked change as the agent's new path) until its copy works, so a copy that fails is tried again by the next Repair, after a restart too, without repeating the git or session steps: the provider adapter's `copyPathData` (Claude Code: `~/.claude/projects/<path as name>/`, transcripts and `memory/`) copies to the new path's name for the project and each moved worktree (the Assistant's home too), never moving or deleting, never overwriting: a file already there with the same size and time counts as copied, one that differs is kept and listed; Codex keeps threads by id and has none; **Open Recent**: the old folder replaced by the new; **Working on**: the projects marked at the old folder (`activeProjects`, kept by folder path) marked at the new one too. A plan with files that couldn't be read, or only files already there with other content, still counts (`moveHasWork`): the banner shows it, failures stay pending, conflicts are listed once and never overwritten. The report lists what was done, skipped and failed (logged with `userText()`); Repair is complete only with nothing failed; a project is recorded as repaired (its `lastPath`) only when all its steps worked and nothing is left, the workspace when all are, so the banner stays until then and Repair can run again, repeating nothing done. Nothing at the old location is ever deleted. **Missing worktrees** (`main/agentWorktree.ts`): an agent whose worktree folder is gone, with its branch still in the repository, can have it made again: `git worktree prune`, then `git worktree add <path> <branch>` (no `-b`) at the recorded path (Repair: the moved-along place), or the agent's default worktree location if that can't be used; with the branch gone too, a new worktree on the same branch name from its base (else the project's current branch), as Add Agent makes one. Then the copied ignored files, `needsSetup` so the setup command runs at the next start, and the agent's path updated under the lock; at a new place, the same locked change records the old→new move in `pendingCopies`, so its sessions' `cwd`s and the CLIs' data follow it afterwards (Repair, or `finishPending()` after a Start's recreate) and a failure or exit in between leaves it to retry. Repair keeps a move pending when the sessions.json rewrite fails, too. A project with only such moves left gets the banner's third wording ("A worktree of x was made again at a new folder, and not everything has followed it yet"). Only when the folder is truly missing; refused while the agent runs, while git still lists the folder after the prune (locked), and when the prune would drop another missing worktree's entry (`git worktree prune --dry-run`), which a moved one still needs for `git worktree repair`, naming it (Repair passes the others it is recreating in the same run). Offered by Repair, by Start (New Session, Resume) with a confirm (`agents:missingWorktree`, `agents:recreateWorktree`; with the branch gone: Create a New Worktree or Remove the Worktree Link, `agents:unlinkWorktree`), and named in the error of a start that finds the folder missing. **File → Move Workspace…** was considered and not built: moving needs the old folder deleted, a cross-drive move of a workspace (and its worktrees) while terminals and watchers have it open is fragile, and Explorer does the move better; Repair handles what follows.
- **Project ids** (#508, `shared/projectIds.ts`). A project's stable id (`id` in its project.json, `p-` and 12 hex digits) is made once, when the workspace opens (`WorkspaceService.projectIds()`, hidden projects too; folders without Hive's files have none), and goes with the folder; Hive never drops it (older versions keep it too), and a project.json without it gets a new one, so that project's earlier cards are no project's. What the workspace keeps about a project names it by the id beside its folder name: cards (`projectId` after `project` in `.hive/tasks/<n>.json`), lanes (`lanes.json`), card watches (`watches.json`: the watcher's project, and each agent an agent watch follows) and the merge slot's record of holds. **The id decides whose a record is** (`projectNow`): read back, it is the project that has its id now (renamed or moved within the workspace since, names swapped or not, whatever project has its saved name now). With an id no project has (its project gone from the workspace), it keeps its saved name, and while another project has that name it shows as `<name>: gone` (never a folder name, so never that project's, nor in its agents' view; saved with its name as it was). Only a record saved before ids (no id) goes by its name. Saving a card writes its project's id; one with an id no project has keeps it, and none ever takes another project's. The ids are listed again when the workspace's folders change: at most once a second, at once after the workspace folder's watcher sees a rename (which also tells the board, `tasks-changed`, when an id is at another folder than where it was last seen while the workspace is open, so the window reads its cards again; compared with where it was last seen, not only with the read before, so a read part way through a rename, which misses the folder, or a project out of the workspace for a while loses no rename; of the projects a read missed, the 64 missed most recently are remembered, and all are forgotten when the workspace closes, #555), and at once for a lane or watch check, so a lane and its cards agree. The folders are compared with their project.json's identity and time, so a folder replaced by another of the same name, or two projects that swapped names, are read again. A rename can't reach a running agent's folder on Windows. A copied folder brings its project.json along: of the folders sharing an id, the one still where it was last opened keeps it (else the one with the same folder name), and the others are given new ids, so the copy starts with no cards. **One-time stamping**, each time the workspace opens (`stampCardProjects`, `stampLaneProjects`, `stampWatchProjects`): a record saved before ids whose name is a project of the workspace gets its id, and one whose project was renamed since gets its name now; a record with an id no project has, and one saved before ids under a name no project has, are left as they are. Cards under the board's lock, each in its own, read again there and written back with every field; lanes.json and watches.json in their locks. Each record changed keeps its content from before beside it once (`<n>.json.before-ids.bak`, `lanes.json.before-ids.bak`, `watches.json.before-ids.bak`; never replaced by a second run or a later change; a backup that can't be made stops that write). A second run changes nothing. A watch whose id is no project's while another project has its folder is set aside in the file (`invalid`), never that project's; an agent watch's watched agents follow their project's id the same way (their marks and fired agents re-keyed), and a watched agent whose project is gone while another project has its folder is marked `gone`: told as stopped, with nothing recorded at that folder (its status, events or replies) taken as its own, even when the new project has an agent of the same id. **The whole workspace moved** (#146): a watch and its agents saved under the workspace's old folder (workspace.json's `lastPath` as the workspace opened: `movedFrom`) are read as under its folder now, then placed by their ids in the workspace's own index, so they come to their projects at the new place (one saved before ids, and the Assistant's, by folder). Wherever else a watch was saved, its project id decides: an id in this workspace's index makes it that project's, at that project's folder now (always one of this workspace's), and the usual checks then apply as to any watch of that project (`followIds`, #536). One saved under another folder with no id, or with an id no project here has, stays set aside: a saved path outside the workspace is never taken as a project. A merge hold left in the record when Hive closed is told in the project its id is in now; one saved before ids, in the project at its folder (under the new place, if the workspace moved) or else the one project last opened there (project.json's `lastPath`); the holds recorded at the workspace's old folder (the record is by the workspace's folder) are told too when nothing is there now. A hold whose project can't be known (gone, or another project has its folder) is told in the workspace, saying so, and kept beside the record (`<record>.unplaced.json`, the latest 50); the record keeps its content from before beside it once (`.before-ids.bak`) when any hold is placed elsewhere. The Agent API and the board never show the id. **Records left under an old name** (saved before ids, by a project renamed since: the nine hive-website cards after website's rename, 10 Oct): cards, lanes and card watches without an id under a name no project of the workspace has (hidden and removed projects' names count as the workspace's) are given, by Repair, to the project Repair is moving from that name, else to the one project whose agents (project.json, or sessions in its sessions.json) worked on them (`orphanNames`: a card's agent and reviewer, a lane's builder and reviewer, a watch's watcher); one whose agents are no project's, or more than one's, is left alone. The banner and the plan list them (`MoveCards`, "9 cards of hive-website belong to website"); Repair moves each card still under that name and without an id in its lock, editing only its project, id and a history line by Repair (its other fields and `updatedAt` as they were, the backup kept as above), and likewise its lanes and watches. Repair also keeps a project renamed in place marked **Working on** under its new name. **What keeps a project by its folder name or path** (#514). Whose a record is goes by the project's id, never by a name alone. *The record of names* (`projectNames.ts`, `.hive/project-names.json`, a kept file, the workspace's and not the project's, so a copied folder brings none of it along): each project's folder name as Hive last saw it and its earlier ones, by id, and every rename, numbered, those seen in one read of the ids in one batch. After each read of the ids (`WorkspaceService.onIdsRead`), a project seen under another name than its record's was renamed (by id: two projects that swapped names are two renames, never copies); one not recorded yet is recorded under its folder name, or, one from before whose project.json's `lastPath` is another folder here that is no project now, as renamed from it; a copy just given an id of its own starts from its own name. *Owned by id* (never rewritten): **handovers** (`.hive/handover-owners.json`: file name → project id, written as Hive writes a handover, and carried to its new name when it is renamed in Notes (case only too, and to another type and back), in the record's lock, where adoption also lists the handovers, so neither loses it; forgotten only when its file is gone (a case changed outside Hive keeps it; a folder that can't be read forgets nothing); a handover is a `.md` file in any case, not the README; an older one, or one written by hand, is given its project once, from what it says. Its header's Project line names a folder name, which more than one project may have had (renamed, and the name taken by a new project): the projects that had it are that name's project now and those whose record has it, now or before (`ownersOfName`). The conversation its header names settles which (the one project whose sessions.json has it, if that project had the name); with none, only a name one project ever had counts. With no header, the one project whose name, now or before, its file name begins with (the longest). A header that names another project is never overruled by the file name, and evidence that disagrees or can't decide leaves it nobody's, never a guess, until something tells: a rename, or Repair, which records in the record of names that the project it gives a folder name's cards to had that name (before moving anything, so a Repair that fails part way, or a restart, loses none of it; Repair isn't done until those handovers and comparisons are given to it, and "Nothing to repair" only once they are), and **kept comparisons** (each project entry's `owner` in the index; an older one given its project once by its scope's name, and a choice of baseline and run kept by the project's id). A project's handovers (`handoversFor`: the session guidance, `hive_read_latest_handover` through `GET /v1/shared/handovers`, Hand Over to…, Remove from Hive's packing) and comparisons are those its id owns, so a renamed project keeps its own, and a project that takes a name another had, or a copy, never gets that one's. These are given owners at the workspace's first follow and after each change of the record of names. Ownership work stays in the workspace it began in: a window that opens another workspace while it waits (for the owners' lock, a file) makes it stop and write nothing. *Followed once, each rename* (`projectRename.ts` `applyRenames`, batch by batch, each batch all at once, so a swap keeps each project's own): the per-project view preferences kept by path (Skills tab provider and folds, Sessions tree, the .hive notice, the Assistant panel's folds; windows hear, `ui-pref-changed`) and Working on, with config.json's `renamesApplied` for the workspace; Hide or Remove from Hive (workspace.json's hiddenProjects, and its `renamesApplied`); Progress runs, open and kept, with the kept runs (`renames`); metrics measured under the old names, merged into the new names', with the measurements (`renamesApplied`). Each place saves the number of the last rename it followed in the same write as what it changed (workspace.json's settings change in memory only once saved; the others are saved whole from memory, the number with them), so a rename is followed once, and one a failure or a stop left behind is followed later: a run that fails is tried again after 5 s, doubling up to 10 minutes, while the workspace is open, and at its next opening. A retry follows the projects as last read, never the read that failed: a project renamed again after a failed run has its settings and runs follow its newest name, never back to a folder it no longer has (#555). The Progress panel's filter isn't moved: one on a folder with no runs shows them all. The removal pack restores under the folder's name now, so it needs nothing.
- **Project** — any direct child folder of the workspace, excluding dot-folders (so `.hive` is never a project) and folders hidden or removed from Hive (§6.7). Creating a project in Hive creates the folder.
- **Active project** — a project switched on with its toggle. Only active projects get a terminal, watchers, status indicators and notifications. Activating a project **never** starts or resumes a session; it offers "Resume last session" and "New session". Deactivating requires stopping the live session (after confirmation). Active state is personal, so it is stored in app data, not in the workspace.
- **Provider** — the CLI an agent runs: **Claude Code** (Anthropic) or **Codex** (OpenAI). The UI uses product names, and "provider" where a generic word is needed. Providers are enabled in Settings → Providers; a fresh install has none enabled (a banner links to the settings), and an upgrade from 0.1 keeps Claude Code enabled. With none enabled, no agent can start and none can be added.
- **Agent** — one of a project's session slots: up to twelve, or as many as Settings → Advanced → **Agents per project** allows (#562, at most 24). **All agents are equal** and a new project has **none**: agents are added (Add Agent) and removed (when stopped) freely, and each works in the project folder or in its own **git worktree** (a separate checkout on its own branch). Agents are named ("Agent 1", "Agent 2"…, renamable), have random ids that are never reused, and always store their **provider**, chosen when they are added; defaults never move an existing agent. An agent can override the project's model, effort and permission mode for its provider. Changing an agent's provider clears those overrides and its last session. Each agent has at most one live session. Agents of a disabled provider stay listed, greyed out, and can't start. Upgrading from 0.1 clears every project's agents and layout (their sessions stay and can be resumed by an agent added again).
- **Session** — one conversation of a provider's CLI. Claude Code sessions have a UUID Hive chooses; Codex chooses its own ID, which Hive learns from the first hook (with the first prompt). A session can only be resumed by an agent of the same provider. A project has at most one live session per agent plus any number of past (resumable) and archived sessions.
- **Hive Assistant** — one per workspace, in a panel on the right: the workspace's **overseer**, a provider session like an agent's that looks after every project rather than working in one (§6.5). It never takes one of a project's agent slots.
- **Mode** (a *persona* file) — how the Assistant works: what it puts first and how it hands things back, a Markdown file of instructions in `<Workspace>/.hive/personas`.
- **Task board** — one per workspace: cards (tasks) in four fixed columns, each belonging to a project and optionally given to one of its agents. The user, the Assistant and agents all work on it (§6.6).
- **Global scope** — settings in app data or `<Workspace>/.hive/workspace.json`, applied to every project unless the project overrides them.
- **Project scope** — overrides in `<Project>/.hive/project.json`.

---

## 4. On-disk layout

### 4.1 Workspace (committed to git if the workspace is a repo)

```
<Workspace>/
  .hive/
    workspace.json        # MCP servers turned on, hidden and removed projects
    shared/               # cross-session notes, handovers, instructions (markdown)
      handovers/
    skills/
      <skill-name>/SKILL.md (+ supporting files)
    mcp/
      <server-name>.json  # one MCP server definition (§7.2)
      <server-name>/      # optional server code
    personas/
      <id>.md             # a Hive Assistant persona (§6.5)
    bundled.json          # which of Hive's bundled skills and personas the workspace was given, and from which version (§7.1)
    tasks/                # the task board (§6.6)
      <number>.json       # one card
      board.json          # the next card number
    lanes.json            # the card loops running (lanes, §6.6)
    assistant/            # the Hive Assistant's home (§6.5); its own .gitignore keeps it out of git
      .hive/              # like a project's: project.json (its one agent), sessions.json, backups, launch-assistant/
    README.md             # explains the folder to humans browsing the repo
```

`<Workspace>/.hive/` is intended to be committed so a team can share skills, MCP definitions and notes. MCP definitions must reference secrets via environment variables (§7.2).

### 4.2 Project (entirely git-excluded)

```
<Workspace>/<Project>/
  .hive/
    project.json          # project overrides
    sessions.json         # session index
    sessions/<id>.jsonl   # backup copy of each session transcript
    archive/<id>.jsonl    # transcripts of archived sessions
    attachments/<id>/<time>[_<name>]  # files pasted or dropped into a session (#387; was images/); deleted only by Clean Up… (§4.2.1)
    removed/              # only after Remove from Hive (§6.7): manifest.json, cards.json, handovers/
    project.json.bak, sessions.json.bak   # last good copies (see §2)
    launch-<agent id>/    # Claude Code agents: regenerated on every launch — do not edit
      plugin/.claude-plugin/plugin.json
      plugin/skills/<skill>/...     # copies of enabled Hive skills
      mcp.json
      settings.json                 # hooks Hive injects
      sync.json                     # hash of each item copied
  .agents/skills/hive-<skill>/  # Codex agents: copies of enabled Hive skills (Codex reads skills only here),
                                # each with a .hive-copy marker (only marked folders are replaced or removed);
                                # git-excluded with a /.agents/skills/hive-*/ line in .git/info/exclude
```

Codex takes everything else from `-c` overrides on its command line (§6.4), so it needs no launch folder.

Worktrees Hive creates for agents live **next to the workspace**, in `<Workspace>.worktrees/<Project>/<agent>` (e.g. `D:\Dev\HIVE.worktrees\hive\agent-2`), so the project's own tools (tsc, test runners, search, file watchers) don't scan a second copy of the code, and Hive doesn't take them for projects. Transcripts, backups and images of worktree agents still live in the project's `.hive` folder.

#### 4.2.1 Storage and Clean Up

- **Project Settings → Storage** (also for the Assistant's home, from Settings → Workspace) shows the sizes of `.hive/sessions`, `.hive/archive`, `.hive/attachments` and each agent's worktree, and their total. The CLIs' own transcripts aren't counted. Main measures in the background (`storage.ts`): a directory at a time, links and junctions not followed, yielding to the event loop every few hundred entries; one measurement per project at a time, and the last result comes back at once until **Refresh**. Attachment and worktree folders are cached per folder by modified time (a folder that hasn't changed counts its files as before; its subfolders are still checked); backups and the archive are measured afresh. The Storage pages pass a request with each call and abandon it when they close or measure again (`storage:abandon`, after the new call is sent): the abandoned call fails, and a measurement no call waits for any more stops between entries, leaving no result (the folders it finished stay cached). A request is its window's (by its web contents): only that window abandons it, and closing the window or reloading its page, which run none of the page's clean-up, abandons all of its requests (#260). Calls that reuse a request id while one under it still runs share it: abandoning it stops every one of their waits, and it goes with the last of them (#299). A call without a request (none in the UI today) can't be abandoned and keeps its measurement going. An `.asar` file counts as a file of its size.
- **Settings → Workspace → Storage**: the workspace's total and every project, the Assistant included, biggest first, ten to a page (a data table, #250), each opening its Storage page (the Assistant's opens in place).
- **Clean Up…** runs per project, only when asked; nothing is automatic. Options: attachments of archived sessions last active more than N days ago (on, 90); attachments of deleted sessions and of `run-<runId>` folders of launches that never got a session (never a running launch's); Hive's backups of archived sessions older than N days, **only where the CLI still has the transcript**; and, apart from that, with a warning, backups of archived sessions the CLI no longer has. Age is the session's last activity.
- A preview lists every file and its size and the total before anything is removed (`storage:cleanupPreview`). Applying it (`storage:cleanup`, with the listed paths) plans again and removes only listed items that still qualify (a session resumed or unarchived meanwhile is skipped and reported); a session's backups go only if every copy of it was listed. Each session is reserved while its files go (a resume of it is refused until then: "Clean Up is removing files of this session") and checked with both of its backups locked, again right before they go (still archived, not running or starting, its copies exactly the ones listed); an image folder is checked the same way just before it goes. Anything else is skipped. A backup goes only after what its session used has been read and saved on its record (`keptUsage`; a session whose only copy it was then moves it to `deletedUsage` as it is deleted), so its totals survive a failure partway; if that can't be read or saved, the backup stays and is reported. Everything goes to the Recycle Bin. Running sessions and sessions that aren't archived are never touched, nor `~/.claude` or `~/.codex`.
- Usage totals don't change: removing the backup of a session the CLI still has keeps its usage on the record (`keptUsage`), which counts if the CLI later drops its transcript; removing the last copy deletes the session as Delete does (§6.2: `deleted`, `deletedUsage`).

#### 4.2.2 Antivirus scanning (#316)

Windows only, behind a platform-neutral status (`shared/antivirus.ts`, `main/antivirus.ts`, `components/Antivirus.tsx`): elsewhere it is "not applicable".

- **The folders**, worked out per workspace and always as resolved absolute paths (`realpath`, never `%VAR%` or `$env:`): the workspace root (its projects live in it), `<workspace>.worktrees` only once it exists, and `%LOCALAPPDATA%\hive-test` only for people developing Hive (an unpackaged build, or a workspace holding Hive's repository: a project whose `package.json` is `hive`/`Hive`) and only once it exists. Each is resolved first and checked after resolving: one that is, or holds, a drive or a share's root, the home folder, the profile or a system folder (`unsafeFolder()`), or whose real location can't be established, is listed as not offered and never excluded; duplicates are dropped (`folderPlan()`).
- **Status** (`antivirus:status`): a read-only PowerShell probe without administrator rights (`Get-MpComputerStatus`, `Get-MpPreference`, Security Center's `AntiVirusProduct`, `Get-Volume` for the folders' drives), at most 20 s, off the UI's thread, cached ten minutes per workspace (Check Again refreshes). What scans: Defender (active, not passive), another antivirus (named; Hive can't manage it), none, or unavailable (said, with why). Each folder: **excluded** or **scanned** when Defender's list is visible; **unknown** when Defender hides it (it shows it only to administrators, the usual case), or **added by Hive** (unconfirmed) for one Hive added; once read with administrator rights, the list as of that check. **Dev Drive**: without administrator rights only the volume's file system is known, so ReFS is a **maybe**; the administrator check (`fsutil devdrv query`) says whether it is a **trusted** Dev Drive or one that isn't trusted (scanned like any other), and Defender's `PerformanceModeStatus` (readable without rights) whether performance mode is on. **Slowed**: Defender with real-time protection on and a folder Hive may offer that is neither known to be excluded nor on a trusted Dev Drive in performance mode. The cached status is probed again when the workspace's folder set changes (a worktrees folder appears); requests share a probe only for the same folder set and workspace lifetime, and an older probe finishing late never replaces a newer one's answer. Defender's hidden-list answer ("N/A: Must be an administrator…") is unknown from whichever read produced it (`readableList()`), never a list.
- **Changes**, only when the user asks, in two steps: `antivirus:prepare` works out the exact operation (the workspace, its lifetime, the action and its resolved folders), the dialog shows exactly those folders (one per line, with a Copy button) and the trade-off (excluded folders aren't scanned at all, `node_modules` and downloads included), and `antivirus:apply` runs that operation once (within ten minutes), refusing it if the window's workspace changed or the folders it would change now differ (a new set needs new consent, never an expansion). **Add Exclusions…** (the folders not known to be excluded), **Remove Hive's Exclusions…** (only those Hive added), **Check with Administrator Rights…** (reads the list and the Dev Drives). Each runs one elevated PowerShell (one UAC prompt; `Start-Process -Verb RunAs` with the whole script as its encoded command, the paths quoted literals, never a script file another process could change). The script reads Defender's list first, and changes nothing if that read is redacted (which exclusions already exist couldn't be known); it asks only for what is needed: Add skips a folder an existing exclusion (itself or a parent) covers, Remove takes only exact entries that are there, so the user's own exclusions are never removed; it then reads the list again. What Hive added (`config.json` `antivirus.added`) is what Defender lists afterwards of what it asked for, never what was asked; the list read with administrator rights is kept only when it was read (a run that couldn't read it leaves the states unknown). Outcomes: done, **declined** (the prompt), **refused** by Defender, or a **policy** (an organisation's settings, or the change accepted but not listed afterwards). Never another antivirus; never silently; paths logged with `userText()`.
- **Where**: Settings → Workspace → **Antivirus scanning** (the status, each folder's, the actions, About Dev Drive with Microsoft's documentation); the Performance view (the workspace) says when scanning slows the workspace, linking there; the palette's **Antivirus Scanning of the Workspace**.
- **The suggestion**: when the window sees a reason (two or more agents running at once, or a command in the Progress panel that ran two minutes or more; and, while that lasts, an agent getting a worktree), it asks main (`antivirus:suggestion`), which suggests only when the workspace is slowed, wasn't declined for good (**Don't Ask Again**, per workspace, `antivirus.dismissed`) and these folders weren't offered in the last day (`antivirus.offered`: per workspace `{ offerKey, count, lastAt }`, the slowed folders as a key; #316's plain key reads as offered once). Folders not offered before (a worktrees folder appearing) are suggested at once with a new count; the same folders again as a reminder at most once a day (`REMIND_MS`, `nextOffer()` in `shared/antivirus.ts`), with no limit (#348): closing the toast or reviewing without adding postpones, only Don't Ask Again stops it, and a status no longer slowed (excluded, a trusted Dev Drive, Defender not active) suggests nothing. From the second time the toast says it's a reminder, at most once a day, and that Don't Ask Again stops it. The reply also says when a reminder may come (`remindAt`): while two or more agents keep running, the window asks again then (a timer it checks at least hourly, cleared when the reason goes); a long command is a reason once, each new one asking again, never a standing one. Main checks and claims the offer with nothing awaited in between, after the status is known, so two requests can't both offer and a Don't Ask Again given meanwhile holds. A toast with **Review…** and **Don't Ask Again**; answers for a workspace the window no longer shows are dropped.
- **Tests never touch Defender**: a test copy (unpackaged, `HIVE_USER_DATA`) takes the probe's answer and the elevated changes' outcomes from `HIVE_TEST_ANTIVIRUS` (a fixture; changes act on a simulated list) and logs every call to `HIVE_TEST_ANTIVIRUS_LOG`; without a fixture it reports "test copy" and refuses changes (`tests/e2e/antivirus.cjs`, `tests/antivirus.test.ts`).

### 4.3 App data (`%APPDATA%/Hive/`)

- `config.json` — settings, recent workspaces, active projects per workspace, window state. Version 2 keeps each provider's settings in `settings.providers[<id>]` and plan usage per provider and limit. Before the first version-2 save, the 0.1 file is copied to `config.v1-backup.json`, and the 0.1 `settings.claude` block is still written, so 0.1 can read the file again. Version 3 (0.3) moves the Assistant's model and effort, when still at 0.2's saved defaults, to the agents' (§6.5).
- `agent-api.json` — Agent API token.
- `usage-cache.json` — each transcript's usage, so a restart doesn't read unchanged transcripts again (§9). Derived and disposable.
- `merge-slots/<hash of the workspace path>.json` — which agents held a merge slot (§6.2), so a hold Hive closed under is reported when the workspace opens again. This machine's state: never in the workspace's `.hive`.
- **A window's page that crashes or hangs** (`main/rendererWatch.ts`, per window): agents run in main, so they are never affected. On `render-process-gone` (except a clean exit) Hive logs the reason and reloads the page; terminals reattach to their running sessions as after View → Reload, the window keeps its workspace, and a toast says "Hive's window stopped and was reloaded. Your agents kept running." with how many files' unsaved edits were lost (the window's last report; hot exit would keep them). A second crash within a minute doesn't reload again: a native dialog says "Hive's window stopped working. Your agents are still running…" with **Reload**, **Open Logs** (then asks again) and **Quit Hive**. On `unresponsive`, after 4 seconds a native dialog offers **Wait** / **Reload**; `responsive` closes it. Reload ends the hung page (`forcefullyCrashRenderer`) and loads it again, without the crash note.
- `logs/hive.log` — **Help → Copy Diagnostics…** puts its last 50 lines in a bug report, with Hive's version and update channel (stable/beta), installed or development build, Windows version, display scaling and locale; each coding agent (on/off, version and path, signed in, update available, anything its adapter adds (`diagnostics()`: Codex's Windows sandbox, default/non-admin/not set up), and its readiness issues); counts (windows, active and total projects, agents, running agents, agents in worktrees, whether the Assistant runs); and the settings that change behaviour (file locks, merge style, quitting, tray, backups, cache TTL, Agent API, Assistant control, notifications, theme), never free text. A dialog shows exactly what will be copied; **Copy** copies it. It is built and redacted in main (`diagnostics.ts`, `shared/redact.ts`). Hive marks the user's own text as it logs it (`userText()` in `logger.ts`: card titles, Assistant activity, session, project, agent and branch names, paths; of the arguments an agent is started with, everything but flags and the values of known flags such as `--model` and `--session-id`, whether separate or written as `--flag=value` (`argsForLog()`)), and the report replaces each marked span with `<text>` or `<path>`. Its start line says so (`your text marked`); lines from a run whose start line doesn't (an older version), or from before the first start line in view, keep only their time, level and scope. For older lines and anything unmarked: the home folder becomes `~`, workspace and worktree folders `<workspace>`, including the workspaces in the recent list; workspace, project, agent and branch names, and the project folders of recent workspaces, are taken out of the log lines (not the summary, which has none, so a project called "Hive" doesn't take Hive's name with it; each line's time, level and `[scope]` stay); any other absolute path becomes `<path>` unless it is under AppData, a dot folder in the home folder, Program Files or Windows; tokens, keys, everything after a credential's name in a line (`Authorization:`, `token=`, `--api-key`, `password:`, and the lines a continuation carries it onto; a quoted JSON key too), OAuth codes in URLs, sign-in and device codes (`maskText`, #699), well-known key formats, JWTs, long hex or base64 runs and email addresses are masked.

### 4.4 Schemas

```jsonc
// <Workspace>/.hive/workspace.json
{ "version": 1, "mcp": { "enabled": ["github"] },
  "hiddenProjects": [ { "name": "old-site", "mode": "hidden", "at": "…" } ] }   // hidden | removed (§6.7)

// <Workspace>/.hive/tasks/12.json
{ "number": 12, "title": "…", "description": "…markdown…", "project": "hive", "agent": "a-7b21e4aa", "agentName": "Agent 2",
  "column": "todo",                     // todo | doing | review | done
  "order": 3.5, "labels": ["ui"], "blocked": null, "blockedBy": [11], "links": [],
  "comments": [ { "at": "…", "by": "Agent 2 (hive)", "text": "…" } ],
  "history": [ { "at": "…", "by": "You", "what": "Moved to Doing" } ],
  "archived": false, "archivedFor": "user",   // user | done | project-hidden | project-removed
  "createdAt": "…", "createdBy": "Assistant", "updatedAt": "…" }

// <Project>/.hive/project.json
{
  "version": 2,                          // 2 since 0.2.0: reading an older file clears its agents and layout
  "mcp":    { "disabled": [] },
  "chime": "inherit",                     // inherit | on | off
  "defaultProvider": "inherit",           // inherit | claude-code | codex — for new agents
  "providers": {                          // per-provider overrides; each field inherit | value
    "claude-code": { "model": "inherit", "effort": "inherit", "permissionMode": "inherit", "extraArgs": "" },
    "codex":       { "model": "gpt-6-sol", "effort": "high", "permissionMode": "approve-for-me", "extraArgs": "" }
  },
  "agents": [ { "id": "a-7b21e4aa", "name": "Reviewer", "provider": "codex", "role": "reviewer", … } ],   // none at first; role: free text (#126), empty = its name
  "layout": "auto",                      // the Session tab's layout (one for the project, #134): auto (the one that shows its agents, up to the 3×2 grid) | single | columns2 | columns3 | grid | grid6; replaces 0.2 builds' sessionLayout
  // 0.1's flat model/effort/permissionMode/extraArgs are still written (Claude Code's values) for downgrades.
}

// <Project>/.hive/sessions.json
{ "version": 1, "sessions": [ { "id": "…", "agent": "claude-code", "agentId": "a2", "name": "…",
  "createdAt": "…", "lastActiveAt": "…", "archived": false,
  "transcriptPath": "…",        // Codex: where its rollout file is (it can't be found from the folder)
  "titleAtRename": "…",         // the CLI's own name for it (/rename) when it was last renamed in Hive (null: none)
  "handedOverFrom": "…" } ],    // the session whose work was handed over to this one (Hand Over to…)
  "deleted": ["…"] }              // sessions deleted in Hive: the CLI still has them, so Hive keeps them hidden
```

---

## 5. Settings resolution

**Skills** — no switches: every Hive skill in `<Workspace>/.hive/skills` goes to every session of its audience (§7.1). The 0.1 lists (`workspace.json` `skills.enabled`, `project.json` `skills.disabled`) are dropped when the file is next saved.

**MCP**

```
on(project)       = projectEnabled ∪ (allProjects − projectDisabled)      // the project's own choice wins
effective(project, agent) = on(project) ∩ agent's choice (all, unless it chose some)
```

- **Available, then on** (#713, Darren's decision of 13 Oct: the Godot server added at workspace level turned up, with its 187 tools, in every project). A server in `<Workspace>/.hive/mcp` is **available** to every project and **on** only where turned on. Three layers: the workspace (available, and optionally **on for all projects**: `workspace.json` `mcp.enabled`), the project (its own choice: `project.json` `mcp.enabled` turns one on, `mcp.disabled` turns one off, and either wins over all projects), the agent (#537, below). `shared/agentMcp.ts` holds the rule (`mcpOnInProject`, `serverOnIn` for a listed server); launches, the Agent API and the window all use it. A list Hive can't read turns nothing on.
- **Adding one** at workspace level (Add MCP Server from File) asks **Turn on for:** the currently selected project (named, "Amberlight (the selected project)", and the default when a project is selected), **All projects** or **None yet**; nothing else is turned on. The add is held to the copy of the file the dialog showed (the preview's hash of its bytes, read once): a file changed since, its definition or a zip's code, is refused before anything is written ("…has changed since it was shown to you. Add it again to see what it runs"), and turning a new server on anywhere needs that hash. **New MCP Server** creates its template on nowhere (it is turned on once written). **Copy to workspace** (a server a project defines in its own `.mcp.json`) shows what each runs first and turns it on in that project straight away; one whose definition changed after it was shown is copied off. A server added, replaced or deleted forgets every project's choice for its name (hidden projects' too) and its all projects, so an old switch never turns on a new definition.
- **Turning one on** for all projects (the MCP view's switch) or in a project (its MCP tab, when it isn't on for all projects) shows what it runs and asks first, and is refused if the definition changed since it was shown (§7.2). Turning all projects on or off leaves each project's own choice as it is.
- **Migration: nothing changes at upgrade.** Before #713 the workspace switch meant on in every project unless the project turned it off; that is exactly **on for all projects** with the project's `disabled` list, so `workspace.json` and `project.json` are read as they are (a project without `mcp.enabled` has chosen nothing) and nothing is rewritten. Only servers added afterwards start off.
- **Shown:** the MCP view's list and a server's view say where it is on ("On in Amberlight", "On in all projects (not in mcp)", "Not on in any project") beside its **All projects** switch; the project's MCP tab lists every workspace server with an on/off switch, a `workspace` badge and `on for all projects` where it is; `GET /v1/projects/{name}` has `mcp: { available, on }`, and `GET /v1/mcp`'s `globallyEnabled` is on for all projects. MCP servers aren't Hive settings, so they aren't in the Assistant's settings catalogue (`hive_list_settings`): the Assistant reads them with `hive_project_status`.

- **Per agent** (#537, Darren's decision of 11 Oct: for focus and a smaller context, not safety): an agent's settings say **MCP servers: All of the project's** (the default, unchanged: no field) or **Only these** (`AgentDef.mcpServers`, the names; `[]` none), in Agent Settings and a template's agent dialog, saved by templates (`TemplateAgent.mcpServers`), and through the Agent API (`mcpServers` on adding or changing an agent; `["*"]` all again). `shared/agentMcp.ts` holds the rule (`agentGetsMcp`): a name chosen that isn't on in the workspace and the project reaches nobody (the dialog lists it as "not on in this project", so a save keeps it); a value Hive can't read gives none, never more. It narrows only the workspace's servers: the built-in `hive` server is always given (`hive` isn't a name to choose), and the Assistant still gets no project servers. The launch passes only the chosen servers, the same way for every provider (`sessions.effectiveHere`, before the adapters: Claude Code's `--mcp-config` with `--strict-mcp-config`, Codex's `-c mcp_servers.*` with the user's same-named servers turned off, Copilot's `--additional-mcp-config` with `--disable-mcp-server` for the user's), so a server an agent wasn't given is off for it like any other. The choice is part of the launch signature: a running agent shows "restart to apply". Shown in the agent's tooltip, Project Settings' agent list (its own settings), a template's agents, the project's MCP tab (under each server, once any agent has a choice: "Given to every agent", "Given to Tester only", "Given to no agent") and `GET /v1/projects/{name}` (each agent's `mcpServers` and `mcpChoice`). A per-server "only agents given it" option (the card's first idea) wasn't built: an agent with **Only these** doesn't get a server enabled later, which covers part of it.
- MCP servers must be deployed to `<Workspace>/.hive/` before any project can use them.
- Changes (to skills and MCP) apply to **new sessions only**, including restarts. A live session keeps what it launched with; the UI shows "settings changed — restart to apply".
- If a project defines MCP servers in its own `.mcp.json` that are not in the workspace, Hive notifies the user that they stay disabled until copied to the workspace, and offers "Copy to workspace". (`--strict-mcp-config` enforces this.)

**Provider** — stored on each agent when it is added, so a change of default never moves an agent. The project's default provider, else the global one (Settings → Providers), is what the quick add uses (the palette's **Add Agent**, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N, and New Session in a project without agents): an agent with that provider and its default settings in the project folder; the strip's **Add Agent** button (Configure Agent and Add…) opens the **Add Agent…** dialog, preset to it, to choose the provider, where it works and its settings.

**Model, effort, permission mode, chime** — per provider: the global value is on the provider's settings page (Settings → Claude Code, Settings → Codex), the project's in Project Settings under the provider's name, and the project value overrides unless it is `inherit`. An agent's own model, effort or permission mode (set when adding it, or in its settings) overrides the project's; unset, it follows the project. A mode that isn't allowed (e.g. Bypass after the option was turned off) falls back to the project's, then the global one.

**Agents** — *File locks* (global Settings → Agents & Worktrees, default **Block**; project override), *Copy into new worktrees* (global list, default `.env*`; project override), *Setup command* (project only, empty by default), *Default merge style* (global, default **Merge**; a Squash saved before 0.3 is moved to Merge once, config version 4).

**Number settings** (Settings and Project Settings) save on Enter or blur, once. A blank box saves nothing in Settings (the saved value comes back) and means Inherit in Project Settings; it is never read as 0. Text that isn't a number, or a number out of range, saves nothing and shows the range under the box. Where 0 turns the feature off (*Suggest compacting above*, the Assistant's *Highlight Compact over*, *Warn when a transcript is over*, *Archive Done cards after*, *Pause after you type*), 0 is chosen with a **Never** / **No pause** checkbox beside the box (typing 0 does the same); unticking it restores the last other value, else the default (Settings) or Inherit (Project Settings). A failed save shows the saved value again.

**Permission mode**
- Global default: **Auto** (Claude Code's safety classifier approves low-risk actions and asks about risky ones; Manual asked too often to be a good default for supervising several agents).
- Project dropdown: Inherit, Manual, Accept edits, Plan, Auto, Don't ask — always available.
- **Bypass permissions** is only offered when Settings → Claude Code → "Enable the Bypass permissions option" is ticked (ticking asks for confirmation). Projects using it show a warning indicator.
- If the option is later unticked, projects set to bypass revert to Inherit and the user is told which ones changed.
- **In a running session** the mode can be switched without a restart. Hive knows the session's real mode from its launch flag, from Claude Code's footer as the terminal redraws (`⏵⏵ auto mode on`, `⏸ plan mode on`, …; read from the rendered screen, a headless terminal of one screen per session with no scrollback, since Claude Code may redraw only the characters that changed, as it does with `NO_COLOR` set, which Hive leaves as it is) and from `permission_mode` in hooks, so a Shift+Tab typed in the terminal shows in Hive at once. Choosing a mode (the mode badge in the project header, a pane header or the status bar, or **Switch Permission Mode…**, Ctrl+Alt+M) sends Shift+Tab until the footer shows it. Claude Code's cycle is Manual → Accept edits → Plan → Auto; Don't ask leaves it once left, and Bypass is only in it for a session launched in Bypass (it shows Claude Code's own warning the first time), so those two restart the session in the mode and resume the same conversation, after confirmation. Not while the agent is asking something (Shift+Tab could change the answer). A live switch affects that session only.
- **Codex presets**: Read only (`-s read-only -a on-request`), Ask for approval (`-s workspace-write -a on-request`), **Approve for me** (`--approve-for-me`: Codex's reviewer approves low-risk actions; the default, like Auto) and **Full access** (`-s danger-full-access -a never`). Full access is treated like Bypass: only offered when Settings → Codex → "Enable the Full access option" is ticked (with confirmation), badged as a warning, and reverted to Inherit when unticked. A running Codex session switches preset live: Hive clears the input (Ctrl+U), types `/permissions`, and picks the preset from Codex's menu; Plan mode is a separate toggle (Shift+Tab, shown as "· Plan" on the badge).
- **Changing the setting** (global, project or agent) never restarts anything and no longer marks sessions "restart to apply". New sessions use it; for running agents whose setting changed, a notification offers **Switch Now**, which switches them live (or reports which couldn't be).

---

## 6. Session lifecycle

§6.1 and §6.3 describe Claude Code; §6.4 is how Codex differs. Every launch has a **run ID**, so hooks reach the right agent before the session ID is known (hook URL `<hook server>?run=<runId>`).

### 6.1 Launch

On "New session" or "Resume":

1. Resolve effective skills and MCP servers (§5).
2. Rebuild `<Project>/.hive/launch-<agent id>/` from scratch (copy skills, record hashes) and write the launch's `mcp.json` and `settings.json` to its private folder in Hive's user data (`launches/<run id>/`, #345: they can hold the user's secrets).
3. For a resume, if Claude Code's transcript is missing (e.g. cleaned up) but Hive holds a backup, restore it first.
4. Spawn in a pty, cwd = the agent's folder (the project folder, or its worktree):

```
claude --session-id <uuid> | --resume <uuid>
       --name "<session name>"
       --plugin-dir .hive/launch-<agent id>/plugin
       --mcp-config <user data>/launches/<run id>/mcp.json --strict-mcp-config
       --settings <user data>/launches/<run id>/settings.json
       [--model <m>] [--effort <e>] [--permission-mode <p>] [extra args]
```

Environment added to the pty: `HIVE_HOOK_TOKEN`, `HIVE_API_URL`, `HIVE_API_TOKEN` and `HIVE_API_TOKEN_FILE` (the agent's own Agent API token for this launch, never the workspace token), `HIVE_PROJECT`, `HIVE_PROJECT_PATH`, `HIVE_WORKSPACE`, `HIVE_AGENT` (the agent's name), `HIVE_PROVIDER`, `HIVE_RUN_ID` and, when known at launch, `HIVE_SESSION_ID`. Every provider's own session variables inherited from Hive's environment (e.g. `CLAUDECODE`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`) are removed.

**A `--settings` in the user's extra arguments** (#330): Claude Code reads only the last `--settings` it is given (2.1.291), so the user's would replace Hive's and with it every hook and the status line. Hive instead reads it (the last one, as Claude Code does: inline JSON, or a file relative to the agent's folder) and merges it into its own `settings.json`: every setting of the user's, their hooks added after Hive's for each event, and Hive's status line (a session has one; Hive's reports context, cost and limits). Their HTTP hook allowlists, which Claude Code takes whole from the highest file that sets one (not merged across files), gain what Hive's hooks need: `allowedHttpHookUrls` its hook URL, `httpHookAllowedEnvVars` `HIVE_HOOK_TOKEN` (without them Claude Code 2.1.292 blocks Hive's HTTP hooks, or sends them with an empty token); unset, they are left unset. Managed settings, above the command line, still apply. The CLI gets only Hive's `--settings`; the user's are dropped from the arguments (`providers/claude/launchSettings.ts`). One Hive can't merge stops the launch before its folder changes, with a **Couldn't start** that names the argument: a `--settings` given no value (at the end of the arguments, `--settings=` or an empty value, even after a valid one: Claude Code reads the last, and a mistake shouldn't launch with Hive's settings alone, #361), a missing file (Claude Code refuses it too: "Settings file not found"), one that isn't a regular file or is over 2 MiB (Claude Code 2.1.292 refuses those too; checked before the file is opened and read no further than the limit, #333), a file that isn't JSON (Claude Code ignores it silently), JSON that isn't an object, `disableAllHooks: true` (it turns off Hive's hooks and status line with the user's, 2.1.292), or an HTTP hook allowlist that isn't a list (Claude Code then blocks Hive's hooks). `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_HOOKS` in the settings' `env` block doesn't turn hooks off (2.1.292) and is left as it is. Codex has no equivalent: its `--profile` layers a config file under the `-c` overrides Hive passes its hooks in (0.160.1), so a profile can't replace them.

Each Claude Code agent launches from its own folder (`launch-<agent id>/`), because a launch replaces the folder while the project's other agents may still read theirs (skills are read when used).

Rebuilding `launch/` on every launch means a restarted agent has exactly the workspace's current skills (for its audience, §7.1) and MCP servers.

### 6.2 Operations

| Action | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| New session | New UUID. A live session is stopped first (with confirmation). In a project without agents, New Session (and Resume) first adds one with the quick add, then starts it. |
| Resume | `--resume <uuid>`, in an agent that works in the folder the session ran in (sessions record their agent, folder and branch). Shows the re-cache estimate (§9) first. If the session has no transcript yet (nothing was typed before it stopped or was restarted), it is started fresh with `--session-id <uuid>` instead. **One conversation runs in one agent at a time**: the main process refuses to resume a session that is open in another agent (two terminals would both append to its transcript), for the UI and the Agent API alike; the UI shows that agent instead. A paused session can move between agents that share its folder (never across worktrees); the record then names the new agent, and any other agent whose *last session* it was forgets it. |
| Stop | Ends the pty. Session stays resumable. |
| Leftover processes (#507) | Ending the pty ends what is attached to its console (node-pty asks the console for its processes), not what the agent started detached or hidden (a preview server, a `tail -f`, a Claude Code background shell, a Codex background terminal), which then outlive it and hold the project's folder and ports. So each launch's CLI (and its worktree's setup command) runs in a **Windows job object** of its own from its first instruction, and everything it starts joins the job by itself, however it is started and through whatever exits since (`agentProcesses.ts`). node-pty can't start a process suspended and knows its pid only once it runs, so the terminal first runs a **gate** (`launchGate.ts`): a hidden PowerShell with no cmdlets (a console program, so it shares the pseudoconsole; Electron's own executable run as Node doesn't), given the command and its arguments in its environment (removed before the command starts). It connects to a pipe made for the launch and waits; Hive puts the gate in the job (`ptyHost` `track`, which checks the process is still running), then answers `go`, and the gate starts the command with the command line node-pty would have built (`commandLineOf`, tested against node-pty's), on the same console, and exits with its code. The gate starts the command **only on `go`**, however long that takes: `stop <why>`, a failed connection or the pipe closing without a word (Hive gone, or the terminal stopped) end it with `Error: Hive didn't start <exe>: <why>` (a failed start), never running the command. A launch stopped while its gate was being tracked is told `stop`, and its job let go even if the assignment lands after (nothing is assigned to a dead or reused pid). **When tracking can't work** (Darren's decision B, 10 Oct): a launch first waits until the job host is running (it starts it; `trackingReady`). If it can't run (PowerShell blocked, Constrained Language Mode refusing Add-Type) or doesn't say it is ready within 60 s (it is then killed and given up on for the run), the CLI starts directly, untracked; if a launch's assignment then fails, the gate is told `go` all the same. Once the host runs, every reply is bounded too (60 s): one that doesn't come means the host is stuck, and it is killed, so no launch, End or quit waits on it longer. **A host that ends after starting** (a crash, or killed as stuck) closes its jobs: running agents' launches are no longer tracked (they keep running, nothing is ended); the leftovers listed stay listed by identity, as after a restart (#614, below), and those whose start time isn't known go. Hive says so at once through the same once-a-run notice (what was listed and what running agents start are no longer tracked), and Diagnostics keeps it (`; earlier, the process tracker stopped at <time> (<why>): …`) even once a new host, started by the next request, tracks later launches. Either way Hive never refuses to start an agent over it, and says so once a run: a notification ("Hive can't keep track of agents' processes": why, and that what agents leave running isn't listed) and a Diagnostics line (`Agents' processes: tracked` or `not tracked: <why>`). Test copies (unpackaged): `HIVE_TEST_PROCESS_TRACKER=fail` acts out a host that can't run, `=silent` one that starts but never says it is ready, and `HIVE_TEST_TRACKER_WAIT_MS` shortens the 60 s waits. One hidden PowerShell per Hive holds the jobs (C# through `Add-Type`, no files; one line per request); without it (PowerShell blocked) nothing is tracked and agents stop as before. The jobs never end anything themselves (no kill-on-close). Two seconds after the CLI exits, what is still in its job is what it left (consoles' hosts not listed): logged, kept per agent (the newest 5 launches each, 100 in all; older ones forgotten, never ended), looked at again every 10 s while any are listed (those that ended go), and sent to the agent's window (`agent-leftovers`). **Settings → Agents → Processes left running** (`agents.leftoverProcesses`): **Ask** (default) shows a notification listing them with End Them / Keep Running and an **⚠ n left running** chip in the agent's pane header (hover: the list; click: end or keep); **End them** ends them as the agent stops. End terminates the launch's job, so only processes its CLI started are ended, never one started outside it (a process started outside it isn't tracked either, except what escapes it from inside, below; a launch whose job couldn't be ended stays listed and the End fails with a message (the header's End shows it; with End them set, the notification is shown instead, saying Hive couldn't end them), so it can be tried again or kept; only what was ended is counted. Keep Running stops listing them and leaves them running. Offers whose agent has nothing listed any more are dismissed. Quitting Hive with some listed asks once in a native dialog (End Them / Leave Running) while a window is shown; a quit with none shown, or to install an update, leaves them running and still listed. **Across a restart** (#614: a dev copy of Hive a suite started under an agent outlived it and two of Hive's restarts; that Hive predated #507, and a later quit to install an update would have forgotten it): what is listed is also kept in the profile (`leftovers.json`, written on every change once within the bounds, so a crash keeps it too), each launch apart and within the same bounds when written and when read (the newest 5 launches an agent, 100 in all, 50 processes a launch), each process by its pid and start time (a Windows FILETIME the host reads with `GetProcessTimes`, `created` in the listing; a process whose start time couldn't be read isn't kept). The next start, once the first window has loaded, lists again those still running as the same processes, each launch on its own (the host's `identify`, which answers with pid and start time pairs, matched as pairs: a record whose pid names another process by then is dropped, also beside another record of that pid that does match) and offers them as when an agent stops ("<agent> left n processes running", End Them / Keep Running; with End them set, ends them). Their jobs are gone by then, so End ends exactly the listed processes, each only while it is still the same process (the host's `endexact`: opened by pid, its start time checked, ended through that handle; one gone counts as ended), not what they started since; they're looked at again every 10 s like the rest. Without the job host, nothing is restored and the list stays for the next start. Test copies of Hive name their profile on their own command line (`--hive-test-profile=…`, which Hive ignores; `tests/e2e/lib.cjs`), so the e2e runner's clean-up of what names a suite's folder also stops a test Hive whose suite died before closing it, as the one in #614 did. Renderer IPC: `session:leftovers` (the window's workspace), `session:settleLeftovers` (the session host is checked; only that agent's). Windows only. **The real CLIs** (#515, real-tier suites `leftovers-claude` and `leftovers-codex`): a Claude Code background Bash (`run_in_background`) outlives Hive's stop (its terminal ended) and is listed, and End ends it; `/exit` ends it with Claude Code (2.1.296). Codex ends its background terminals itself as it exits, stopped by Hive or `/exit` (0.162.1), so they're never left, except that Codex runs commands in PowerShell 7 where there is one, and doesn't end what runs through the Microsoft Store's: that is listed and ended as below (#543). **What escapes the job** (#543): the Store's PowerShell 7 (`WindowsApps\pwsh.exe`) is a packaged app, so Windows starts it in its package's job and its children in none: neither would be listed or ended. The job host takes them in: a live process whose parent is in a launch's job, and that isn't in that job itself, joins it if it is in no job (its later children with it); one already in another job (the Store's PowerShell, in its package's) is followed instead: held open, listed, and ended with the job. Never assigned (#659): Windows would nest the jobs, and the package's job, which every Store PowerShell joins, when it had no parent became the launch's, so every Store PowerShell started after it (any agent's or app's) was listed and ended with that launch. Nor is a packaged app's process, even in no job (#678): Windows puts the Store's PowerShell in its package's job only once it has started (4 to 160 ms after it shows in a snapshot, measured; more under load), and a sweep that caught it before then and assigned it nested the package's job in the launch's just the same (about 1 in 50 loaded test runs). A process with a package identity (`GetPackageFullName`) is followed whatever job it is in; its children (not packaged) still join the launch's job when they are in none. Ids are checked against what they named when seen (#543 round 2): a candidate, or a parent looked up by id and not held, created after the sweep's snapshot is refused (the id names another process by now; Windows stamps creation in ticks of about 15.6 ms, so one created within the snapshot's own tick still passes: a documented limit), the parent must have started before the child, and a process the port reports as joining is held only if it is in that job (a notification read late may carry a reused id). End ends the job and each followed process; one Windows won't end that still runs makes End fail (listed, to try again), and one already gone counts as ended. The host sweeps for them as processes of a job exit (the job's completion port reports each joining and leaving process; the host holds each open until the sweep after its exit, so the exited parent's id and start time stay known; exits are gathered for up to 250 ms and swept for together, since a sweep's snapshot of every process costs a few ms and a build ends hundreds), before listing or ending a job, and every second. Still missed: an escaped process that starts something and exits within a second, before any sweep saw it (a `Start-Process` from the Store's PowerShell that returns at once). The host's C# goes to its PowerShell as the first line of its input, base64 (a command line holds at most 32,767 characters). The log says how many processes of a launch were taken in this way. Unit tests stand in for the escape on any PC (a child started before its CLI joins the job) and use the Store's PowerShell where it is installed; `leftovers-codex` keeps it on PATH. **Agents stop what they start** (#613: one agent's own `tail -f | grep` log watchers, 16 of them, outlived the runs they watched until Hive quit): the agents' session contract says to watch a run with something that ends with it (`hive-progress`, the CLI's notice that a background command ended), never `tail -f`, and to end what they started that won't end by itself before their card ends, unless the user wants it running; work-on-card (a Finish step, before Review), card-loop (a loop's turns end on a watch, never a watcher) and merge-ready (finishing loose work) say how. The `watcher-stopped` scenario checks it: Hive lists nothing the agent left once its card is in Review and it is stopped (`session:leftovers` with `settled`, which answers once every exited launch has been looked at). |
| Failed start | The CLI exits while the session is still **starting** (before its first hook, or for Codex before its prompt shows), and Hive didn't stop it: a missing or broken CLI, an argument or config it refuses. Main reads the CLI's last error lines from the terminal output (`shared/startFailure.ts`: escapes removed, from the first line naming an error among the last 12, at most three lines and 300 characters) and asks the adapter for a hint (`startHint()`: what is wrong and where to fix it: Agent Settings, Agent Setup, or the terminal), and sends them with `session-exit` (`failure`). The pane shows them in place of "Session ended", in red: **Couldn't start:** the reason, the hint, **Retry** (the same launch again: a resume resumes the conversation), **Agent Settings…** (or **Agent Setup…** when that's where the fix is) and ✕; the agent's tab dot and header turn red ("Failed to start"); a toast with **Show** comes only when its pane isn't on screen, and the window's attention inbox lists it under *needs you* ("Couldn't start: …"; the tray's list, built in main, doesn't know it). It lasts until the agent's next launch or ✕, and isn't kept across restarts. The terminal keeps the full output. Not covered: errors the CLI shows without exiting (Claude Code's sign-in screen, an unknown model or expired sign-in on the first prompt). |
| Compact | Header button next to Stop, Session menu, command palette. Enabled only while the agent is idle (Ready/Finished) and the conversation has content. A dialog shows the context size and cache state and takes an optional **focus**; empty lets the agent decide what to keep. Hive sends Ctrl+U (clears half-typed input; Claude Code's Ctrl+Y restores it), then `/compact [focus]`. Status shows "Compacting…" until `PostCompact`, a new compaction in the transcript, a refusal in the output ("Not enough messages to compact"), no `PreCompact` within 20 s, or 10 minutes.
