- **What it is told.** Hive's session contract for the Assistant (`hiveInstructions(…, 'assistant')`, the hive MCP server's instructions: use the hive tools for Hive's things, and which skills cover its workflows), then at launch its role (`assistantInstructions`): the workspace and its projects at launch, what Control lets it do and the boundaries it keeps whatever else it reads (`controlRules`), that nothing wakes it but the user and its own tool calls, that its mode never changes what it may do and that it may suggest another mode, and its mode: Claude Code through `--append-system-prompt-file` (a file in its launch folder), Codex through `developer_instructions`. How to run agents and cards well (briefs, starting cards, waiting, handing over, stalled cards) is the `coordinate-agents` skill's, not its prompt's; how to run a team's lanes (planned by files, complete builder and reviewer briefs, a confirmed watch on each lane's last card, a board sweep on each wake, changing a running lane, checking agents' claims against git and the board) is the `run-lanes` skill's (#402, from the coordinator playbook). Its habits fit the setup (Darren's decision on #402): `coordinate-agents` brings in `run-lanes` for three or more agents in a project, builder and reviewer pairs or agents in worktrees, and with one or two agents in the project folder stays light, suggesting more structure in one line at most; the setup changes how it works, never what the user can do. The Coordinator mode puts first a watch on running work (never "they'll tell you") and checking what agents report. The rules on what it may do come from Hive and replace anything a mode says; Hive's four modes carry no procedure or permissions. Its hive tools (below) are pre-approved where the provider allows it (Claude Code: `--allowedTools`); writing shared notes still asks.
- **Control** (Settings → Assistant → Control, for every workspace; default **Control agents and create projects**): **Look and advise** (it reads and suggests; you act; it lists lanes too, #500: `hive_lane` is trusted at every level, its description says that only `list` works there, and the Agent API refuses starting, joining and ending one below Control agents), **Control agents** (also adds agents, changes their settings, starts and stops them, gives idle ones tasks, turns projects on), **Control agents and create projects**. It never removes agents, discards worktrees or deletes projects. Changing it marks a running Assistant for restart.
- **Hive's settings** (#186). Every setting is described once, in the **settings catalog** (`src/shared/settingsCatalog.ts`): its id (`<section>.<key>`, `<provider>.<key>`, `project.<key>`), scope (app, provider, workspace, project), control and values, default, description, tip, "when it helps", place in Settings, user guide heading, whether a change waits for a restart (agents' sessions, or the Assistant), and whether it is **sensitive**. Settings and Project Settings draw their rows from it (the views add only the custom controls, by id), and the Assistant's tools read it, so the two never drift; a unit test fails when Hive's settings or a project's config gain a field it doesn't describe. The Assistant reads and explains settings at every Control level (`hive_list_settings`, `hive_read_setting`; `GET /v1/settings`). It changes one (`hive_update_setting`; `PATCH /v1/settings/{id}`) only with **Settings → Assistant → Control → Change settings** on (`assistant.changeSettings`, off by default, independent of the level; the tool is offered only then, and turning it on marks a running Assistant for restart, while turning it off refuses at once), when the user asked or agreed (its rules and the `tune-settings` skill: suggest first, one line). The value is checked as Settings checks it and set as Settings sets it (`config.updateSettings`, or the project's config under its lock). **Sensitive settings are read-only to it**, enforced in the API: permission modes and the dangerous-mode switch, the Agent API, what Hive runs (CLI paths, extra arguments, a project's setup command, background sessions, pre-release updates) and its own Settings → Assistant (Control, Change settings, the typing pause, its provider, working mode and launch settings); the Assistant's own provider settings are an entry each (`assistant.<provider>.model`, `.effort`, `.permissionMode`, `.extraArgs`, `.use200kContext`, drawn together as "With <provider>"), readable like the rest. Tables are set whole as JSON: the board's column colours (an object of column → colour, merged), a provider's prices and its fallback model and effort lists (replaced through the same setters as Settings; null puts back Hive's, for prices the shipped models removed from the table too, as Reset to defaults does). An effort level must be one the effort picker offers for the model that would run (or the default). Keybindings are read-only for now; agent settings stay with `hive_update_agent`. The permission and the value it replaces are checked again at the moment of the change, under the project's lock for a project's setting: a change waiting behind the user's own write is refused if Change settings was turned off meanwhile, or if the Assistant session that asked for it has ended (its launch's token isn't the current one: a restart doesn't carry out the old session's changes), and a Revert if the value changed since. A table's change is listed and answered by the entries that changed ("doing: #3b82f6 → doing: #ff0000"). Each change counts towards its 30 a message and is listed with old → new and a **Revert** button, which sets the old value back through the same path (refused if the setting has changed again since) and is listed too.
- **Messages for a busy agent** (#530). `hive_prompt_agent` refuses an agent that is working, starting, waiting on background tasks or for the user, or watching, which in a running card loop is nearly always: on 10 Oct the user pasted about ten of the Assistant's messages into agents' panes, and cards waited hours for them (#521). `hive_message_agent` (`POST …/agents/{agent}/message`, Control agents) leaves the agent a message instead (`main/agentMessages.ts`), a **delivery** (#647, below) of kind `rebrief`, which the agent reads with `hive_inbox` (#650: never typed in). Its **doorbell** is typed at once when the agent is idle or **watching** (a watching agent is idle, so it is woken by it; its watch carries on, since typing never ends one, and the line says so: `[Hive] New in your inbox: 1 message (m4). Call hive_inbox to read it. (Your watch is still on, waiting for #506 → Review: do what this asks, then end your turn, and Hive wakes you when it changes.)`). A busy agent is told at its next tool call or before its turn ends (Notices and the doorbell, below); else it waits for its next line, whichever comes first: **a line with a wake** (#649: a watch's or a loop's wake is a delivery too, and the delivery service names whatever waits for the agent in one doorbell, `[Hive] New in your inbox: 1 message and 1 card event (#2) (m5, m6). …`, whose inbox lists the message first, so the agent has it before it acts on the wake; a delivery cancelled, refused or added while that line is typed stops it, and it is offered again with what waits then), or a line of its own, as a wake is: only while it is idle, nobody types there (Pause after you type) and no earlier line waits to be taken. Tried on its status changes and every 5 s. Several waiting go in one line (`[Hive] New in your inbox: 2 messages (m4, m5). …`). One the agent reads first (`hive_inbox`, mid-turn) is received and never offered after. **Each keeps its sender's permission**: the Assistant's `assistantChange` guard (its Control level, its session) is asked before its doorbell is typed, before each piece and Enter, and again as the agent reads its inbox: one whose sender may no longer send it (Control turned down, its session ended) is refused, never given, however long it waited. **A doorbell is never taken back** (#652): once typed it names ids, never what they say, so #376's Enter again (`confirmTaken`) submits it even when what it names has ended meanwhile, and the agent's `hive_inbox` gives nothing of that. (Before #650 a line carried the message's text, and Hive cleared such a line with Ctrl+U, `clearTyped`, and typed nothing more until a draft typed over it was submitted; #652 retired that.) **Tied to a card round** (#652, from #699's history: the Assistant's re-brief for a round's finding reached its builder a second after the card passed, and the builder reopened the passed card to apply it). `card` ties a message to that card's round under way (its failed reviews plus one: the fix after a failed review is the next round; refused unless the card is in Doing or Review). Once the card has moved on (passed, done or archived, out of Doing and Review, or another review failed) the message is **stale**: ended with why (`#699 passed review`, `#699 is in round 7 now`), never offered, and not given by `hive_inbox` even when its doorbell has already rung, checked under the delivery lock as it is offered or read (judged against the board right before the plan, and read again if the board changed meanwhile; if it keeps changing, the message is held back unread, never judged on an outdated read), and as the board changes; the Assistant's activity list records it. The reply names the round (`Gave HB6 a message (m12) for #699 round 6`). At most 5 waiting per agent (`MAX_QUEUED`, counted as one is made) of at most 4000 characters (`MESSAGE_MAX_CHARS`); saved, so they survive the agent's stops and Hive's restarts (the Assistant's are then refused, since the launch that left them has ended). The call's reply says what became of that message by the time it returns, with its id: told (its doorbell typed), queued, or (a `409`) cancelled meanwhile, refused, expired or stale, never told when it wasn't. The agent shows them (`inbox`): its pane header has **✉ n unread** (each one's id, who from, its state and the start of it in the tooltip; a click offers **Cancel the Message(s)** for those not read yet (#650: until the agent reads one, it can still be taken back), `session:cancelMessages`), and `hive_agent_activity` has `inbox` (unread, overdue, and the latest deliveries with their states and times), so the Assistant sees whether it was received and acked. `cancel: true` cancels them. `hive_prompt_agent`'s refusals name the tool. A message is for what the agent already works on (a re-brief, the user's answer, a lane addition); a new task is still `hive_prompt_agent` or `hive_start_task`.
- **Deliveries and the inbox** (#647, the messaging redesign's first card: `plans/2026-10-12-messaging-redesign.md` §3.1, decisions on #645 and #649). Everything Hive has for an agent to read is a **delivery**: a record (`shared/deliveries.ts`) with a short id unique in its project (`m12`), its target (agent and conversation), kind (task, re-brief, card event, the user's answer, handover, control), sender, card, created and expiry times, body and state, saved in its session host's `.hive/deliveries.json` (a project's, or the Assistant's home's) with atomic writes and restored when Hive starts (`main/deliveries.ts`). **States**: queued → offered → received → acked, or cancelled (by whom), refused (its sender may no longer send it), expired (not received within 24 hours, `DELIVERY_EXPIRY_HOURS`, or its agent is in another conversation now, or the launch it was for has ended), stale (#652: tied to a card round, and that card has moved on) and uncertain. **Received is automatic, acked is explicit** (#645): received means **read through `hive_inbox`**, the only receipt (#650: the line typed is the doorbell, which names it by id, never its text, so its CLI taking that line is no receipt; #649's `receivedVia: 'typed'`, when the line carried the whole text, is still read back from saved records). A card event or a control notice is done once received; a message, a task, a handover or the user's answer waits for the agent's ack (`hive_inbox` with `ack`), once it has read it: an id alone, as a doorbell names it, acks nothing (it is `unread` in the ack's reply, unchanged, and its source isn't told it was received). One of those not acked 30 minutes after it was made (`OVERDUE_MINUTES`) is **overdue**: shown in the agent's pane (**✉ 1 overdue**, in the warning colour) and in its activity, so a message that went nowhere is a named state, not a mystery. **One scheduler offers them**: the delivery service alone, which types the doorbell for an idle agent (#650, Notices and the doorbell, below), saved as offered before the first key. A doorbell its CLI took whose agent is idle again without having read it (the Stop drain asked it first) **rings once more**, never a third time; it then stays offered, unread in the pane. **Uncertain** (#649): one offered when Hive stopped, or when its agent's launch ended (or another began) before it was read, may not have reached the agent: it is offered once more, its doorbell saying so (`[Hive] New in your inbox: 1 card event (#7) (m2). Call hive_inbox to read it. (m2 again: Hive restarted while offering it, so it may repeat.)`), and stays uncertain (shown, cancellable, fetchable) if that one isn't received either; agents tell a repeat by its ids. **One transaction at a time, saved before anyone sees it**: each host's changes are checked and planned on a copy, saved by one atomic write (a temp file, then a rename), and only then published (`transact`, under the host's lock), so a read or the pane never sees a change that isn't on disk, and a save that fails changes nothing, on disk or in memory, and fails the call (`NotSaved`); a delivery made, offered, fetched or acked is on disk before the call says so or the line's first key is typed. **An operation takes effect at its save**: its checks (its sender's permission, its expiry, the agent's conversation) are made inside the lock as it is planned, with no check after the save: a permission change that lands while it is saved applies to the next operation, not this one (the fetch was decided before it). **A typed line is checked at each write**: the writer checks its offer (`offerHolds`: still offered by it, its sender's permission, its expiry, the agent's launch and conversation, and whether its producer still wants it) before each piece and Enter; one that no longer holds gets no Enter, and each delivery in it is recorded as what is true (refused, expired, cancelled, stale, or put back to wait and offered again). Once typed, the doorbell isn't checked again before its Enter again (#652): what it names is checked as the agent reads it, and one that ended meanwhile is recorded so when Hive next looks (its status, the 5 s tick, the read). One can be **cancelled** until the agent reads it (#650): while it waits, while its doorbell is typed (which then gets no Enter), and after; read, it is the agent's. Taken is counted from the CLI's prompts just before the line's own Enter (the writer's last check, after its offer was saved), so a prompt of the user's submitted while that save was slow is never taken for the line. **Permission is worked out again at every step** from the sender saved with it and the settings now (never a token or a closure): the Assistant's needs Control agents and the launch that sent it still running, so **a waiting message (or task) from an Assistant launch that has ended is refused, and shown** (#645) in the agent's activity and the Assistant's activity list (`A message for B1 (m3) was refused`). A delivery for a known conversation goes to that conversation only. Waiting ones survive the agent's stops and Hive's restarts; a card's events coalesce into one inbox item (`#607: 3 changes, read the card`). **`hive_inbox`** (agents and the Assistant; `POST /v1/inbox`): lists the caller's unread deliveries, oldest first, each with its ids, sender and card, marking them received, and the ids received before and not acked; `ack` takes ids, and one that isn't the caller's is unknown, like one that doesn't exist. Both check what is unread as an offer would, as they plan (its sender's permission, its expiry, the agent's conversation, and its source's say, #649: made again from things as they are now; an offered one keeps the version it was offered as while that still holds, and one that no longer does is made again rather than never given, since its doorbell carried no text (#650; a receipt covers exactly the version read), so a wake telling a reply the user no longer lets be told, a watch that has ended, a loop wake or a decision line that is no longer that agent's is cancelled, and a delivery its producer no longer holds is neither listed nor acked): one that may no longer be delivered is refused, expired or cancelled, never listed or acked; one already received can still be acked. The Agent API gives each delivery's state and times (`GET …/agents/{agent}/deliveries`, and the activity's `inbox`). Bounded: 500 live per project, ended ones (and card events and control notices once received) kept 50 per agent for a week, received ones never acked expire after a week; the file within a 4 MB budget (counted as written, UTF-8, with room for each live one to reach its end), while Hive reads back up to 8 MB, so a file it wrote is never set aside as too big: a delivery that doesn't fit after the oldest ended ones make room is refused when made. A damaged file is set aside, never overwritten; saved entries that aren't deliveries are kept aside in the file (or moved to a file of their own when they don't fit).
- **Every way into an agent is a delivery** (#649, Messaging 3). Each producer makes a delivery and neither types nor keeps a cursor of its own; the delivery service alone offers them, in one doorbell per offer (#650: the counts by kind, cards and ids; the agent reads each in its inbox, a message first, then each other as its producer made it, a wake's words as they were); a task or a handover in a line of its own. A producer registers itself as the delivery's **source**: it makes the line again just before each offer from things as they are then (none left to tell: cancelled), says whether it still holds before each key (a board or lane change since: made again at the next try), and is told when the delivery is received, which is when its cursor moves on, never on "typed". What an agent must be doing to be offered each is one check (`readiness.ts`), read from the turn ledger's facts (#648): its CLI running (or, for the carry-on after a sign-in, stopped by the refused sign-in), no turn running and none whose state is unknown (unknown is never idle), no compaction, nobody asked, and, for new work, no background tasks that will start it again (#726: an agent whose turn has ended while its own background tasks run, a sleep or a timer, takes a message or a card event as an idle one does: HB6 slept 40 minutes to renew a merge slot and heard of neither #720's comment nor a message until it woke; its CLI takes the line as a new turn, Claude Code's; Codex and Copilot, whose tasks start no turn, were idle then already; card-loop, work-on-card and merge-ready have agents wait with a watch's `limitMinutes`, never a timer); a watch is Hive's policy on top: a watching agent takes a message or a wake (its watch carries on), not new work (a task, a handover, the user's answer, a lock's go-ahead). A line put off part-way (the user began typing, what it was made from changed), or held back by the typing pause, is tried again in a moment, and on every status change and a 5 s tick. **The producers**: `hive_message_agent` (re-briefs, above); **`hive_prompt_agent`** and **Start** on a running agent (`hive_start_task`, the board): a task, given now or not at all (refused as before for an agent that is busy, waiting or watching; never queued; a Start whose task isn't given puts the card back), its doorbell typed and the task read from the inbox; **an agent started with a task** (`hive_start_agent` or Add Agent with a prompt, Start on a stopped agent, `hive_prompt_agent` with `newConversation`): a task its launch carries, its doorbell as the launch's prompt, offered into that launch and received once the agent reads it (`hive_agent_activity` shows that task as the agent's current one, not the doorbell); **card, lane and agent watches**: a fired watch's line is handed over as a card event once its agent can take a line (made from the cards as they are then), and the watch stays (the agent shows watching) until that delivery is received, then ends, and what its line told is where the agent's next watch starts (`watches.json` keeps the delivery's id: read back, a watch follows it, ending if it was received, firing again if it ended without that); **loop wakes** (card events, the latest per agent and card, noted on the card once received); **decision wakes** (the user's answer, acked; told once received); **Assistant mode notices** (control, for that launch; the conversation records its mode once received); **a lock's go-ahead** (control, for that launch, once its turn has ended); **the carry-on after a sign-in** (control, given now); **handovers and Continue with** (the source's request to write one and the target's to read it, handover deliveries given now). **Outside the service**: the user's own typing and attachments, and the Agent API's explicit raw session input (privileged, as before); the CLI controls (Compact, the mode menu and Shift+Tab, the Plan toggle, exit keys) stay separate but take the same composer reservation as Hive's lines: none goes in while a line is typed, nor a line while one runs.
- **Notices and the doorbell** (#650, the messaging redesign's channels: `plans/2026-10-12-messaging-redesign.md` §3.2, §3.4, §3.5; the spikes in `notes/2026-10-12-messaging-spike-*.md`). Hive tells an agent it has something to read without typing into a busy one. **A checkpoint**: when one of the agent's own tool calls ends (PostToolUse, never a sub-agent's), the hook's reply carries a notice of what arrived since it was last told in that launch (`[Hive] New in your inbox: 1 message and 1 card event (#607) (m4, m7). Call hive_inbox now to read them, then carry on.`). **The drain**: when its turn would end with something unread, the Stop hook's reply carries the turn on with a notice of everything unread (`[Hive] Before you finish: your inbox has 1 message (m4) you haven't read. Call hive_inbox and act on it before you end your turn.`), at most once a turn: not at a Stop after a continuation (`stop_hook_active`), nor in a turn Hive carried on already. Whose turn a hook is, and whether it is stale, is the turn ledger's word: a hook that may carry a notice is answered in the launch's hook order, after the hooks before it, so a late hook of an earlier turn gets nothing and a prompt sent meanwhile waits behind it; the turn ledger counts that Stop as a continuation (#648), so the turn ends at the next one. **The doorbell**: for an idle agent, one short line of the same kind typed into it (`[Hive] New in your inbox: 1 task (#12) (m9). Call hive_inbox to read it.`): every line Hive types for a delivery is one (a task, a handover, a wake, the user's decisions, a notice), with Enter again once if its CLI doesn't take it, never a third; taken but not read by the time the agent is idle again, it rings once more. **`hive_inbox` is the only receipt** (Deliveries, above). **No inbox** (Darren's decision on #650): an agent that can't read an inbox (a project agent while the Agent API or the built-in hive server is off: `agentReadsInbox`; the Assistant always has its own) is given no delivery, and its bodies are never typed instead. Every automated or Assistant-originated delivery (messages, wakes, the user's decisions relayed, notices, a handover's steps, the carry-on) is refused with the reason its sender sees ("B1 has no inbox: turn on the Agent API"): when it is made, or, one already waiting when the Agent API was turned off, when it is next looked at. Only the user's own action from Hive, a task they Start, is typed as their input, as their typing in its pane is: no delivery and no receipt. No new typed receipts; historical ones (`receivedVia: 'typed'`) still read back. It gets no notices. Each is **Hive's own fixed wording** with counts by kind, card numbers and delivery ids (`shared/doorbell.ts`), **never a delivery's body, a sender's name or anything a person or an agent wrote**: bodies come only from `hive_inbox`, labelled with their sender, so a "the user says" quoted in a card or a message is data (#643). **Per CLI** (the adapter's `noticeReply`, beside `lockReply`; PreToolUse stays for locks, since its context reaches the model only after the tool ran, as PostToolUse's does): Claude Code (2.1.296) gets context at PostToolUse and Stop (a user-role reminder; Stop with context, not a block, which the user would see as "Stop hook error"); Codex (0.162.1) context at PostToolUse (a developer message, which is why the text is fixed) and a block at Stop (the reason goes on in the same turn as a user-role hook prompt); Copilot (1.0.95, checked offline against a stand-in model) top-level `additionalContext` at PostToolUse (added to the tool's result; the `hookSpecificOutput` form doesn't reach its model) and a block at Stop, whose reason comes back as a new prompt hook: Hive takes that prompt as its own in the running turn (`drainIsPrompt`, the turn ledger's injected prompts), never a new prompt or turn. A notice is answered in the hook's reply, so a working agent is never typed into; a CLI that can't carry one on an event is never asked about it.
- **How it acts.** Through the Agent API with **its own token**: a new one each time it starts, in a per-workspace file under the profile (`assistant-api/`) that its hive MCP server reads, so its launch settings don't change. When it stops, its token stops working and any question it was asking is answered no. Its session doesn't get the Agent API's token, and its hive tools ignore one inherited from the CLI. Asking to stop a busy agent stops only the run it asked about, and only if Control still allows it when the user answers. The API applies Control (not Settings → Agent API) to its calls and confines them to its workspace; with the Agent API off, the server still runs for it alone, and its hive MCP server is always there. Its tools: `hive_list_providers`, `hive_agent_activity` (status, the task it was last given, its latest reply, this turn's tool calls, locked files, when the user last typed there), `hive_wait_for_agents` (which project agents have too: #416), and as Control allows `hive_activate_project`, `hive_add_agent`, `hive_update_agent`, `hive_start_agent`, `hive_stop_agent`, `hive_prompt_agent` (with `newConversation`, #437: the agent's conversation ends, kept in the Sessions tab, and it starts in a new one on the task, as Hand Over to… itself does but with no handover, through `sessions.newConversation` and the provider's adapter; the same refusals, and also while Hive is typing into it; the Assistant's Control and session are checked again after the stop and just before the new launch spawns, so a request whose permission ended meanwhile starts nothing), `hive_hand_over` (Hand Over to…: the first agent writes a handover, Hive waits until it exists, then starts the second on it; refused at once when project agents lack Hive's tools or the user just typed in either), `hive_create_project`. Project agents don't get these (AGENT_API.md lists them).
- **Its rules** (enforced by Hive, and in its instructions): a first task goes on the CLI's command line, last and behind `--` (`claude … -- "<task>"`, `codex … -- "<task>"`, so extra arguments ending in a list option such as `--allowedTools a b` or Codex's `--image a.png` can't take it as one of their values, #523, Darren's decision; extra arguments never hold a bare `--` of their own, which would end the options early or be read as the task (Claude Code 2.1.296 runs on a second `--`): Settings, Project Settings and Settings → Assistant refuse one when saved ("Hive passes the task after `--`; remove it from extra arguments"), a launch with one saved before refuses to start, before anything changes, and an option whose value is `--` is written `--option=--`; Hive doesn't guess what a bare `--` meant; Copilot's `-i "<task>"` is an option, which ends a list already; through cmd.exe, below, it becomes one plain line); a task is typed into an agent only when it is idle (ready or finished), never while it works, starts or waits for the user, and never while the user may still be writing there: within **Pause after you type** (Settings → Assistant, default 15 seconds; **No pause** turns it off) of their last keystroke in its terminal, unless that keystroke was Enter and **Enter ends the pause** is on (the default). Hive records the user's keystrokes per terminal, ignoring the terminal's own replies (focus, cursor and device reports, colour queries such as Codex's at start, ended by BEL or ST); stopping a working, waiting or starting agent asks the user on a card at the top of the panel (or a notification with the panel hidden), and the call waits up to 10 minutes for the answer; a worktree only if the user asked or agreed; at most **30 changes per message** from the user (a new turn starts with each prompt it receives), then the API answers 429 and it must ask to go on. A batch of cards (`hive_update_tasks`) counts as one change, however many cards it has (#488: a bulk move never stops part-way for the limit), and applies none of them when no change is left. Every change and refusal is listed in the panel (**Done by the Assistant**: the last three, or all) and in hive.log.
- **The user's view never moves.** Its actions don't change the selected project, the page or the focus. Pages that weren't given a layout by hand follow their agents as usual. A new agent on the project on screen marks its page's button with a dot until that page is shown; on another project, that project opens on the new agent's page next time.
- **Trusting a folder.** When a CLI asks whether to trust a folder before it starts (Claude Code and Codex: "trust this folder"), the agent shows as **waiting** ("Asks whether to trust this folder") and notifies, instead of looking stuck in Starting, for any agent; the Assistant tells the user.
- **Settings.** **Settings → Assistant** holds the defaults: the provider (default: the default provider), the default mode, **Highlight Compact over** (500,000 tokens; see Compact, §6.2), and per provider the model, effort, permission mode, 200K context (Claude Code) and extra arguments. The model and effort default to the agents' (empty: Settings → each provider's defaults; until 0.3 they were lighter, Sonnet with low effort, which made the Assistant careless, so 0.2's saved defaults move to the agents') and the mode defaults to the provider's `assistantMode`, the one where the CLI approves safe actions itself and only asks about risky ones, as agents default to: **Auto** for Claude Code, **Approve for me** for Codex (never a plan mode, which blocks MCP tools). That it only looks and advises comes from its instructions, not its mode. A provider can warn about a mode it may not run with a model (`modeCaveat` in its descriptor): Settings → Assistant, Assistant Settings and the agent dialogs say so under the mode when the model that would run doesn't take it. For Claude Code's Auto, whether a model takes it depends on the CLI's version and the account (#129), and is what Claude Code reports (`supportsAutoMode` in its initialize reply: absent for Haiku 4.5 with 2.1.289, §10); such a session runs in Manual instead (2.1.286 and 2.1.289), which the warning says. Without the reply's word, the shipped guess ("may not offer Auto with Haiku"). The real-CLI suite (`claude-real`) launches the Assistant with Haiku in Auto and checks that the mode Claude Code itself shows (`LiveSessionState.modeObserved`: from its footer, a hook or its transcript, never the mode asked for at launch, which `permissionMode` starts as) is the one it said, that Hive shows that mode, and that it warns exactly when Auto isn't offered; `automode` runs the same checks with the fake Claude Code acting out each answer (Haiku takes Auto, doesn't, or the CLI doesn't say) (#234). It is a warning, not a rule: Hive shows the mode the CLI really runs in. **Assistant Settings** in the panel (⋯, or clicking the footer's model) overrides any of them for the workspace; they are stored on the home's agent. Changing its provider or persona while it runs asks, stops the conversation and starts a new one. Its provider is for new conversations: any of its past conversations resumes in the provider it ran in (#506, §13 Providers), so the Sessions tab never refuses one for running in another provider.
- **Modes** (#259; files and code still say *persona*). Files in `.hive/personas` with a header (`name`, `description`, `icon`, and `summary`: the mode's habits in a few lines, on one line or a `summary: |` block) and the instructions. A mode changes what the Assistant puts first, its defaults and its output shape, never its tools or permissions. Hive ships four: **Coordinator** (the default, `DEFAULT_PERSONA`), **Planner**, **QA triager** and **Release manager**, in a neutral tone, with no procedure or permissions of their own. They are kept up to date in each workspace like the bundled skills (§7.1): added when the workspace has never had them, an untouched copy of an older version updated, an edited one kept (with **Update available** when it came from an older version, and **Revert to Default**), a deleted one left deleted (with **Restore**). **Switching** (the header's mode menu, **Use in This Workspace**, or Assistant Settings) never restarts it: the workspace keeps the choice, and a running Assistant is told in its conversation, which keeps its context: Hive types `[Hive] Mode: <name> (chosen by the user). <summary> Your tools and permissions are unchanged.` (the last sentence always Hive's, never the file's; a file without a summary gives its first lines). It is a delivery (#649: a control notice for that launch), offered only while the Assistant is idle and the user isn't typing there, else at its next idle (only the latest switch; a stop drops it: `assistantMode.ts`). Each conversation records the mode it was last given once its message is received (`SessionRecord.persona`: the one it started in, or the last one it was told), and **a resumed conversation** in another mode, or with none recorded (one from before this), is told its mode the same way, once (#334): a switch while it was stopped, or a message a stop dropped, reaches it on resume. It is checked as taken as every line of Hive's is (#376): Enter again once if its CLI doesn't report it submitted, since a CLI still drawing a resumed conversation can lose the Enter (never over the user's typing; still not taken, the agent's pane says so). A switch made while a resume's check or an older message is under way wins: each home counts its switches, and older work is dropped. Resumes also get the mode's full instructions: Claude Code (2.1.267 on) records a conversation's system prompt on its first request and sends that record on every resume until it compacts, so the adapter resumes the Assistant with `--system-prompt-snapshot off` (unless the user's own arguments set it); agents have no launch instructions and are unaffected. **Restart in This Mode…** (in the same menu, while it runs) asks, then stops and resumes the same conversation with the mode's full instructions, which re-caches it. Its launch instructions say the user picks the mode and that it may suggest another in one line when a request clearly fits it, never switch or insist. **Retired personas:** Overseer, Orchestrator and Reviewer (`RETIRED_PERSONAS`: Coordinator, Coordinator, QA triager) are no longer shipped; the sync removes a workspace copy that is a version Hive shipped (nothing of the user's), keeps an edited one, moves a workspace's Assistant that chose a removed one to its mode, and says so once in a notice. Config version 7 does the same for Settings → Assistant's default. The **Modes** section of the Assistant view (below) lists, creates, edits (with the unsaved-edits protection of the other editors), deletes and restores them.
- **Assistant view** (the robot in the activity bar, "Hive Assistant"): the sidebar has a **summary** of what the Assistant has used, with the Overview's periods (conversations, prompts, tokens, API-equivalent cost; not counted in any project's Overview), then the sections **Personas**, **Conversations** and **Attachments** (#243, #387), each folding. **All Conversations** shows its conversations in the main area with the same tree as a project's Sessions tab (§ Sessions tab: by provider, with the conversations' sub-sessions; search, counts, Archive All / Delete All with the in-use checks, can't-resume markers, reading in full, export, rename, resume), in its own words and with Hive's conversations only; **Show** on the running one opens the panel. **All Attachments** shows the files pasted or dropped into its conversations (kept like agents' in its home's `.hive/attachments/<conversation>`) with the project Attachments tab's grid, filters, viewer and actions, grouped by conversation (name, count, last time; newest first; archived conversations last, marked): a group's name opens that conversation in the tree, and its bin moves all its attachments to the Recycle Bin after asking (`attachments:trashGroup`: all or none, none if another program has one open; deleting attachments follows the Attachments tab's rules below). Main reads them through the session-host path (`assertSessionHost`), and watches the home for new ones. A persona opens in the editor. The panel's ⋯ has **All Conversations…** and **Manage Personas…**, which open this view; the commands **Show Assistant Conversations**, **Show Assistant Personas** and **Show Assistant Attachments** do too (its id, `view.assistantImages`, and the section's, `images`, are from before #387, kept).
- **The panel.** Header: provider, the persona menu (no status dot since #399: the status line says it), Compact and Stop while it runs, and when it doesn't, **Resume** (|▷, its last conversation, when there is one it can resume with its provider) and New conversation, or Start when there is nothing to resume, as icon buttons (below 380 px of panel width they fold into ⋯, except Resume), ⋯ (New Conversation while it runs or Resume when it doesn't, Resume a Conversation…, All Conversations…, Assistant Settings…, Manage Personas…, Move Panel to the Left/Right, Hide), and hide. Under it, the **status line** (#312, `AssistantStatus`, wording in `assistantStatusLine` in `shared/assistant.ts`): "Status: " and Not running, Starting…, Working (or its status message), Idle (· n background tasks running), Waiting on n background tasks, Waiting for you (waiting, sign-in, error and a question it works on through are the attention tone: the warning colour's AA shade, `--attention-fg` (#399: 4.5:1 or more on the line's tint in both themes; the status icon keeps its own colour), a faint tint), "Waiting for your approval: <title>" (n questions) while Hive's own questions its actions wait on are pending (`assistantQuestions`, the cards under the line: first, over working, idle, a watch and the CLI's waiting; sign-in and an error keep their words with " · waiting for your approval"; answered, the line is as before), or a card watch's "Waiting for #12 #13 → Review (watch until 22:22)", each card the app's card chip (#399, `TaskChip` in `CardChip.tsx`, the one an agent's card uses: icon and `#n` tinted with the card's column colour from Settings → Board, the eye in Review; a click or Enter opens it, `taskOpen`), side by side without commas (the tooltip's whole line keeps them); one line cut with an ellipsis, the whole line in its tooltip. It starts with the Assistant's **status icon** (`AssistantMark`, #399): the Assistant's own glyph in the status's colour (the agents' dot colours), filled while it runs something (starting, working, background, waiting, sign-in, error), outlined while it doesn't (idle, watching, stopped), with the agent's status `dot` as a badge on its corner, so it reads as an agent's status does and isn't colour alone. Under the status line (and Hive's question cards), **Done by the Assistant** (#312, `AssistantActions`; between the status and the workspace since #399), not shown until it has changed something: a header button with the mark, "Done by the Assistant (n)" (`aria-expanded`), that folds the section to that row (remembered per workspace: `ui.panes`, `assistant-actions:<workspace>`), then the last 3 changes newest first (rows of one height, 24 px, a setting's with its Revert button too, so the 3 rows are the list's height unfolded; ✓ or ⊘, text, Revert for a setting, time ago; tooltip: date and time, "Not done:" reason, the setting's old → new) in the shared `ShowAllList` (#352): Show all scrolls every change inside the same height, Show fewer goes back to the top. It sits above the workspace and the terminal, so the terminal refits as the section appears, folds or unfolds (its `ResizeObserver`). The header no longer repeats the status as text. **Panel side** (`settings.assistant.panelSide`, `right` by default; one app-wide setting, not per workspace): Settings → Assistant → Panel side, the command `assistant.moveSide` (View menu and palette, its label saying where it goes: "Move Assistant Panel to the Left"), and ⋯. On the left the panel (or its folded strip, arrow pointing right) sits between the sidebar and the main area, with its border and resizer on its right edge (`PaneResizer edge="right"`, the same `assistant` width); the Progress panel stays on the right. The tip card and toasts only move aside for the panel when it is on the right (`.assistant-panel:not(.on-left)` in `CornerPlacement`). At the top, as tall as it needs up to a third (drag the line under it to set its share; double-click to go back): the workspace at a glance, with the agents that need you first (the attention inbox, §14), then each active project (or one with an agent running) and its agents, listed under its name with a fixed indent whatever the panel's width (#240; lined up under the name, after its fold chevron; click one to go to it), each with the short card chip and an activity line (#311, `activityOf` in `Assistant.tsx`): its status when it needs the user (waiting, a question, sign-in, error), else its open progress run in a few words (`agentOpenRun`, `runWords` in `shared/progress.ts`: "e2e: 12 suites 4/12", "· stopped reporting" when stale), else its status (background tasks, a card watch's label); nothing while it isn't running. One line: the activity shrinks first with an ellipsis, then the name (to 3 em); the chip stays whole. **Each agent's row is backed** (#399, after the Performance chart; `rowBacking` in `Assistant.tsx`): a soft wash of its status's colour while it runs something (working, background, starting: 16%), the chart's faint diagonal hatch in its status's colour while it waits (watching, waiting or a question, sign-in, error), plain while it's idle or stopped; the backing fades out to the right (a mask), and hover adds the usual highlight. Its padding reaches out over the indent, so its dot still lines up under the project's name. The activity is in the muted grey on a plain row and in the text colour on a backed or hovered one: at least 4.5:1 on every backing in both themes (the muted grey isn't, on a tint). The "N inactive projects" row has 4 px above it, clear of the last agent. The row's tooltip: status, every Doing and Reviewing card with its title, progress (with its step) and the worktree's branch; the other inactive projects fold into one "N inactive projects" row (a button, `aria-expanded`) that expands them, remembered per workspace. **Each project folds** (#399): the chevron before its name (a button, `aria-expanded`, Enter or Space) folds it to one line: its name, its most urgent agent's dot (`mostUrgent`; a stopped dot when none runs; none with no agents) and "n running", "not running" or "no agents", which keep their room while a long name gives way with an ellipsis; the name still goes to the project, the summary unfolds it. Remembered per workspace and project (`ui.assistantFold`, a per-project preference by `<workspace>|<project path>` in lower case, folded ones only, the newest 200; `ui:setProjectPref`, merged per project across windows). It scrolls under its title, which stays put with the counts. Then its terminal, and a footer like an agent pane's (model and effort, mode, context, cost). When it isn't running: **Start Assistant**, Resume, and its past conversations. Hiding the panel doesn't stop it; the strip's status icon (the mark with its dot, #399) shows what it's doing. **The Assistant's mark** (`AssistantMark`, Darren's pick of three on #399: a hive cell with a crown in it) is used wherever the Assistant is listed: the status line and the strip (with its status), the Done by the Assistant header, its runs in the Progress panel (in place of the provider's icon), its row in the attention inbox (the item's dot on it) and in the Workspace Overview (its group, its table row, and its running row, with its status). The activity bar keeps the robot. Its colours are tokens (`--mark-accent`, `--mark-off`) that keep 3:1 on the sidebar in both themes.

### 6.6 Task board

- **What it is.** One board per workspace, in `<Workspace>/.hive/tasks` (one JSON file per card, so the workspace can commit it; §4.4). Cards have a number (#12, unique in the workspace and never reused, including past cards whose files were lost or damaged), a title, a Markdown description, a project (or none: about the workspace), an agent of that project (optional; its name is kept for when the agent is removed), labels (up to 12), a **blocked** reason, the cards it **depends on** and **related** cards, comments and a history of who changed what (**You**, **Assistant**, or the agent, "Agent 2 (hive)"). Card files edited by hand are read safely (anything missing or of the wrong type gets a default).
- **Columns, fixed** (#170): **On Hold**, **Todo**, **Doing**, **Review**, **Passed**, **Done** (`hold`, `todo`, `doing`, `review`, `passed`, `done`), so the Assistant, agents and the API all speak the same six; not configurable. **On Hold** is parked work nobody picks up: only the user and the Assistant put cards there (a project agent's move there, or a card it creates there, is refused, 403; it may take one out when the user asks it to work on it). **Passed** is work whose review passed, waiting to be merged: the reviewer moves the card there with its verdict. **Done means merged**: the builder moves its cards there after merging its branch (the merge-ready skill), or the user does. **Blocked** (a reason, or cards it depends on) stays a property of a card in any column: a blocked card doesn't move to On Hold by itself. Cards in On Hold and Passed are never stalled (only Doing cards are, and cards in Doing or Review that an agent in a card loop left with no watch, #376). Nothing moves on upgrade: cards already in Done stay there. Agent states aren't columns (they change by the second): a Doing card shows its agent's live status (working, waiting on background tasks, needs your input, finished, not running), and a Doing card whose agent has finished gets a green edge. **Blocked** is a red flag on a card in any column. **Archive** hides a card from the board but keeps it searchable (the **Archived** view), and **Bring Back** returns it to the end of its column. **Archived (n)** in the board's toolbar shows the archived cards as a data table (#249; `DataTable`, below): number, title, project (the whole board only), labels, agent (the last it had), **From** (the column it was archived from), **Archived** (its latest "Archived…" in its history, `archivedAt()`) and **Created**, the dates in the shared date format; newest archived first; filters: text for number, title and agent, a choice of project, label (a card with several shows for each) and From; pages with rows per page (`table-rows:archived-cards`). The board's search narrows the rows too (both apply). A row (click or Enter) opens the card, read-only as an archived card is. **Unarchive** on a row, or **Unarchive Selected** for the rows ticked (the header's box ticks every row the filters let through, on every page), brings each back to the end of the column it was archived from, as Bring Back does; nothing else changes an archived card. Cards in Done are **archived automatically** after **Settings → Board → Archive Done cards after** days (14, or **Never**; the same for every workspace), counted from when the card last went into Done or was brought back, not its last change; Hive checks when a workspace opens, every hour and when the setting changes, and the card's history says so (`archivedFor: 'done'`).
- **Column colours.** Each column has a colour on its top edge and heading, and a subtle tint of it on its cards (mixed with the theme's background, so it reads in light and dark): On Hold grey, Todo slate blue, Doing blue, Review purple, Passed teal, Done green. A card's tint follows its column (a colour saved before a column existed falls back to its default). The state edges (blocked red, stalled amber, finished green) stay solid over the tint, with their text. **Settings → Board → Colour columns** turns it off; **Column colours** has a picker per column (stored as `#rrggbb` in `board.colors`; anything else falls back to the default) with a reset for a changed one.
- **Long text on a card** (#372). Text the user or an agent wrote stays inside the card however long its words (hashes, paths): the title and the blocked, stalled and reviewing notes wrap anywhere (`overflow-wrap: anywhere` on the note's text, `.task-note-text`, its icon at the top); the blocked reason is clamped to 3 lines with the whole in its tooltip ("Blocked: …") and in the card dialog, whose Blocked field wraps to show it all (up to six lines, then it scrolls; one line of text: Enter and pasted line breaks become spaces). A long label ends in "…" (whole on hover); the agent line's status stays on one line while the name gives way. Elsewhere: tooltips break long words (`overflow-wrap: break-word`) and are never taller than the window: one that would be is measured again as wide as the window allows (`.tip.wide`), and only if it still doesn't fit is it cut at the window's height with its end faded (`.tip.clipped`); and a sidebar row with a tooltip (the Board's Stalled list) ends its title in "…".
- **An agent's card.** An agent with cards given to it in **Doing** shows the first (in board order) as a chip with the board icon, in the Doing column's colour (plain with Colour columns off): only `#4` (and `+1` with more) in its agent strip tab, whose tooltip lists them, in its pane header at every width (#311; the chip's tooltip lists Doing and Reviewing with titles) and on its row in the Assistant's workspace overview (whose row tooltip lists them); `#4 title` on its row in the Overviews' running lists. The short chip keeps its size: the agent's status gives way first. Clicking it opens the card's dialog in place. The project's tooltips (the compact rail tile's, the sidebar's agent count and status, the project header's status) show its agents as a table (#529, `AgentsTable`): **Agent** (with its status dot) · **State** (working, waiting on n background tasks, watching, ready, not running…) · **Card** (the card in Doing, else "reviewing #n …", cut to one line) · **Waiting for** (a watch's cards and column, "#506, #509 → Review"; the column only when one waits). Rows are in lane order (`laneOrder`, `shared/agentTable.ts`): each running or paused lane's builder then its reviewer, the lanes in the Progress panel's order (#568: the user's own once they move one), then the other agents in the project's order. **The same table elsewhere** (#572; `AgentRowsTable`, each place choosing its columns after Agent, five columns taking smaller shares of the room, `cols-5`): **Stop All**'s confirm (the running agents: Agent · State · Card · Waiting for), **Start New (All)**, **Archive and Start New (All)** and **Remove All**'s (`BatchAgentsTable`: Agent · State · Card · Worktree (its branch; Remove All's "merged and clean" or "always kept (reason)", `removeWorktreeText`) · What happens (`batchOutcome`: "Stopped (will be interrupted), then a new session", "Removed"…, in the warning colour when stopping interrupts something)), in a wide confirm with the note under it (`AgentsDetail`; in a dialog the table takes its width and a long cell wraps rather than being cut, so a kept worktree's reason reads in full); **Every table has a plain-text form** made from the same rows (`tableText`, `agentRowsText` in `shared/agentTable.ts`): a line an agent with every cell the table shows, in its order, the card too ("• Worker — Working… · card #12 Lanes · worktree hive/w · Stopped (will be interrupted), then a new session"; "card reviewing #9 …" for a reviewer; an empty cell left out), a heading's line ("workspace-a:") over its agents. In a dialog (`AgentsDetail`) the table is `aria-hidden` and the plain text, visually hidden, is what screen readers get, so nothing is said twice; it is also the confirm's `detail`. **The quit dialog** (quit, close window, close or switch workspace) lists its agents as the table, wide: Agent · Project · State (a watcher's watch) · Card · Stopping it (only when one is busy: will be interrupted, background tasks will stop, waiting for you, its card loop pauses, in the warning colour), under a heading row per workspace when several windows are open, with its plain text grouped the same way; main adds each agent's name, id and card (`withCards`: each workspace's board read once, as the question is held, then shown). **The status bar's running sessions** (`RunningAgentsTip`): the counts, then the workspace's running agents as the table (Agent · Project · State · Card · Waiting for), at most 24 rows ("and n more"), with the table's plain text (every running agent) as the item's `aria-description`. It shows whenever the project has agents (one idle agent too). The tooltip is wider than a plain one but never scrolls: each column takes at most its share of the room the window leaves, so all four fit at any width and zoom, and a longer cell (a card title, an agent's name) is cut with an ellipsis. Keyboard: the rail tile is a button (Tab reaches it, Enter or Space opens the project), and it, the sidebar's status and the header's badge show the table on keyboard focus; each has the same rows as text in its accessible description. The row itself is unchanged. Todo, Review and Done cards aren't shown; it follows the board live. The Assistant has no cards. **Each session records its cards**: whenever an agent has cards in Doing (the board changed, or its session was recorded), main adds those not already there to the running session's record in `sessions.json` (`cards: [{ number, title }]`, in order, the title as it was then; only for a session Hive has recorded). The Sessions tab shows **Worked on #5 Prompt snippets, #7 …** under the transcript's toolbar; each card links to its dialog, and a deleted card shows its number and recorded title without a link.
- **Reviews.** An agent asked to review a card (check its work, often another agent's) marks it rather than taking it: `review: "start"` on `hive_update_task` / `PATCH /v1/tasks/{n}` (a card in Review, on its own, by an agent of the card's project through its own token; the user, the Assistant and scripts don't) sets `TaskCard.review` (the reviewer's id and name, and since when). The card stays in Review with the agent that did the work. `passed` or `failed`, with the verdict as the comment, ends it ("Review passed" / "Review failed" in the history); with `passed`, a `column: "passed"` in the same change moves it to Passed, which the review-agent-work and card-loop skills tell reviewers to do (#170; `"done"` still works, for a user's explicit say-so). A `failed` verdict with any other column than `review` is refused (400): a failed card stays in Review for its fixes. One reviewer at a time: another agent's start is refused, naming the reviewer; the same agent's start renews its mark (saved, without another history line); only the reviewer gives the verdict, and only while its review is going: once the card has moved on (out of Review for fixes, or the review ended with the reviewer's session), an old verdict is refused, so it can't settle newer work; a new review starts again. **Returning a failed card for review** (#214): a failed verdict leaves the card in Review, so a move to Review by the card's own agent (its hive tools, or the Agent API with its own token) or the user (the board's **Move to → Review (next round)**), on a card in Review whose latest verdict failed with nothing since that starts another round (a move, a review started, another agent, an earlier return), records "Returned for review, round N" (N: one more than the reviews failed since it last passed one). That is a move into Review for waits (`movedIntoSince`), so it wakes a reviewer waiting for the card's return, whose line says so (`#159 (Claudette's card) was returned for review (round 2)`), and it starts a new round (the failed verdict is no longer named). Any other move to the column a card is already in (by another agent, the Assistant, a script; a card not failed, or already returned) is no change. The review also stops (with a line in the history) when the card leaves Review or its project, when the reviewer's session ends (`session-exit`) and when the reviewer is removed (`endReviews`). The board shows "Reviewing: <name> · <how long>" on the card (eye icon; click shows the reviewer), in amber with the reason when the reviewer has gone or isn't running (`reviewStalled`); the reviewer's chip (strip tab, pane header, the Overviews' running list) shows the card with an eye in the Review column's colour when it has none in Doing, and tooltips say "reviewing #n". Its session records the card as reviewed (`cards[].review`), shown as **Reviewed #n** beside **Worked on** in the Sessions tab. Agents' session contract, the tool's description and the bundled review-agent-work skill (which also covers finding the exact state reviewed, findings with repro and impact, and reviewing again after fixes) say that reviewing isn't working on a card: only fixes they're asked to make move it to Doing. Cards and records without these fields read as before.
- **Waiting on cards** (#128). An agent that has to wait for another agent's work on a card (a builder for its review, a reviewer for the fixes) waits without spending tokens: `hive_wait_for_tasks` / `POST /v1/tasks/wait`, on up to 20 of the caller's cards (a project agent: its project's; others are unknown to it, 404). What counts as a change: a column move, a new comment, a review verdict, a new agent (`changes`, default all). `column` alone waits until a card is in that column (at once if it already is), and nothing else counts: a comment on a card still in Doing doesn't end a wait for it to reach Review. `column` with `changes` naming `column` waits until one *moves into* it, or for another change listed (the builder's "a verdict, or into Passed"). Either way a card already in that column answers at once (#434: a builder watching for "→ Passed" on a card already passed must not wait for a move that has happened), whoever waits and whatever its history. **`fresh`** (with `column`) asks for the next move into it instead: a card already there waits until it leaves and comes back (the card's history is checked, so a quick out and back between checks still counts) or, in Review, is **returned for review** (the reviewer's wait for a failed card's next round); `fresh` without `column`, or `column` with `changes` that don't name it, is refused. A watch keeps the agent's view of the board: a project agent's own project's cards (worked out from the agent, never from what is saved), the Assistant's whole board. A card moved to another project, archived or deleted counts as **gone** ("#12 is gone from your board (archived, deleted or moved to another project)") and nothing about its new state is told; a line that fired is checked again just before it is typed.
  - **`wake: true`** (an agent or the Assistant; not scripts) starts a **card watch** and answers at once: the agent ends its turn, and **A wake is confirmed taken** (#376: on 7 Oct a Codex reviewer's wake was typed and its Enter dropped, so the line sat unsent in its prompt for hours while its watch had ended). **How the line goes in** (#597; since #650 the only line Hive types is the doorbell, or the user's own task for an agent with no inbox): `sendPrompt` clears the prompt (Ctrl+U), puts the line in and presses Enter. A CLI busy for a moment (a CPU-pinned machine, its own work after a turn) reads all of that in one go, and Claude Code (2.1.296) takes a read of several characters as a paste in which Hive's Ctrl+U and Enter are text: the line isn't sent by Enter, nor by Enter again, and only a third Enter (the user's) sends it. On 8–11 Oct about 2% of wakes went so, the Assistant's and agents' alike (9 of 492 and 8 of 531 in Hive's log: not long lines, a narrow pane or the end of a turn, which a narrow 45-column pane with Assistant-sized lines, idle and straight after long turns, took every time). So a CLI that reads a bracketed paste as text however it arrives (`capabilities.bracketedPaste`: Claude Code) gets the line as one (`ESC[200~…ESC[201~`, control characters left out) while its terminal has bracketed paste on (Hive's screen of it, `TerminalScreen.bracketedPaste()`); a line starting with `/` is still typed, since a pasted slash command is sent as a message. Codex and Copilot still get it typed in pieces. Claude Code shows a pasted line of more than about 800 characters as `[Pasted text #1]` in its prompt until it is sent; the conversation shows it whole. After typing it, Hive waits up to 10 s (`TAKE_MS`) for the CLI to take a prompt (UserPromptSubmit: `promptsTaken`). If it doesn't, Hive presses Enter once more (`submitAgain`: the same launch, idle, nobody typing, Hive not typing, and no input of the user's since the line began: #652, a line the user typed into is their draft, whatever the typing pause, so Hive never sends or clears it; what it named waits again, and nothing more is typed there until a prompt is sent, `leaveToUser`), as soon as that is safe within six times that wait (#430: not given up at once), waits again, and if it still isn't taken, logs it; the loop check above then flags the card. **Still not taken** (#430: in 0.4.0 the Assistant's and project agents' Claude Code sessions sometimes weren't reading their input at all, and took the line by themselves 1 to 19 minutes later; not the window's focus, which quiet test copies ruled out), the agent is marked (`untakenLine`: "A line Hive typed wasn't taken" in its pane header, and in the Assistant's status line), the user is told once, and Hive types nothing more into it until its CLI takes a prompt (the user's Enter alone isn't proof). The same holds, centrally in `sendPrompt` (checked before the input is cleared, before each piece and before Enter) and in a wake's delivery, while a wake is being confirmed (held from its typing until it is taken, marked not taken or the launch ends, however long the wait for a safe Enter again: `holdForConfirm`), and while any other line Hive typed in the last 30 s isn't taken yet (`lineWaiting`), so nothing is typed over a line being confirmed; `hive_prompt_agent` is 409 meanwhile. The notice says whether Enter was pressed again. Enter stays at most twice a wake. The log has each wake's typed (`Woke …`), taken (`… was taken after N ms`), Enter again and not taken. When a watched card changes, Hive gives the agent its **wake**: a card event delivery (#649) that it reads with `hive_inbox`, rung by the doorbell while it is idle (#650; told at its next tool call if it works), whose text is `[Hive] #12 is in Review; latest comment by Codex (hive): "first line…". Your card watch has ended: carry on (…)`. **The Assistant's card wake while lanes run** (#402) ends with `Lanes are still running (hive_lane list): before you end your turn, watch them all again in one watch, each lane by its last card (run-lanes).` whenever a lane of the workspace is active or paused (`withLanesTail`, kept whole: the rest of the line is cut to make room): a model that reads no skill on a wake otherwise ended its turn watching nothing. **Changes landing together are told together** (#224): a watch fired by a change waits 1.5 s (`SETTLE_MS`) before its line is made, and it is made then (as it is handed to the delivery service) from every watched card's changes since the watch began (`wakeLines`): one card as below; several, each with where it is and its verdict, `[Hive] #217 is in Review: Codex (hive) passed it (latest comment …); #119 is in Review: Codex (hive) failed it (…). …`, comments cut first; when even that is too long, every card is still named, in a few words without names or comments (`#217 in Review, passed; #119 in Review, failed`, a gone card as `#12 gone`) or by number alone, and the line says details were left out and to read each card (#226). **No gap between watches:** a watch keeps, per card, where its view ended (`seen`: the last history entry and comment it counted), and counts comments, verdicts and moves into a column by the entries after it, never by time, so an entry sharing a millisecond with one already seen, even one alike in author and words, still counts, and the one seen is never counted again. Every history entry and comment gets an id when written (`id`, unique on the card; the Agent API's card views and latest comment leave it out); entries from before ids are found by time, a hash of who and what, and their place. History and comments only grow at the end and are capped at the start, so when the entry seen is no longer kept, everything kept came after it. Hive remembers, per agent and card, what its wakes told (in memory, the latest 100 cards an agent): the card's mark and `seen` as the line was made from it, or for a limit line the watch's own starting point. The agent's next watch starts each such card from there (`carriedOver`), moved on only past what the agent did itself since (by its board name), kind by kind, unless someone else made that kind of change too; column, agent and archived compare with the told state. So a verdict, comment, move, reassignment or archiving by others between a wake and the next watch fires it at once; the agent's own work in between doesn't. **A move into the watched column counts for every watch with a column** (#521), not only one asked for as a move: a watch that waits for a card to be in Review, started after a wake that told the card in Review (since gone back to Doing), compared the card with that told column and never fired when it came back (on 10 Oct three Codex reviewers sat "watching" cards already in Review for five hours). So it can't be misread (#143): when the change is a review verdict, the line names the reviewer (`#12 is in Review: Codex (hive) failed it; …`); on another agent's card (a dependency, or the builder's card a reviewer waits for) it says whose (`#12 (Claude's card) is in Review`), and in Passed that Passed isn't merged (`#12 (Claude's card) is in Passed: Codex (hive) passed it (Passed isn't merged); …`; #170: before it, Done said so). In Done, which means merged, it names the verdict that passed it and nothing more. The watcher's own card and a card without an agent get no owner. A verdict is named only for the current round: once the card has gone back to On Hold, Todo, Doing or Review, a new review has started, or it has gone to another agent, an earlier one isn't (a card moved to Passed or Done by hand names none). Names in the line are cut to 40 characters and the comment's first line gives way, so the card, its state and what to do always fit the line's 1000 bytes. A condition already met answers with the change instead of watching (and ends the agent's earlier watch). One watch per agent; a new one replaces it. The line is handed over only when the agent is idle and the user isn't typing in its terminal (the same guard as the Assistant's prompts); otherwise Hive tries again on the agent's next status change and every 5 seconds. The watch stays until the agent has read its wake with `hive_inbox` (it still shows watching and takes no other work meanwhile), not when the doorbell is typed; cancelled or replaced meanwhile, its delivery is cancelled (a doorbell being typed stops before Enter, and `hive_inbox` no longer gives it), and a cancelled watch never comes back. Typing in its terminal doesn't cancel the watch. A workspace's watches change one at a time, so a board change made while a watch is being set up is checked once it is in place (and the board is checked again right after every new watch).
  - **Wait limit.** `limitMinutes` (1–1440, default 120): with no change by then, Hive wakes the agent with `[Hive] No change on #12 in 2 h: your card watch has ended. Tell the user (hive_notify) and ask what to do.`
  - **Kept.** Watches are kept in `<Workspace>/.hive/watches.json` (with each card's state when the watch began, so a change while Hive or the agent was closed still counts: they are checked as soon as they are loaded), and a stopped agent with a watch is woken when it is resumed. They belong to their workspace: its folder is taken when they load, and once it closes or its window opens another workspace they are dropped from memory and nothing more is read or written for them (a registration under way then fails). Saves are a temp file renamed into place, the rename checked against the workspace's lifetime before every try. One that lands after the workspace closed fails, and is undone only while the file is still exactly that save: it is moved aside (renamed, never deleted), and the file as it was is put back only where nothing has taken its place (otherwise kept beside it as `watches.json.before-close-<time>-<id>`, or in its staging file if even that rename is refused); anything written since is left as it is. A copy is removed only once another is confirmed, so whatever fails, the earlier watches are left in some file. Each saved watch is checked in full when read; one that isn't valid (or is another workspace's agent's, or over the limit) is kept aside in the file as it was, under `invalid`, and never acted on, and one bad entry doesn't stop the others. At most 500 watches and a 1 MB file, for what Hive writes as for what it reads, with room for each watch to grow to its longest (fired, with its line cut to 1,000 bytes, and being typed; the cards as last seen aren't saved), so a watch Hive took can always fire, be typed and end. A new watch that wouldn't fit first moves the entries kept aside to a file of their own (`watches.json.kept-aside-<time>`, created new); if it still wouldn't fit it is refused (409; replacing an agent's watch is always allowed). A file read back too big for that moves its entries kept aside, then its last watches, to such a file, so nothing is lost. Only a missing file means no watches: one that can't be read is an error. A file too big or not JSON is renamed (`watches.json.too-big-<time>`, `.damaged-<time>`) before anything is saved; if it can't be renamed, that is an error and the file is left as it is. A watch or a cancel that can't be saved is reported as failed, and nothing changes. **Followed to its receipt** (#649): a fired watch's line is handed over as a delivery, and the watch saves its id before the delivery service can type it (nothing is handed over if that can't be saved); the watch ends once that delivery is received (fetched with `hive_inbox`, #650: the only receipt), not when its doorbell is typed. A watch read back with a delivery follows it: received while Hive was closed, it has ended; ended without that, it fires again; offered when Hive stopped, the delivery is uncertain and offered once more, saying it may repeat. A watch read back from before #649 marked as being typed is handed over once more, saying it may repeat. A save that fails in the background is tried again by the 5-second tick. Keeping within the file's limit is measured watch by watch, each once (#321: making the whole file's text for each watch set aside, and every watch reading its 20 cards from disk at each check, took seconds at 400 watches); one check of the watches reads each card once, whatever the number of watches on it.
  - **While watching** the agent shows **watching** (§6.3) and its pane header says what for ("Waiting for #12 → Review", eye icon; short of room only the label shortens, with the whole in its tooltip) with **Cancel**, also **Cancel Card Watch** in the agent's menu; the agent can cancel its own watch (`cancel: true`). It takes no other work meanwhile: `hive_prompt_agent` is 409 and Start… can't choose it. *Quit when agents finish* waits for it while a card it watches is in Doing or Review with another agent (the work it waits for is going on), and not otherwise. Every status reply says what it waits for: `statusMessage` is the watch's label, and agents have `watching { cards, column, changes, label, limitAt }` in project status, the project list, activity and `/v1/agents/wait` (the short rows: `watching`, the label).
  - **A card leaves the watch of an agent whose part in it ended** (#420). A project agent's watch records its part in each card as it begins (`on`): the card's agent, its reviewer, or a reviewer waiting for it to arrive in Review (`awaiting`, a `column: review` watch on another agent's card). A card it has no part in (another agent's card its own depends on) and the Assistant's watches are never released. The card leaves the watch (`releaseOf`) when it is given to another agent (or none) while the watcher had it; another agent reviews it while the watcher reviewed it or awaited it; or the agent an awaiting reviewer waited on is replaced by another. **A blocked card is never released** (#481, Darren's decision: it stays in its lane, paused; the reviewer never drops it): #420 released it too, so a loop moved on while the user decided, but on #468 (9 Oct) the reviewer dropped the card its builder had blocked to ask a question, and when the builder unblocked it and returned it to Review nobody reviewed it (builder waiting for a verdict, reviewer for the next card). The watch now carries on over the block; the card-loop skill says a blocked card is paused, not gone, and the loop doesn't take the next card meanwhile. Watches saved before (with `on[n].blocked`, or a `released` entry `why: 'blocked'`) still load. The watch's only card: the watcher is woken once (`[Hive] #354 was reassigned to B4 (your watch on it ended). Your card watch has ended: drop it from your list and carry on with your next card.`). With other cards, it is dropped and told first in the watch's next line (`released`), including the limit's (`… (your watch on it ended). No change on #2 in 2 h: … Drop the first from your list; for the rest, tell the user …`). A watch that had fired but whose line wasn't typed yet (the watcher was busy) checks its cards again as it is delivered, through the same release step (`applyRelease`, under the lock): a card released meanwhile is told as released, not as the change that fired it, and when that change was all it had to tell, the line isn't typed: the watch keeps its other cards and tells the release with its next line. An awaiting reviewer is also released when the card's agent is taken off it (no agent now), and a reviewing watcher when its review is taken from it (stopped, or cleared by someone else; not its own verdict). Each is noted in the card's history as Hive (`Ended R6 (hive)'s watch on it: reassigned to B4`). The card-loop skill: such a wake means drop that card and carry on, without asking. Prompted by #354/#359 moved to another lane while R6 waited on #354 (7 Oct), and #356 blocked mid-lane while R3 waited on it and lane 3 stalled (8 Oct).
  - **Without `wake`**, the call waits in place (event-driven) up to `timeoutSeconds` (default 300, at most 840, under the CLIs' tool timeouts) and replies with each change (the card, its column, who changed it last, its latest comment's author and first line) or no change, with a `since` token to pass to the next wait so nothing between them is missed. It reads the cards as the caller sees them (a card that leaves its view is gone), listens for board changes before its first check (so none falls between), checks again after a change that came during a check, and ends (409) if its workspace closes. At most two such waits per caller at once (429).
  - **The `card-loop` skill** (agents) runs a builder and a reviewer through a list of cards in turn with these watches: the builder sends a card to Review and watches for a verdict or a move into Passed; the reviewer watches for a card arriving in Review and, after failing it, for its return, and moves a card that passes to Passed with its verdict (#170; once the builder has merged its work, it moves its Passed cards to Done); the builder moves a failed card to Doing while it fixes it, then back to Review (a move to Review without leaving it returns it too, #214). Each starts its watch *before* the step that lets the other act, so a fast reply isn't missed. It counts rounds, a round being one build and its review (round 1 is the first build and review), and asks the user when the last round's review fails (5 rounds by default), and tells the user when a wait limit passes. A finding that comes back is marked recurring in the failed verdict and is another round, not a reason to stop early; one the builder disputes is a design question and stops the loop to ask (#163). **Asking the user** is the same for either side, and for any agent with a question only the user can answer (card-loop, review-agent-work, work-on-card; #840): it sets the card's `blocked` to the question and tells the user (`hive_notify`), never only a comment, since a lane watch wakes on a card becoming blocked and never on a comment (a reviewer's question left as a comment on #805 sat unseen for over an hour); once Hive tells it of the user's decision, it clears `blocked` and carries on.
  - **Card-loop defaults** (#412, Darren's decision: Rounds 10 and Wait 6h, set per workspace, a project can override, a prompt's own values win). **Settings → Workspace → Card loop rounds** and **Card loop wait** (`workspace.loopRounds`, `workspace.loopWaitHours`: whole numbers, 1–50 and 1–24 hours) are the workspace's own, kept in its `workspace.json` (`loops: { rounds, waitHours }`, absent: 10 and 6).
