They are the first settings with a value per workspace: the catalog's `settingValue`/`settingDefault`/`settingPatch` take the workspace's config for scope `workspace` (`{ workspace: { … } }`, saved by `WorkspaceService.setLoops`, which checks the range; the window through `workspace:setLoops`, the settings tools through `applySetting`, so the Assistant reads and, with Change settings, changes them). **Project Settings → Card loops → Rounds** and **Wait (hours)** (`project.loopRounds`, `project.loopWaitHours`, null inherits) override them for a project. `loopDefaults(ws, project)` (`shared/lanes.ts`) resolves them; a lane started without rounds or a wait (`startLane`) takes them, so the card-loop skill gives `hive_lane start` only the values its request named and uses the lane's from the reply (10 and 6 hours with no lane); the reviewer's `join` reply has the lane's too. coordinate-agents briefs a lane with rounds and a wait only when the user gave them. A prompt's values always win; a change applies to lanes started afterwards.
  - **Loop wakes** (#405, Darren's decision: on by default for cards with a reviewer, with a per-project opt-out). The loop only worked if every agent remembered its card watch; Hive knows each card's agent and reviewer, so it delivers the two routine handoffs itself (`main/loopWakes.ts`). `tasks.ts` reports a **handoff** once a change is saved (`onLoopEvent`): the card moved or returned into Review (`review`), or a review's verdict (`verdict`). A card's **reviewer** is the reviewer of a running lane (active or paused) with the card, else the project agent that last started reviewing it (its board name in the history); a card with neither has no wakes, so a solo user never sees one. A `review` handoff wakes the reviewer, a `verdict` the card's agent (its builder); never the agent that made the change, nor a builder reviewing its own card; never an agent whose own card watch tells it of that change (`watchCovers`, with the card's column: a watch on the card counting a move into Review; one counting a verdict, or a move into the column the verdict left the card in, so a watch for Passed alone stands in for a passing verdict that moved the card there but not for a failed one, which leaves it in Review; or one that has fired with it), checked when queued, again before typing and before each piece. **Whose wake it is is worked out again as it is delivered** (`targetOf`, from the lane and the card then): a lane whose reviewer changed while the wake waited gives it to the new reviewer, and one left with no reviewer drops it; a change to the board or the lanes while it is checked or typed stops it, and it is worked out again on the next try. Off when the project's `project.json` has `loopWakes: false` (**Project Settings → Card loops → Wake reviewers and builders**, `project.loopWakes` in the settings catalog; not inherited). The line is a card watch's, with its own tail (`loopWakeLine`: `[Hive] #12 (B1's card) is in Review. You review this card in its loop: carry on with it (hive_read_task with latestComment for the comment in full).`; the builder's: `… is in Review: R1 (hive) failed it; latest comment by …. This is your card in its loop: carry on (…)`), made from the card as it is when it is offered or read. It is a delivery (#649: a card event), made at once (#652: no settle of its own; what lands with the handoff is in it, since it is made again from the card just before it is offered or read) and given as a wake is (its doorbell only while the agent is idle, the user not typing, no line of Hive's waiting there, no fired watch of the agent's going first, one line at a time per agent, checked as taken, #376; a busy agent told at its next tool call, #650); until then it waits, saved, the latest per agent and card (made again from a newer handoff; a day each), tried on the agent's next idle status and every 5 s, and made again from the card just before each offer. It is dropped once it has nothing to tell, judged by what the card's history has since the handoff's entry (`historySince`): a reviewer's when the card has left Review or that round has been reviewed (a review started or a verdict given since, also one that has ended: a round reviewed and failed while the reviewer was busy is the builder's now); a builder's when the card is another agent's, the builder has acted on it since the verdict, or another round has started. A later arrival queues a wake of its own. Each received (read with `hive_inbox`) is noted in the card's history as Hive (`Woke R1 (hive) to review it (a loop wake)`, `Woke B1 (hive) with its verdict (a loop wake)`). The card-loop skill says Hive may wake the agent itself, and that watches stay how it waits.
  - **Lanes** (#483). A loop is registered as a **lane**, so Hive knows the loops it runs: `hive_lane` / `/v1/lanes` (Agent API §12), kept in `<Workspace>/.hive/lanes.json` (`main/lanes.ts`, logic in `shared/lanes.ts`; read and written whole under the file's lock). The builder's card-loop starts it (`start`: the cards in order, the reviewer, rounds, wait in minutes, an optional name), the reviewer's joins it by the builder's name (`join`: with its cards, only a running lane sharing one; with none such it starts one from its cards as the builder's start would, which that start then updates), and the Assistant (Control agents, through `assistantChange`) or a script can start one for any builder. **One running lane per builder:** a start sharing a card with its running lane updates it in place (resumed, a card added, the order changed; a reviewer given replaces the old one), one sharing none ends it (ended when it has no card left to do, every one Passed, Done or gone, since it only waits for its merge; else abandoned). **Lifecycle:** `active`; `paused` while it waits for the user, read from the board, not kept: its first card not in Passed or Done is blocked and no other is in Doing or Review (#512: a builder that moved on to the next card while one waits for the user is working). Its current card is the one being worked: the first in Doing that isn't blocked, else the first in Review, else the first unfinished (`currentCard` in `shared/laneBar.ts`); `ended` once every card is Done (Hive notices on the board's next change) or when ended after the merge (with its merge commit); `abandoned` when its builder or reviewer is removed from the project (`removeAgent`, and found on the next look if removed while Hive was closed), or when ended unmerged. An ended or abandoned lane keeps its cards as they were when it ended (`frozen` in the file, #512) and reads them from there, never from the board: a card another lane has taken on shows only in that one. Without that record (a lane ended before #512, or one that doesn't read back) its cards are `unrecorded`: not known, with no current card, and never read from the board. A stopped agent doesn't abandon its lane: quitting, restarts and setting changes stop agents too. Ended lanes are listed until their builder starts another or for an hour, and kept a day (at most 200). **Lean replies:** a line per lane (`B1 / R1 (hive) lanes and loops: active, on #405; #483 Passed, #405 Review r2, #412 Todo; rounds 10, wait 6 h. lane-3fa2c1`). **Scale-down:** a lane is bookkeeping, never a gate. Nothing refuses or waits on one; a solo agent on a card (work-on-card) makes none; the skill carries on if the tool is missing or a call fails; an unknown reviewer leaves the lane without one rather than refusing. The window gets `lanes:list` and `lanes-changed` (the listed lanes with each card's column, blocked reason, round and review, sent when a lane or one of its cards changes); the Agent API's stream sends `lanes-changed` with the workspace only.
- **Waiting on lanes** (#531). A coordinator follows card loops, not cards: on 10 Oct the Assistant, with seven lanes and about 25 open cards, swapped cards in and out of its 20-card watch on every wake, missed one (#390 sat in Review unreviewed), and was woken by every column move, most of which needed nothing. `hive_wait_for_tasks` / `POST /v1/tasks/wait` with `lanes` (lane ids, or `["all"]`) and `wake: true` (an agent or the Assistant) starts a **lane watch** instead of a card watch (`shared/laneWatch.ts`, `registerLaneWatch` and `evaluateLanes` in `main/watches.ts`): the agent's one watch, kept in `watches.json` like the others (`lanes`, and `laneMarks`: each lane as last measured, its state, whether its merge is due, and each card's column, blocked, stalled, and latest failed and passed verdict times), with no cards and no card limit. `"all"` follows every running lane in the watcher's view (a project agent: its project's; the Assistant: all), lanes started later too, and each lane's cards as the lane goes on (cards added included). It counts only **events**, `events` narrowing or widening them: `failed` (a review failed), `blocked` (a card became blocked: a question for the user), `stalled` (a card became stalled, by the board's own reasons, `cardStall` in `main/cardStall.ts`, shared with the Agent API's `stalled`: nobody on a Doing card, a loop agent with no watch, #376, a reviewer watching but not reviewing, #521), `merge-due` (every card passed or done, one not yet merged), `ended`, `abandoned` (the default), and `passed`, `moved` (any column change) when asked. Plain moves stay quiet. While nothing that counts happens each lane's mark moves on to now, so a card blocked again after an unblock counts; a lane that starts after an `"all"` watch began, and a card added to a lane it follows, start from an empty mark (a block or stall they come with is told once, and verdicts since the watch began). A watch on `"all"` keeps a lane it follows in view until that lane's end has been told, also across a wake: a lane that ends or is abandoned between a wake and the watch after it is told by that watch. Once a watch fires, its marks stay where they were until its line is handed over, and the line is worked out again then (`laneRound`), so events that land meanwhile are told in the same line; the marks become what that line told once the agent has read it (#649). One line tells every lane's events (`laneWakeLine`, within the wake size: `[Hive] Lane B1 / R1 (greetings): #12 failed review (R1 (alpha)) | Lane B2 / R2: #15 is blocked: "Which icon?". Your lane watch has ended: …`; reasons, then labels, then all but each lane's events give way). A watch after a wake starts each lane from what that wake told (`wokeLanes`: its marks, and when its line was made), so an event in between fires it at once; and what is new is measured from when that line was made (`laneSince`), not from the new watch: a card added to a lane or a lane started in between (also one already ended or abandoned) starts from an empty mark, so its verdicts, block, stall or end are told once. Checked on board changes, `lanes-changed`, agents' status changes (debounced) and every 30 s (a stall that comes with time). Its label is "Waiting on all running lanes" / "Waiting on 3 lanes"; its limit line says to check them (`hive_lane list`). `hive_lane start` from the Assistant nudges it to watch the lanes unless a lane watch on `"all"` (or that lane) runs, and its card wakes while lanes run end with the lanes-running tail, now pointing at a lane watch; run-lanes and coordinate-agents use one.
- **Lanes, the cards beside them and agents waiting, in one watch** (#726). On 13 Oct the release card #720 was in no lane, so HB6 waited about 45 minutes for Darren's push before the Assistant noticed, and only because another lane's merge came due: a session has one watch, and a lane watch saw only lane cards. A lane watch now takes `cards` too (up to 20, each in the caller's view, else `404`): cards beside its lanes, kept in the same record's `cond` with their `marks` and `seen` and checked as a card watch checks its cards (`check`, from what a wake told about them, `changes` defaulting to comment, column and verdict), a card becoming blocked counting too (`blocked` on the condition: a `Blocked:` entry in its history since, and still blocked). One line tells each lane's events, then each card that changed (as a card wake tells it, with why it is blocked) and each agent waiting (`laneWakeLine`). A later lane watch keeps the last lane watch's cards (the one it replaces, or the one whose wake was read: `wokeLanes`), less those since done or gone, unless it names its own (`[]` for none); its reply says which (`cards`: `now`, `kept`, `replaced`, `dropped`), and its label names them ("Waiting on all running lanes and #720"). A new default event, `waiting`: an agent of the projects it follows (the watcher's view; named lanes: their projects; never itself) starting to wait for the user, the status Hive shows as waiting (a permission prompt or a question in its pane). Each start is recorded as it happens (`waitStarts`, numbered on the events' clock, in memory); a watch counts those after its `waitSeq` whose agent still waits in the same launch (a wait answered at once has nothing to tell), so a wait is told once, not on every refresh. The line names the agent, its card (its Doing card, else the one it reviews) and what it asks when Hive knows (the status message), and its tail says to tell the user and never answer for them. Like the lanes' marks, `waitSeq` moves on while nothing counts, stays put once the watch fires, and becomes what the line told once it is read; a watch after a wake starts from there. A fired lane watch whose line has nothing left to tell when it is handed over (the wait answered, the block lifted) isn't given, and waits on. Opt out with `events`. coordinate-agents and run-lanes put a release or one-off card in the lane watch as `cards`, and say the `waiting` event means telling the user; the lanes-running tail on the Assistant's card wakes says so too.
- **Waiting on agents** (#416). Work without a card (a release step, an installer build, a push and its CI, a one-off prompt) is waited on with an **agent watch**: `hive_wait_for_agents` / `POST /v1/agents/wait` with `wake: true` (an agent or the Assistant; not scripts) answers at once and Hive gives the caller a wake (a delivery it reads with `hive_inbox`, rung by the doorbell, #650) when a watched agent **finishes its turn** (through its background tasks unless `ignoreBackground`), **waits for the user** (a question, a permission prompt, a sign-in) or **stops** (or is in error), naming every agent that did, with the start of its latest reply as its turn's end reported it: `[Hive] B6 (hive) finished: "The installer for Reinstall 12 is built…". Your agent watch has ended: carry on (hive_agent_activity for more).` Before this, the Assistant polled `hive_wait_for_agents` in calls under 300 s (Claude Code moves longer tool calls to the background): a dozen wake-ups for one 0.4.0 test run.
  - **The same watches.** An agent watch is a watch record in `watches.json` that follows agents (`agents`, each one's launch, prompts taken and whether a typed prompt was pending when it began) instead of cards; it is the agent's one watch (a card watch replaces it and it replaces one, the reply naming the card watch it replaced), checked on every status change and on the watches' tick, settled, handed over as a delivery (only while the watcher is idle and the user isn't typing there; its doorbell gets #376's Enter again once if not taken) and ended once the watcher has read it, limited (`limitMinutes`, default 120: `[Hive] B6 is still working after 2 h: your agent watch has ended. Check on it (hive_agent_activity) and watch again, or tell the user (hive_notify) if it seems stuck.`), kept across restarts and cancelled (`cancel: true`, the pane's **Cancel**) as card watches are. The watcher shows **watching** with the label "Waiting for B6 to finish" (`watching.agents` in status replies). What it tells comes from each agent's **events**: Hive records every agent's events: waiting for the user, a stop or an error, and the end of a turn under way, one seen working (or on its background tasks) in that launch since the last turn ended (finished, interrupted, or into background tasks; a launch becoming ready, also after a startup question such as folder trust or a refused sign-in, ran no turn and isn't one) (the last 8 each, numbered from the clock, whether or not anyone watches it yet: `noteAgentStatus`), and a line is made, again just before it is handed over, from each watched agent's latest event since the watch began, so one that finished and was given work again meanwhile is still told ("… finished: \"…\" (working again now)"), with any that finished since. A watch started after a wake starts each agent from the event that wake told (`wokeAgents`, as `woke` for cards), so an event in between (an agent that finished and took new work while the watcher read its wake) fires it at once; one already told isn't told again. Agents still not working when it is asked for are the answer at once (below). Events aren't kept across a restart: a watch read back is checked against the agents as they are.
  - **What counts as working**: `working`, `starting`, `background` (unless `ignoreBackground`), or a prompt Hive typed in the last 30 s that its CLI hasn't taken yet (`SessionManager.agentNow`: the agent was just given a task), also for the bounded wait, so a wait right after `hive_prompt_agent` waits for that task. A launch given its task on its command line (`hive_start_agent` or `hive_add_agent` with a prompt, `hive_prompt_agent` with `newConversation`, a card's Start) counts the same until its CLI takes that task (#712): the CLI is ready at its SessionStart and submits the task only after, and a wait in between was answered "ready" before the task began; at most 30 s after it became ready. An agent not working when the watch is asked for is the answer at once (`Already: B6 (hive) is idle: "…"`) and no watch starts, as for a card already in the column; a prompt that was pending and is never taken is told as such ("its CLI hasn't taken the prompt it was given"). Without `agents`, every agent working now (the caller excepted); never the caller itself (400), at most 32 (a project's most, 24, and more, #562).
  - **The user's replies** (#418). The Assistant's watches (never an agent's) are also told when the user sends an agent a prompt from its pane: an agent watch on that agent, a card watch on a card that agent has or reviews (`card.agent`, `card.review.agent`), in the same line as other changes (`User replied to B1 on #399: "C, the hive cell"`, `… to B1 (hive): …`; the user is named by **Settings → General → Your name** (#426: `general.userName`, "User" by default and when empty, made one line of at most 40 characters by `userNameOf`), which goes into the Assistant's context, so it is the user's to set). It is the user's when the CLI reports the prompt taken (UserPromptSubmit, with its text: `HookEvent` prompt `text`; Codex's answers to its questions without their tag) within 15 s of the user's own Enter in that pane, and later than any line Hive typed there (`SessionManager.noteUserReply`); slash commands and the Assistant's own pane aren't. Kept on the events' clock (`noteUserReply` in watches.ts, the last 200, first line to 120 characters), and counted from what the watcher's last wake told (`replySeq`, `wokeReplies`), so one is told once. In a card watch's line each reply is told once (with the watched cards its agent has or reviews), first, and fitted with the card changes within the line's size (`cardWakeLine`: what was said gives way first, then whose and the verdicts; the end always stays), and only a reply the line names counts as told. An agent watch's line too long for each in full says whom the user replied to and which agents changed, by name, else by count (`Darren replied to 12 watched agents; 3 watched agents changed`): the user's replies are always told as theirs (`agentWakeLine`). **Settings → Assistant → Tell the Assistant when I reply to an agent** (`assistant.tellReplies`, default on) off: none is kept or told, also a reply already waiting to be typed in a wake (`revokeReplies`, and the setting is checked again whenever a reply is picked, and before every piece of a wake that tells one is typed and before its Enter: also when it was turned off and on again in between (each revocation raises a generation the wake was made under: `replyGeneration`), what was typed is cleared, the wake is dropped without counting anything as told, and the watch fires again on whatever else changed, without the reply, or waits on); turned on again, none comes back. The Assistant may read it, not change it (its own permissions). The coordinate-agents skill: a reply that decides something is recorded on the card as a decision, not asked again.
  - **Who may watch whom.** Any agent of the workspace's projects (never the Assistant), as the bounded wait has always allowed: agents' status is open to every caller. Project agents now have `hive_wait_for_agents` too (it left the Assistant-only tools), but a project agent's line leaves out the reply of another project's agent (`shownTo`), whose conversation holds that project's cards, as its activity is closed to it. The `coordinate-agents` skill prefers an agent watch to polling for card-less waits.
- **Who may do what.** The user does everything from Hive. Agents (through their hive tools or the Agent API) and the Assistant create cards, change them, give them to agents, comment, block them and move them between the columns, Done included; putting a card On Hold is the user's and the Assistant's (a project agent is refused, 403). **A project agent works on its own project's cards only**: known by its own Agent API token (never by what a request says), it lists, reads, comments on, moves, creates and orders only its project's cards; another project's card, or one with no project, is to it as if it didn't exist (404, the same as a missing number), a card it creates is its project's and can't move to another, a linked or blocking card from another project shows only as its number (listed in `elsewhere`) and stays when it edits the list, and its top, bottom and reorders work among its project's cards in the column without moving other projects' cards. Since a card started on an agent is that agent's prompt, it also can't read another project's agents' activity or session list, or type into them (403), and its event stream (`/v1/events`) has only its own project's session events; their status stays open to it. The Assistant, the user's board and scripts with the workspace token see the whole board. An agent runs as the user and could read the workspace token from disk, so this keeps agents in their lane rather than containing a hostile one. **An agent that moves a card into Doing takes it**: through its hive tools, moving a card into Doing from another column without naming an agent gives the card to it (if it is an agent of the card's project), also when another agent had it (out of Doing, nobody was working on it; the history says "Given to …"), and so is a card it creates in Doing; naming an agent (or none, with empty) wins, a card already in Doing keeps its agent, and the Assistant, the user and other Agent API callers give it to nobody. **Review is the default for finished work**: the agents' session contract and the work-on-card skill (which a card's prompt points to) tell agents to move a card to Review when its work is done, also one that was in Passed or Done before (more work on a finished card is Done → Doing → Review); a reviewer moves a card that passes to Passed; Done means merged, so an agent moves a card there once its work is merged, or when the user asks; and On Hold is the user's, so agents don't move cards in or out of it unasked. Those are conventions, not permission checks (Hive doesn't read prompts to decide), apart from parking a card On Hold. **Another agent's card in Doing is its work in progress**: a project agent can't move a card that is in Doing with a different agent to Review, Passed or Done (`409`, saying newer work is in progress, the card unchanged), checked under the card's lock against the card as it is before the rest of the change, so giving it to itself in the same change doesn't get round it. This covers a reviewer whose review ended because the card came back for more work: its verdict is refused, and so is a plain move. Its own agent may, and so may the user, the Assistant and scripts with the workspace token; a card in Doing with no agent, and other changes to the card (comments, labels, moving it back to Todo), aren't restricted. Every move is in the card's history with who made it ("Claudette (hive): Moved to Done"), and any move is undone the same way, from the board or the tools, with no approval step. What stays the user's: creating a card straight in Done, putting Done in order, archiving, deleting and bringing archived cards back. Archived cards change only once the user brings them back. **Changing a card's project** (the user, the Assistant, scripts; never a project agent) always takes it from its agent, recorded in its history ("Moved to beta", "Taken from Agent 1 of alpha") so it can be put back; an agent named in the same change is refused (give it to one of the new project's agents afterwards; the card dialog does that as a second change). Every change to a card, including each card of a reorder, is checked against the card as it is when its change is made (under the card's lock): a card moved to another project while a project agent's change waited is unknown to it then, and is left as it is. **Decisions** (#357): a card's `decisions` (`{ id, text, decidedBy: "user", recordedBy, at, editedAt? }`, oldest first, in its file, absent when none) are what the user decided about it: the card is the record, not an agent's terminal. Anyone who can change the card records one (`hive_update_task` / `PATCH` `decision`, up to 4,000 characters and 100 a card), always as the user's decision with who wrote it down ("Recorded a decision: …" in the history, under the card's lock); only the user changes or removes one (`tasks:editDecision`, "Changed a decision to …" / "Removed a decision: …"). The card dialog pins them above the comments (newest last, who recorded each, **new since start** when recorded after the card last moved into Doing; add, change, remove; a decision being written or changed counts as unsaved when the card closes, and the card waits for one being saved), a tile counts them (its tooltip lists them) and an agent's card chip says how many. `hive_read_task` / `GET /v1/tasks/{n}` give them first, without ids. Agents pick them up at fixed checkpoints, never by text typed into their session while they work: work-on-card reads them when starting and again before moving the card to Review, and follows them over the description; review-agent-work checks the work against each (a mismatch is blocking); card-loop has a reviewer whose card is back in Review read it with `comments: 1` (decisions first) rather than only its latest comment, and anyone re-check when flagged; coordinate-agents has the Assistant record the user's decisions as decisions (moving old "Decisions (…)" comments of open cards only when asked). The nudge while it works is a flag: a project agent's reply to any Agent API call (the `X-Hive-Notice` header, which the hive tools add as a last line) says "#12 has 1 new decision since you last read it…" for a card it works on (Doing, its agent), reviews, or reviewed before and is back in Review, or that was so when the call began (unless the call read it: the move to Review still hears of an unread one), when a decision was recorded by someone else (one the agent recorded itself isn't news to it) or changed by the user since it last read the card in full, else since its work began or its last verdict on it (starting another review round doesn't count as reading it; `decisionNotices.ts`; reads kept in memory, verdicts from the card's history). **An agent waiting for the user** (#401) makes no tool calls, so the flag never comes: when such decisions are new to a running project agent whose turn has ended (ready or finished; never `waiting`, a question or permission prompt it shows, nor while it works or runs background tasks, which aren't ready), Hive gives it one line, a delivery of the user's answer (#649, with its id to ack): `[Hive] #392 has 2 new decisions from the user: read them (hive_read_task) and carry on from where you asked. (Its id: m7. …)` (`decisionLine`, every such card of its in one line). It is checked after a settle (15 s) from the last board change of the workspace, or from the agent's turn ending (decisions recorded while it worked), the settle never more than 60 s past the first change it waits on, so decisions recorded together give one line. It goes as a wake does: only while the user isn't typing in that terminal (**Pause after you type**) and no line of Hive's waits there (`sessions.lineWaiting`), and guarded again before each piece and before Enter, also against any change on the board and the agent reading a card since the line was worked out (then it is made again at the next try). One refused or stopped waits as its delivery and is tried again in a moment, on the agent's status changes and every 5 s, however long the user types or a line waits, until it is told, nothing is new to it any more (cancelled then), its session ends or its workspace closes; each try is made afresh from the decisions new to it then, so a card reassigned or a decision read meanwhile isn't told to the old agent; nothing counts as told until the agent has read its line with `hive_inbox` (received, #650); the doorbell that rings for it is checked as taken (#376, #430: `confirmTaken`). An agent watching cards hears of them from the flag in its wake's tool calls: its watch isn't `ready` (not typed into). Each decision is told once per run of Hive (`decisionWakes.ts`: per agent and card, the newest decision a line covered, in memory), counted from the same baseline as the flag, and the card's history says whom Hive told ("Told B6 (hive) of 2 new decisions", by Hive). The work-on-card skill: that line is the user's answer; read the decisions, then carry on from where it asked. Since a card counts as an agent's only once it is in Doing with it, work-on-card has an agent take the card (move it to Doing, which gives it to itself) before asking the user a question about it, also one it reads in Todo (#438): asked with the card still in Todo, the decision was nobody's and no one was told. **The latest comment**: `GET /v1/tasks/{n}/comments/latest` (`hive_read_task` with `latestComment`) gives only the newest comment, so an agent asked to check it doesn't read the whole card. **Summary lines** (#532: round reports and reviews ran to 3–6k characters, each read in full by the builder, the reviewer and the Assistant every round): work-on-card has a report to Review start with a one-sentence summary ("Round 2 ready: …") and review-agent-work a verdict with it in one line ("Round 1: FAILED: 2 findings: <titles>"), the evidence below; `?summary=true` on both reads (`hive_read_task` with `summary`) gives each comment as its first line that isn't blank (cut at 300 characters, `commentSummary` in `shared/tasks.ts`), with `length`, the whole comment's, when there is more. card-loop and run-lanes read wakes that way when the gist is enough; a wake line's comment is already its first line. **Stalled cards**: a Doing card that nobody is working on (no agent, its agent removed, or its agent not running; an agent that finished its turn is waiting for the user, not stalled) gets an amber edge and "Stalled: <why>", the board's sidebar lists them under **Stalled**, and the Agent API's card has `stalled` (the reason). **A card loop left with no watch** (#376) stalls too, in Doing or Review: "<agent> isn't watching #n: its turn ended with no card watch." (`notWatchingReason`, from the agent's `notWatching`; its tooltip says to show the agent and ask it to carry on). An agent is in a loop once it has started a card watch in this run of Hive (`loopCheck.ts`, `noteLoopWatch`; not the Assistant). When it is idle (ready or finished) with no watch for 45 s (`GRACE_MS`), and no background tasks that will wake it (#521, `tasksWillWake`: some, and its CLI's descriptor says they start a turn, `backgroundWakes`; so a Claude Code builder that ended its turn on an e2e run isn't flagged, and a Codex agent, which they don't wake, still is), its cards in play are marked on it (`notWatching`) and the user is told once per idle spell (a `waiting` notice, "<agent> isn't watching #n"). Its cards in play are the ones its latest watch named, in Doing or Review, and those in Review it is the agent or reviewer of; not its own Doing cards, since it may be asking the user. The mark clears as soon as it works or watches again, or has background tasks; a card that moves on (Passed, Done) drops out. A compaction the CLI does while the agent waits on background tasks leaves it **background** again, not ready (#521: that "ready" was what flagged HB3 on 10 Oct). **A card waiting on its reviewer** (#521) stalls too: in Review, not blocked, nobody reviewing it for 15 min (`REVIEW_WAIT_STALL_MS`, from its latest move or return into Review, with no review started or verdict given since: a failed round waiting in Review for its builder's fixes isn't) while an agent other than its own has a card watch on it: "<agent> is watching #n but hasn't started reviewing it: in Review for 40 min." (`reviewWaitReason`; its tooltip says its reviewer's watch didn't wake it). On the board, through the Agent API and the hive tools (`stalled`, so the Assistant sees it), and in the Progress panel's lane row (a **stalled** marker in the header, the card and reason in its tooltip, for any stalled card of a running lane); the window re-reads it each minute. Hive learns of a loop only from a watch, so an agent's first turn without one isn't caught; the card-loop skill has the agent check the watch's reply ("Waiting for #n…") before it ends its turn, start it again once, then tell the user. The Assistant's `coordinate-agents` skill tells it to report stalled cards when it looks at the board and suggest who could take each; its rules say it reassigns or restarts one only when the user agrees. **Removing an agent** (Remove Agent…, Discard) with open cards (not done or archived) asks: **Move them back** (nobody has them; Doing ones go to Todo) or **Leave them** (they show the agent as removed, Doing ones as stalled); Cancel keeps the agent. Removing an agent after a merge with clean-up leaves its cards as they are. **Archiving and deleting** (to the Recycle Bin; other cards' references to it are dropped) are the user's. **Archiving in bulk** (#351), the user's too (no tool or Agent API call does it; `PATCH /v1/tasks/{n}` with `archived` is refused, 403): each column's **⋯** (also right-click on its header) has **Archive All in <Column> (n)…**, and the toolbar's **⋯** **Archive All Cards (n)…**, off at 0. They take the cards the board shows: while a search, or the Board view's project filter, hides some of the column's (the board's) cards, the item and its dialog say **Archive the 5 Shown (of 23)…** and only those go (in a project's Tasks tab the project is the board, not a filter). The dialog says how many (Archive All Cards counts per column and has a danger button), and cards an agent is on are skipped and listed ("#305 Title · B4 is working on it · skipped"): a Doing card whose agent is running, a card with a reviewer, and a card an agent or the Assistant watches; **Also archive cards agents are working on** takes them too, warning that their agents lose the card. Main checks each card again as it archives it, under the card's lock (`tasks:archiveBatch` takes the column, project and search shown and whether busy cards are included; `cardBusy()` asks the sessions and watches as they are then): a card that has left the column, project or search, or that an agent took, began reviewing or watching while the dialog was open or the call waited, stays on the board, and the toast says which and why ("#12 moved to Doing"). Each is one **batch**: recorded in `.hive/tasks/archive-batches.json` (the newest 50: id, time, by, what, its cards and each column it left as it was) under that file's lock, so two windows' batches run one after the other; each card is archived with the batch's id (`archivedBatch`) and a history line ("Archived in a batch of 23 (All in Done)"), and the board is told once. A toast, **Archived 23 cards**, has **Undo** for 10 seconds; **Archived** has **Batch** (each kept batch that still has archived cards: when, what, how many), and **Unarchive this batch** brings it back too (`tasks:unarchiveBatch`): each of its cards still archived with it goes back to its column just after the nearest card that was above it then and is there now, else just before the nearest that was below it, else at the top (`restoreOrders()`), "Brought back with its batch (…)" in its history, and the batch is forgotten once every card came back: a card that can't be read or saved stays archived with it, the error names it, and the batch stays in **Batch** to try again. A card unarchived on its own leaves its batch. A card watch on an archived card wakes its agent with "#12 was archived (off the board: only the user brings it back)", and a bounded wait reports "was archived". A reference (blocked by, link) to a card that is gone, left by a card linked to one at the moment it was deleted, is left out whenever cards are read and leaves the file when that card is next saved; numbers are never reused, so it can't come to mean another card. Nothing moves by itself: an agent finishing doesn't move its card; it moves to Review when someone moves it, which the card's prompt asks the agent to do.
- **Order is priority.** A column's order (top first) is its priority; `hive_list_tasks` and `GET /v1/tasks` return cards in board order. Placing a card (creating one, a move, a batch, archiving or bringing one back, importing) works out its order from every card's place under the board's lock, without reading the board (#458): `boardPlaces` keeps each card file's column, order, archived and project with its stamp (time, size, file id), stats the files a few at a time and reads only those whose stamp changed, so a card changed by hand or by another program is placed by what its file says (a file that can't be read at that moment has no place then and is read again next time; what is kept goes when the workspace closes, keyed by its lifetime); the whole board is read 16 files at a time. The user drags cards within a column. While a card is dragged, holding it near the top or bottom of a column's cards (or over its heading) scrolls that column, and near the board's sides scrolls the board, faster nearer the edge (`edgeScroll.ts`, in pixels a second, every frame so it goes on with the pointer still); the drop marker is worked out again from what scrolling brings under the pointer. It stops on drop, drag end or cancel, and when the pointer leaves the board. Agents and the Assistant put cards in order too, on any project's cards: `hive_update_task` / `PATCH /v1/tasks/{n}` take `position` (`top` or `bottom`) or `before` (a card that has to be in the column the card ends up in; anything else is refused with 400, as is giving both), with or without a column change; `hive_reorder_tasks` / `POST /v1/tasks/reorder` `{ column, cards }` puts the listed cards at the top of the column in that order in one call (the rest keep their order below them), and refuses the whole list (changing nothing) if a card isn't already in that column or is listed twice: it never moves cards between columns. **The order of Done is the user's**: placing a card in Done or putting Done in order is refused (403) for agents and the Assistant, before the Assistant would ask about a move to Done. A placement by an agent or the Assistant that moves the card adds a line to its history ("Moved to the top of Todo", "Moved before #12 in Todo", "Moved to Review, before #12", "Placed 3rd in Todo" for a list; a card left where it was gets none); the user's drags are saved without one. For the Assistant (Control agents) each call is one change in *Done by the Assistant* ("Put #4, #2 at the top of Todo") and counts once towards its limit per message. The agents' session contract and the work-on-card skill say a column's order is its priority, and to put the cards in order on the board when asked to prioritise rather than only list an order.
- **Lean tool replies.** The hive tools answer in as few characters as do the job, since agents pay for every reply on each later turn: `hive_list_tasks` gives a line per card (number, title, project, agent and what it is doing, labels, blocked, depends on, stalled, reviewer, comment count; at most 200 lines a reply, saying how many more and the `offset` that carries on), `hive_read_task` the card without its history (`history: true` for it; `comments: n` for only its newest n comments, with `commentsOmitted`), and `hive_create_task`, `hive_update_task` and `hive_reorder_tasks` confirm what changed and where the card is now ("#12 Fix the redirect: Moved to Review; Commented. Now in Review (1st of 4), web."). `details: true` lists whole cards. Other tools follow the same rule: projects a line each, shared notes as a list of paths and read as text, agents and projects confirmed in a line. The Agent API keeps its full replies unless asked for the short ones (AGENT_API.md).
- **Move to Doing asks.** When the user moves a card into Doing from another column (dragging it, the card menu's **Move to → Doing**, or the card dialog's **Column** set to Doing and Save), the **Move to Doing** dialog asks: **Nobody yet** (column doing, agent cleared: also takes it from an agent it had), **Assign an agent** (a list of every agent of the card's project with its status, busy ones included; only the assignment changes, nothing is sent or started) or **Assign and start** (Start's choices, below: busy agents (working, starting, waiting for input, waiting on background tasks) can't be chosen, stopped ones can; Start checks again when confirmed, and an agent that gets busy while the dialog is open can no longer be chosen). The default is the card's agent, assigned (Nobody yet when it has none). **Cancel** changes nothing: a dropped card stays where it was, and the card dialog stays open with its edits unsaved. Confirming saves the card dialog's other edits first, then sets column and agent (the drop position is kept). Reordering within Doing, a new card, a card with no project in the workspace (it moves as it is), **Save and Start…**, agents' and the Assistant's tool calls don't show the dialog. The Assistant's `hive_update_task` description and its `coordinate-agents` skill say to ask the user which of the three they mean when asked only to move a card to Doing, and not when they've said ("have Claude start this" starts it; "assign it to Claude without starting" assigns only). Agents moving a card they were given into Doing take it as before.
- **Start** gives a card to an agent of its project with the card as its prompt, and moves it to Doing. A card is started once at a time (a second Start is refused while the first runs). The card is taken (given to the agent, moved to Doing) before the agent is started, checked again under its lock (still in that project, not archived, and in the column and with the agent the Start read: a card moved or reassigned since the Start began is refused and left as decided), so the prompt is the card as it then is; the agent is checked again just before the prompt goes in and while it is typed (before clearing the input, before each piece and before Enter: busy, or for the Assistant the user just typed there, and the rest isn't typed, what Hive typed is cleared unless the user typed since, and the card goes back), and an agent is given one card at a time (a Start of another card for it is refused while one is being given, and for 5 seconds after a prompt is typed into it, until its status says it's working); if the agent can't be started, an agent added for it is removed again and the card goes back where it was, unless it was changed meanwhile (moved to Done or Review, another project or agent), which stays. The user chooses each time: an **existing agent** (stopped: a new conversation on the card; idle: the card as its next message; busy ones can't be chosen), **a new agent** in the project folder, or **a new agent in its own worktree** (git projects; merged back as usual). **A card in Review or Done can be started again** for more work (the menu's and card dialog's Start… are on for it): it goes to Doing, the prompt says "It was in Review/Done and is back in Doing for more work", and the agent moves it to Review when done. The Assistant's `hive_start_task` takes a `note` (what to do now, up to 4000 characters), put in the prompt before the card; its instructions say to use it rather than `hive_prompt_agent` for more work on a card (such as review feedback), so the card is in Doing under its lock before the agent gets it. The prompt is the card's own words: its title, the note, its description and the cards it depends on, and for a card back from Review or Done its latest comment (the feedback it came back with, up to 4,000 characters). With Hive's tools it adds "Use the work-on-card skill." and how many comments the card has to read with `hive_read_task`: how to carry a card through (Doing first, progress worth a comment, blocked, Review with a summary, Done only when the user asks) is that skill's, and the boundaries are also in the agents' session contract (§7.3), so a session without the skill keeps them. Only the Assistant (Control agents and up) starts cards through the API (`hive_start_task`); project agents can't start other agents. An agent started from the board has no terminal on screen until it is shown, like one the Assistant adds; the notification has **Show**.
- **The card dialog keeps edits**: **Start…** on a card with unsaved changes reads **Save and Start…**, saves first and opens the start dialog only once the save succeeded (a failure shows in the dialog and starts nothing), so the agent gets the card as shown. Closing a card with unsaved field changes, a comment or decision draft, or a decision being changed (Escape, ×, outside, Cancel) asks **Keep Editing / Discard**; an unchanged card closes at once. **Save** keeps everything typed in the dialog (#432): after the fields, it records a decision being written (the user's, as **Add Decision** does) and saves a decision being changed, then posts a comment being written, each once: the drafts are read as they are when the save runs, and each is cleared as soon as it is recorded, so a failure later in the save, or Move to Doing's **Try Again** (which runs the same save again), never records one twice; a failure leaves the dialog open with what wasn't saved. The Move to Doing path's save does the same. A decision being changed to nothing is refused before anything is saved ("A decision can't be empty…", its editor kept open): removing one is its bin's, never Save's. **Add Decision** and **Comment** take the primary button style while their box has text, with a faint "Save adds it too" / "Save posts it too" beside them.
- **Dialogs move** (#87 for the card dialog, every dialog since #133). Each dialog is dragged by its header (`Modal`, movable unless `movable={false}`; pointer capture; its buttons and fields aren't handles) within the window: its header stays wholly in it, below the title bar (where the native window buttons would cover its ×), and is moved back in when the window shrinks or is restored. Escape while dragging puts it back; releasing or a cancelled pointer leaves it where it is, open. It opens centred each time (no remembered place), can be moved while busy, and its fields, menus and pickers work as before wherever it is. A question over a dialog moves on its own, and Escape goes to the top one only (it takes the key for itself, so the dialog under it neither closes nor asks again). Only the two **image viewers** (Images tab, Sessions tab) stay put: they are sized to their image and have their own controls.
- **Closing a dialog gives the keyboard back** to what had it (`Modal`). Closed by Enter or Space (Enter in a prompt's field), the rest of that key press (Enter's keypress, Space's keyup) is kept from it until that key is released (each key on its own), so the button that opened the dialog isn't pressed again (#355). Hive losing the focus ends it: a key released in another window never sends its keyup here, and nothing stays held back.
- **Where it shows.** The **Task Board** in the activity bar (Ctrl+Shift+J): the sidebar filters by project (All projects, each project, Workspace) with open-card counts and lists the cards waiting for review; the main area has search (#12, words, labels), **Archived**, **New Card** and the six columns. **Folding** (#170): each column collapses sideways to a narrow strip (**«** in its header, the horizontal convention of VS Code's panels, #276, so it doesn't look like a card's ▾ / ▸; the strip shows **»**, its name, rotated, and its card count, and clicking it, or Enter on it, expands it); the expanded columns share the board's width equally (flex, the change animated briefly unless the system asks for reduced motion), and the board scrolls sideways only when their least widths (160 px each, 34 px a strip) don't fit. A card dropped on a strip goes to the top of that column: its true top within the board's project scope, above any cards a search hides (#263). Each card folds to one line with its own arrow: its number, its title cut short, its agent's status dot and its blocked or stalled marker; folded, it still opens on a click and drags. A column's **⋯** (also right-click on its header) has **Collapse All Cards** and **Expand All Cards** for the cards it shows. Which columns are collapsed and which cards folded is a view preference kept per workspace in Hive's settings (`ui.boardFold`, by the workspace's path in lower case; never in the cards' files), shared by the Task Board and the projects' Tasks tabs and restored after a reload or restart. A window sends each fold as a change (`ui:changeBoardFold`, `applyBoardFold()`), which main applies to what is saved now for that window's workspace only, keeping the newest 50 workspaces and 5,000 folded cards each; so two windows never write over each other's folds with what they read earlier, and a window reads them again when it opens a workspace. A card keeps its fold when it moves column; a new card starts expanded; the folds of cards no longer on the board are dropped when it is saved. The Assistant's and agents' tools are unaffected. Cards drag between and within columns; click opens a card, right-click has Open, Start…, Move to, Archive and Delete. The activity bar's badge counts cards in Review. Each project has a **Tasks** tab with its own cards. **New Card…** (Ctrl+Alt+T) adds one for the selected project. The card dialog edits every field (description with Write/Preview), adds comments (Ctrl+Enter), shows the history, and has Delete, Archive, Start…. Save sends only the fields changed in the dialog, so an agent's change made while it was open (moving the card to Review, say) stays; if both changed the same field, the user's value wins and a notification says so. Deleting a card waits for a change already under way, and a change waiting for it then finds the card gone rather than writing it back. The board in a window is only ever the latest read of that window's workspace.
- **Guidance**: the Assistant's `coordinate-agents` skill describes the board as the shared list of work: plan multi-step work as cards with a full description, start them with `hive_start_task` (with a note for more work on a card), report stalled cards, ask which kind of move to Doing the user means, and start each new lane (an agent's next set of cards in a card loop) in a new conversation (`hive_prompt_agent` with `newConversation`, #437), keeping the conversation within a lane: every request re-reads the whole conversation, so short ones cost less. A decision recorded on a card in Review, or a block cleared from one, wakes neither side of its card loop (the builder's watch counts a verdict or Passed, the reviewer's a fresh move into Review; Hive's decision wake only reaches an agent idle waiting for the answer): `coordinate-agents` and `run-lanes` tell the Assistant to follow one on a card in a running loop with `hive_message_agent` to its builder (with `card`, so it is dropped once that round is over), and to its reviewer when it changes what they check (#587, Darren's decision of 13 Oct: the message route, no automatic decision wake); a card the loop hasn't reached needs none (a message tied to a card needs it in Doing or Review). Agents' `work-on-card` skill says to move a card they were given to Doing before they start (also one back from Review, Passed or Done with more work; one On Hold only when the user asks), keep it up to date, move it to Review with a summary when done (also one that was in Passed or Done), leave Passed to its reviewer and Done until the work is merged, and add cards for follow-up work rather than doing it unasked; `merge-ready` says to move a merged branch's cards from Passed to Done; their session contract repeats those boundaries in a line. `hive_update_task`'s description for the Assistant says moving a card to Doing starts nothing, and to ask which the user means. Tools: `hive_list_tasks`, `hive_read_task` (every agent and the Assistant), `hive_create_task`, `hive_update_task`, `hive_reorder_tasks` (every agent; the Assistant at Control agents), `hive_start_task` (the Assistant only).

### 6.7 Removing projects

**Project → Remove Project…** (also the project's right-click menu and the project header's ⋯) offers three ways, each described in the dialog with what it will touch: running agents (stopped first, conversations kept), the folder, the project's handovers (files named for it, §7.3), its board cards (and archived ones) and its worktrees with any work not merged. The CLIs' own transcripts (`~/.claude`, `~/.codex`) are never touched.

| | **Hide** | **Remove from Hive** | **Delete** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Folder | Stays in the workspace, left out by Hive | Stays on disk; can be moved, renamed or put in another workspace | Recycle Bin |
| Handovers | Left where they are | Copied into `<Project>/.hive/removed/handovers`, then to the Recycle Bin | Recycle Bin |
| Cards | Archived (`project-hidden`) | Archived (`project-removed`), and a copy in `.hive/removed/cards.json` | Deleted (Recycle Bin) |
| Worktrees | Left as they are | **Refused** while any has commits not merged into the project's branch or uncommitted files; otherwise removed with their branches (and their agents) | Recycle Bin (the repository goes with the folder) |
| Afterwards | Listed in **Settings → Workspace**, **Restore** | Listed there while the folder is in the workspace, **Restore** | Forgotten |

- Hidden and removed projects are kept in `workspace.json` (`hiddenProjects`), by folder name; Hive leaves them out of the project list, the Agent API and new cards, and turns them off. A project whose folder has left the workspace shows **Forget** instead of Restore.
- **Restore** brings the project back with the cards archived when it left (cards the user had archived stay archived). For a removed project, the packed handovers go back into the shared notes (never over an existing file) and its cards come back: the archived originals in the workspace it was removed from, else copies with new numbers (references between them follow; references to cards that didn't come along are dropped). The pack is then deleted.
- **A folder holding a pack** (`.hive/removed/manifest.json`) that appears in any workspace, or was never unpacked, shows a banner in its project view: **Restore** (as above) or **Discard** (deletes the pack).
- **Delete** asks for the project's name to be typed. The folder goes to the Recycle Bin first, with its file watchers paused: if Windows can't move it (a program has it open: a move refused for a moment, as by an antivirus scan, is tried again for about 1.5 s first, as every deletion through `trash()` is, #668), nothing else has been touched, the watchers resume and Hive says so. After that, a worktree or handover that can't follow is reported, not undone. Unsaved edits to its files in Hive are dropped. A deleted project restored from the Recycle Bin is a new project to Hive.
- **Safety.** Nothing is decided from what the dialog saw. One removal runs per project; its agents are stopped (starts in progress cancelled) and **no agent of the project can start** until it ends (the Assistant, a card, a hand-over are refused); then its worktrees, handovers and cards are looked at again, so a file an agent wrote while stopping still blocks Remove. A worktree **git can't check** (a failing `git status` or `rev-list`) counts as holding work. Hive only removes or deletes a worktree that git lists as one of the project's, that doesn't contain the project or the workspace and isn't another project: `project.json` can be edited by hand, so a path it gives is never deleted on its word alone (Delete leaves such a folder and says so; Remove refuses). The handovers moved or deleted are the ones whose header names the project exactly (so `foo_bar` never takes `foo-bar`'s, even after `foo-bar` has left the workspace); one without a header only when no other project could own it. Restoring never overwrites a different note that took a packed handover's name: the packed one comes back as `<name>-restored.md`, and the pack is deleted only once everything is back.

---

## 7. Skills and MCP

### 7.1 Skill levels

Skills have no on/off switches: every skill an agent can see is always available. Hive lists them by where they come from.

| Level (label) | Location | Loaded by | In Hive |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hive** | `<Workspace>/.hive/skills/`, copied into `<Project>/.hive/launch-<agent>/plugin/skills/` (Claude Code) or `<agent folder>/.agents/skills/hive-<name>` (Codex) | Its audience (`metadata.audience`): project agents (every agent, every provider; the default), the Assistant, or both | Skills view: add, add from file, edit, delete; bundled ones restore/revert |
| **Local (User Managed)** | `<Project>/.claude/skills/` (Claude Code), `<Project>/.agents/skills/` (Codex), project root only | That provider's agents in the project | Project Skills tab: add, add from file, edit, delete |
| **User** (`machine`) | `~/.claude/skills/`, `~/.codex/skills/` | That provider's agents everywhere | Project Skills tab: view only |
| **Plugin** | Installed Claude Code plugins | Claude Code agents | Project Skills tab: view only |

- **Deleting from lists** (skills, MCP servers, shared notes, personas, sessions): a bin on the item's row that shows on hover, and another in the top bar of the open item. Rename uses a tag icon everywhere (sessions, notes, files).
- **Skills view** (activity bar): the workspace's Hive skills only, under a warning, "Warning: Editing these skills may change agent behaviour in Hive" (`HiveSkillsWarning`), shown again above the editor while a Hive skill is edited there (not in preview, nor in a project's tab, where Hive skills are view only). **Project → Skills**: what the project's agents get: Hive skills first, in a group that starts open (its count beside it; a note under them counts those for the Assistant alone, which aren't listed, and links to the Skills view) (view, and **Edit in workspace**, which opens the Skills view with the skill in edit mode; if it no longer exists, the view says so), then one turned-on provider's skills, picked from a dropdown (each with its count), in a group that starts folded: Local, User, Plugins (#118; Hive open and the provider folded by Darren's decision of 5 Oct). The dropdown starts on the project's default provider; the last choice, and whether each group was left open, are remembered per project (`config.json` `ui.skillsProvider` and `ui.skillsFold`, by project path; the newest 200 projects). Each change is saved as that one project's (`ui:setProjectPref`, merged into the saved map in main, every window's copy following by `ui-pref-changed`), so windows showing different projects keep each other's choices (#245); `ui:set` doesn't write these maps. **Copy to workspace** on local, user and plugin skills; its tooltip and notification say who gets the copy, from its own audience.
- **Audience in the UI**: a Hive skill's page shows its audience as a badge (**Project agents**, **Assistant**, **Agents + Assistant**; a tooltip says what it means and how to change it), also for a deleted bundled skill. A row shows it only when it isn't the default. The Skills view's footer and empty page, the Hive tooltip and the delete confirmation say the same: deleting says who stops getting it, that a running Claude Code session keeps its own copy until it restarts, and that running Codex sessions share their folder's copy, which goes when another Codex session in that folder starts.
- **Adding** (both places): **+** creates `<name>/SKILL.md` from a starter; **Add from file** takes a `.md` (becomes `SKILL.md`, with the frontmatter `name` set and a frontmatter added if missing) or a `.zip` (unpacked as the skill folder: `SKILL.md` at its top or inside one top folder; at most 500 files and 50 MB; unsafe paths refused). Names: letters, numbers, `-`, `_`, max 64. A local skill can be added for both providers at once (tick box, shown when both are on). **Deleting** moves the folder to the Recycle Bin; only Hive and local skills can be deleted, never Hive's marked Codex copies.
- **Who gets a Hive skill** is its `SKILL.md`'s `metadata.audience` (the Agent Skills format's place for a tool's own keys, so the CLIs read the file as usual): `agents` (the default, also for a skill without it) for project agents, `assistant` for the Hive Assistant, `all` for both. It is not a switch: it says which kind of session a skill is written for, so a user's skills don't reach the Assistant and the Assistant's don't reach agents. Reading a skill grants no tools or permissions. The header is read as YAML (js-yaml, core schema; from the first 64 KB of the file), so comments, flow mappings (`metadata: { audience: assistant }`), quotes and block scalars mean what YAML says, and only `name`, `description` and `metadata.audience` of the right types are used. **A Hive skill whose header can't be read** (not closed, not valid YAML, including a key given twice, not a mapping) **or whose audience is anything else** (a misspelling, a list, or written but empty: `audience:`, `null`, `~`; only an audience left out is the default) **is given to nobody until it is fixed**, never to a guessed recipient: the Skills view marks it **Not given** with the reason, the API's listings and status carry `problem` (`audience: "none"` in the status), tool listings say "given to nobody: …", and each launch lists it in its problems.
- **Bundled skills** ship in the installation (`resources/skills`): for agents `work-on-card` (carry a card from Doing to Review; tests it writes wait for conditions, not for time, #524), `review-agent-work` (review another's work, with the board's review marks; a new or changed test that sleeps and then checks, reads state once after an action, depends on another test's leftovers or raises a timeout with no reason is a blocking finding; it checks the builder's run record instead of repeating it, running the quick checks, the tests the change affects and its own probes, and a whole suite again only when the record is missing, for other code or doubtful, or the change is to shared test code, saying why, #533), `merge-ready` and `use-hive-api` (scripts against the Agent API); for the Assistant `coordinate-agents` (plan as cards, brief and start agents, follow them, stalled cards) and `run-lanes` (a team's card loops: lanes by files, briefs, watches to the end); for both `handover`, `pick-up`, `split-work` (plans only) and `workspace-note`. **They're kept up to date in each workspace** (`bundled.ts`, each time it opens; the bundled personas the same way): a bundled skill the workspace was never given is added; an untouched copy of an older version (its content, line endings ignored, matches a version Hive shipped before this one; a link (symlink or junction) in it is never followed and counts as a change, since Hive ships none: `src/main/bundledHistory.json`, which `npm run bundled-history` appends to and a unit test keeps current) is replaced whole (a copy beside it renamed into place, so nothing reads it half-copied; a copy whose files are open stays until next time). **An automatic update or add never overwrites the user**: the swap (`swapIn`, also used for personas) is told what the copy was when the update was decided (its content hash, or nothing there for an add) and checks it again once the new version is copied (holding Hive's own editors' file locks), on the old copy once it has been moved aside, and once more before the old copy is removed (a write through a file left open); a copy edited, deleted or replaced meanwhile is put back as the user has it, the update is abandoned until the next scan (which sees its real state), and the manifest isn't changed. When both the old copy and the new one were edited, the old goes back in place and the new is kept beside it as a conflict copy; what a putting-back sets aside is removed only if it is still exactly the new version, and if a rename for it is refused, nothing is deleted (what can't go back is kept as a conflict copy). What a crash in a swap left behind (an old copy, a rollback's backup) is removed only when it is a version Hive shipped; anything else is kept beside the item as `<name>-conflict-<time>` (listed like any skill or persona), after the user's original is put back in an empty place. Another program writing through a file it held open in the instant between the last check and the removal is the one case this can't see; on Windows an open file usually stops the swap altogether. Restore and Revert, which the user asks for, replace whatever is there (sending it to the Recycle Bin first); an edited copy is kept, and offers **Revert to default** (with **Update available** only when it was made from a version this Hive knows to be older than its own: not from a newer Hive's, where Revert would go back); a deleted one stays deleted, listed greyed with **Restore**. `.hive/bundled.json` records which were given and the version each copy came from. A workspace from before it has none: a bundled skill missing there was deleted by the user (a new bundled skill is added), and a persona folder that doesn't exist means it never had them. A copy of a version this Hive doesn't know (a newer Hive's, sharing the workspace) is left alone. Revert and Restore send the workspace copy to the Recycle Bin.

For Codex, Hive skills are copied into `<agent folder>/.agents/skills/hive-<name>` on each launch (removing Hive copies of skills it no longer gets), since Codex reads skills only from there; the Assistant's go into the workspace folder, where it works. Those copies carry a `.hive-copy` marker and are never listed as local skills; a changed skill is swapped in whole, and what a crash during a swap left behind (Hive's dot-folders named for it) is tidied at the next launch, under the same lock. Each launch records what it delivered of each skill (`launched` in `GET /v1/projects/{name}`): the revision of the copy the CLI reads, and `problems` for a skill not delivered as asked (a folder of the user's has its name; its old copy was in use, so it kept that one; its folder in the workspace is gone or couldn't be read). A kept old copy leaves the agent needing a restart; a user's folder doesn't, since a restart can't change it. **A skill is copied within the hashing limits as it is read** (`copySkillTree`: entries listed a batch at a time, depth, bytes actually read; the entry limit counts the skill's own entries, with Hive's `.hive-copy` marker allowed on top, one a folder, so a skill at the limit is still within it once its copy is marked), since its source can change while it is copied, and the copy is hashed before it is published: what is recorded as delivered (and in a Codex copy's marker) is that copy's revision, not the source's from before. A source grown past the limits, or with a file gone or unreadable, mid-copy publishes nothing: Codex keeps its old copy (reported with its revision and why), a Claude Code launch leaves the skill out (revision null, and why); nothing is left half-copied. One skill's failure is its own: the launch delivers the others, and the failed one's record is Hive's copy from an earlier launch (its revision, the problem saying it kept it) or nothing, never a folder of the user's. A source that couldn't be read may work at the next launch; one over the hashing limits, or with a link that can't be made, is a lasting problem. If the skills folder itself can't be set up, each record is what is already there, the same way, and a failure to keep the copies out of git changes no record.

A link in a skill (symlink, or junction for a folder) is copied as a link to the same place, never as what it points to, and counts as content by where it points (`contentHash`, also for the markers and when telling the user's same-name folder from a copy of Hive's); a link Windows won't let Hive make (a link to a file needs a privilege) is a lasting delivery problem, naming the links: a Claude Code agent gets the rest of the skill without them; a Codex copy is swapped all or nothing, so it keeps its old copy whole (or, the first time, has none).

The workspace folder is a store only; no agent loads from it directly. Copies are used (not links): a Claude Code agent's are its own (`launch-<agent>`), so a running one keeps the version it started with. Codex agents sharing a folder share its copies, so when one launches with a changed skill, the others read the new version the next time they use it (Codex reads a skill's files when it uses them). Edits reach a session at its next launch either way.

### 7.2 MCP servers

One file per server: `<Workspace>/.hive/mcp/<name>.json`, in Claude Code's format plus an optional `description`:

```json
{ "command": "npx", "args": ["-y", "@modelcontextprotocol/server-github"],
  "env": { "GITHUB_TOKEN": "${GITHUB_TOKEN}" }, "description": "GitHub issues and PRs" }
```

Enabled servers are merged into `launch/mcp.json` for Claude Code, and passed to Codex as `-c mcp_servers.<name>={…}` (with `default_tools_approval_mode = "approve"`). The user's own servers and a project's own (`.mcp.json` for Claude Code, `.codex/config.toml` for Codex) are not loaded, by the same rule as §5. Secrets are passed as environment references only; a definition whose values can't be passed safely to Codex is skipped with a log warning. Server code in `<Workspace>/.hive/mcp/<name>/` is referenced in place, not duplicated. Hive warns when a definition looks like it contains a literal secret.

**Add MCP Server from File** (#550, aligned with Add Skill from File, Darren: "in 0.6.0 we should have the skills and MCP aligned"). The MCP view adds a server from a `.json` definition (it becomes `<name>.json`) or a `.zip` of a server folder (unpacked to `.hive/mcp/<name>/`, its definition from `hive-mcp.json`, else `hive-mcp.example.json` as the mcp repo ships it, at the zip's top or inside its one top folder, which is dropped; `__MACOSX` and `.DS_Store` are skipped; the definition file isn't copied into the folder). The definition is checked as launches need it (an object; a `command` or an http(s) `url`; `args` strings; `env` and `headers` objects of strings) before anything is written. The suggested name is the file's (a `.json`), the folder's, or the zip's own name (a flat zip), made a valid server name (never `hive`); `${HIVE_MCP_DIR}/<folder>` references move to the name it is added as. A name in use (its `.json`, or for a zip its folder) is refused unless the user chooses **Replace** (the old definition, and for a zip the old folder, go to the Recycle Bin; a `.json` replacing a server keeps a folder there, which its definition may run). It is turned on only where the add dialog's **Turn on for** says (#713: the selected project, all projects or none yet), even if the name was enabled before (a definition deleted by hand), and **what it runs** (`mcpRuns`: the command and arguments, or the URL and type, the folder, and environment variables and headers by name, a whole `${VAR}` reference shown as itself, never a literal value) is shown before it is added, in the server's view, and in a confirmation each time a server is turned on for all projects or in a project; the enable carries the server it showed (its definition, and its revision: the definition file's identity, modified time and size, which a replace changes even when the text is the same, as in an upgrade of only the code beside it), and is refused if either has changed since (replaced or edited meanwhile: "Check what it runs and enable it again"). Adding, replacing, deleting and enabling servers take turns, so an enable can't land in the middle of a replace. Safe unpacking: entries with `..`, an absolute or drive path, characters or names Windows can't hold (`:`, reserved names such as `con`), or a Unix symbolic link (read from the zip's central directory) refuse the whole zip; at most 5,000 files and 200 MB unpacked (a `.json` at most 1 MB); the folder is claimed (`mkdir`, never reused) and written in place, removed again if a write fails. Nothing runs during the add. Deleting a server also moves its `<name>/` folder to the Recycle Bin when its definition runs what is in it; a definition that can't be read (broken JSON, not an object) is deleted and turned off with no folder guessed. The workspace's watcher ignores everything in `.hive/mcp` but the definitions: a watched folder inside a server's folder would stop Windows moving that folder (Replace, Delete).

### 7.3 Built-in Hive MCP server

If "Provide Hive tools to sessions" is on (default), every session, of every provider, also gets a `hive` MCP server (bundled script run via Hive's own executable) exposing the Agent API as tools: list projects, project status, read/write shared notes (a read gives the note's revision, a short hash of its text; a write naming it as `expectedRevision` is refused, with the current revision, if the note changed since: checked and written under the note's lock, the one Hive's editors save under, so two agents rewriting one note can't drop each other's change; appends always keep every entry; #349), read the latest handover, create handovers, notify the user, session usage. Its MCP instructions (sent on `initialize`) are Hive's **session contract** (`hiveInstructions` in `shared/hiveGuidance.ts`), kept to what must hold whatever else is loaded: which project and workspace, that handovers, shared notes, other projects and the board are Hive's (use the tools, not the file system), the board's scope, which skill covers which workflow (`work-on-card` for a card given to it, `review-agent-work` to review one), and the board's boundaries in a line (Doing first, Review with a summary when done, reviewing isn't working on a card, Done only when the user asks, follow-up work as new cards, column order is priority), so a session without the skills keeps them, and that Hive sets its folder (#476, #482: no git worktree of its own to work in unless the user asks, else it asks for an agent with its own; a subagent's worktree merged or removed before it finishes: Hive's locks, Changes, merging and card tools follow the folder it gave, not one made there). Procedures live in the skills, arguments and side effects in the tools' descriptions, and enforcement in the Agent API. If the project has a handover, the instructions name the latest one. `GUIDANCE_REVISION` (a hash of this text, the control rules and a card's prompt) is in `GET /v1/status`, Help → Copy Diagnostics, and each running agent's `launched` in `GET /v1/projects/{name}` with its skills' revisions, so an older build's or launch's guidance can be told apart. Skill revisions are kept per open workspace (and once for Hive's shipped copies) and hashed again only when a skill's files change (their sizes, times and file ids, checked on each call without reading them), so status polls don't re-read every skill; callers asking at once share one scan, a closed workspace's revisions are dropped and hashing for it stops, and a skill over the hashing limits (64 MB read, 2000 entries, 12 folders deep) has no revision and says why. Too big by its files' sizes is found without reading them and kept like a revision while nothing changes, so polling an unchanged too-big skill reads no content (over the entry or depth limit, each check is a walk stopped at the limit); a file that grows while it is read is still stopped by the cap on bytes read. Listing skills reads only the first 64 KB of each `SKILL.md` (its header), again only once it changes, and at most 1000 entries of a skills folder. A launch checks each skill against the limits before copying it: one over them isn't delivered (Claude Code) or keeps the copy it had (Codex, which reports that copy's revision), with the reason in `launched.problems`. A handover belongs to a project when its file name is `<date>-<project slug>-<title slug>.md` (as `hive_create_handover` writes it). When project names overlap (`hive`, `hive-website`), a file that could belong to either is decided by its `- **Project:** <name>` header line. Codex doesn't show MCP server instructions to the model, so Hive passes the same text to Codex as `developer_instructions`.

---

## 8. Git

- Project: `.hive/` is kept out of version control (#345, `hiveVcs.ts`): Hive adds `/<path>/.hive/` to the `.git/info/exclude` of the nearest git repository holding the project (its own: `/.hive/`; or one in a folder above, such as a workspace kept in git; a worktree's common git dir), the path's pattern characters escaped (`/project\[1\]/.hive/`), idempotent, never a tracked `.gitignore`. Whether it is excluded is **git's own answer** (`git check-ignore -q --no-index -- .hive/` in the project, kept until the exclude file changes or for a minute), not the line Hive wrote: a `.gitignore` rule (`!/.hive/`) can bring it back, and then it is `not-excluded`; only when git can't be asked (not installed), the line. It is ensured when the project is set up, at every refresh (`projectInfo`) and before every launch, so a repository made later (`git init`) is covered. `ProjectInfo.hiveVcs` says how it stands: `excluded`; `not-excluded` (a git repository whose exclude file Hive couldn't write); `other-vcs` (the nearest is Mercurial, Subversion, Jujutsu, Bazaar or Fossil, which Hive doesn't configure); `none`; and `sync` when the folder is in OneDrive (Windows' `OneDrive*` variables) or Dropbox (its `info.json`), which copies `.hive` whatever version control does. Unless excluded and unsynced, the project's Overview and Project Settings show a notice saying why it matters, with **Exclude** for `not-excluded` (`project:excludeHive`) and Learn more; the Overview's can be dismissed for that situation (`ui.hiveVcsNotice`, a project preference), and shows again when it changes.
