- **Committed before it was excluded** (#364): an exclude rule keeps only untracked files out, so files under the project's `.hive` committed earlier (a `git add -A` before Hive set it up) stay tracked. `trackedHiveFiles()` asks `git ls-files -z -- .hive` in the project (its own `.hive` only, also in a repository above it), again only when the repository's index changes (its index file's time and size: a commit, a `git rm --cached`), not at every refresh; only git's answer is kept, so a failed read says nothing (no `tracked`) and is asked again at the next refresh, never remembered as none. `HiveVcs.tracked` is how many. The notice (Overview and Project Settings, its own dismissal key) says how many files are committed, why excluding doesn't untrack them, and the command (`git rm -r --cached .hive`, then commit; the files stay on disk), and offers **Untrack…**: a confirmation listing the files git tracks there now (`project:hiveTracked`, read afresh; at most 50 shown), then `project:untrackHive` with exactly those files: main asks git again and, unless it tracks exactly those, untracks nothing (a file staged or untracked meanwhile: "changed since you were shown them"); otherwise `git --literal-pathspecs rm --cached --quiet -- <each file>` (never the folder, so nothing added in the last moment is touched), which stages their removal and leaves the commit to the user. Hive never changes the user's index unasked.
- Workspace: `.hive/` is **not** excluded; it is meant to be committed.
- The Git tab's Changes section (the Changes tab before #607) shows `git status` and a Monaco diff (HEAD vs working tree) per file.
- **The Git view** (#607; `src/main/gitRepo.ts`, `src/shared/gitRepo.ts`, `src/renderer/src/views/GitTab.tsx`). Git only: no layer for other version control (a project without git keeps working in its folder, with no worktrees, merges or Git view). The project's Changes tab became **Git** (its id stays `changes`, so saved tabs, `project.tab.changes` and Alt+6 are unchanged), with three sections (`GIT_SECTIONS`, `gitSection` per project in the window, Changes when none): **Changes** (as before: whose changes, from the picker), **History** (the project folder's last 20 commits, `git log`, with their decorations, and its tags newest first, at most 50 listed, with how many more; **Create tag…**: lightweight, or annotated with a message, optionally pushed to a chosen remote) and **Branches & remotes** (`git:info`: the branch or detached HEAD, a merge, rebase, cherry-pick, revert or bisect in progress (from the git dir's files), the upstream with ahead/behind (`%(upstream:track)`), when the folder last fetched (FETCH_HEAD's time), local branches (each one's upstream, ahead/behind, last commit and the worktree it is checked out in), remote branches with no local one, the remotes with their URLs (a password or token never shown or logged, `redactRemoteUrl`, erring towards hiding: the sign-in is everything before the last @ ahead of the query, all of it for http(s) and ftp(s), the password for other schemes, and the whole query and fragment, as •••; git's own failure messages pass through `redactUrlsIn`, each URL taken to the next whitespace, since a password or query can hold an apostrophe or quote; an action's failure is also cleaned of the exact secret parts of every configured remote URL and of a URL being added, as written and decoded (`urlSecrets`, `scrubSecrets`); an edit that keeps a ••• is refused), the repository's other worktrees, read-only, each with its agent and **Changes**, and the commit identity: this repository's (`--local`), the global one and the one git uses, with warnings (`identityWarnings`): no name or email (commits fail), and a personal address on a repository with a GitHub remote (GitHub's no-reply address keeps it private). A **status bar item** for the selected project (`GitStatusBarItem`, `gitStatusText`: "main · origin · ↑2 ↓0", "main · no upstream", "detached at abc1234 · rebasing"; none for a folder that isn't a repository) opens Branches & remotes; its tooltip says when the counts are from. Reads are cheap (no working-tree scan) and happen when the project's branch or agents change, after a Git action, on window focus and every minute. **No automatic fetch**: ahead/behind is as of the user's last **Fetch** (`git fetch --all`), with its time. **The user's actions only**, never an agent tool or Agent API route, and only in the project folder (`assertProject`: an agent's worktree is refused; its work comes in through Merge): switch branch (`git checkout <branch> --`, or `--track <remote>/<branch>` for a remote branch with no local one; not to a branch checked out in another worktree), create a branch (switching to it, or not), add a remote, change a remote's URL (an unchanged redacted URL saves nothing), fetch, pull, push, create a tag, set this repository's identity (`git config --local`; empty unsets it). **Pull** fetches the upstream's remote, then `git merge --ff-only @{upstream}`; a branch that has diverged is refused with the counts and left as it is. **Push** pushes to the upstream (`refs/heads/<branch>:<its merge ref>`), or with `--set-upstream` to a chosen remote when there is none; never forced (a rejection says to pull first). Fetch, pull and push run with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` (a credential helper's own window still opens) and a 5-minute limit. **Safety**: switching branch, creating one to switch to and pulling are refused (`folderBusy`), in the window (the buttons disabled with the reason as their tooltip) and again in main, while an agent working in the project folder (no worktree) is starting, working, waiting for an answer, waiting on background tasks or cards, or stopped mid-task at a sign-in, or while the project's merge slot is held (an agent's, or the user's own merge) or waited for, saying who and why; the change itself runs holding the merge slot of the branch it leaves (`mergeSlots.asUser`) and the project's merge lock, so a merge can't start in its middle; the agents (and, with a detached HEAD, the slots) are checked again once those are held, right before git runs, so an agent that started working while the action waited stops it. A turn that starts during git's own checkout isn't fenced (#646). An idle agent's session in the folder doesn't block: the confirm says it finds the new branch on its next turn. One Git action at a time per project folder. After a switch or pull, the workspace refreshes (the project's branch) and worktree agents' unmerged counts are checked again. A project folder inside another repository (a workspace kept in git: `rev-parse --show-toplevel` is a folder above it, `parentRepo`) shows that repository read-only, with a note: every action is refused (`parentRepoText`), as Hive changes only a project's own repository. A project that isn't a repository shows "<name> isn't a git repository" with the setup notes and **Initialise repository…** in every section; git that can't run shows the problem with Agent Setup and Retry.
- **Initialise repository** (#701; `src/main/gitInit.ts`, `src/shared/gitInit.ts`, `src/renderer/src/components/InitRepo.tsx`). The user's, from the window only (no agent tool or Agent API route), for a project folder (`assertProject`). Its button is on the Git tab of a project that isn't a repository (and the Changes section's hint), and, as **Initialise its own repository…**, on Branches & remotes of a project inside another repository. `git:initCheck` says whether it can be done (refused, with why: git can't run, another version control system keeps the folder or one above it (`otherVcsOf`: its markers looked for at every level whatever git is there, so a colocated Jujutsu repository, .jj beside .git, is refused, never offered as a git one to nest in), or the folder is a repository's top already), the repository around it (`parentRepo`), the identity from the system and global config with their includes (`--includes`), read with `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` above the folder so an outer repository's own config, or a conditional include that matches only it, never counts, and whether they have conditional includes (`includeIf`, `conditionalIdentity`: only the new repository can resolve those, so the fields are optional), whether the folder has a .gitignore, and whether it is empty but for .hive. The dialog offers a starter .gitignore (`GITIGNORE_CHOICES`: none, minimal (OS and editor files, logs), Node, Python, .NET, Godot (4 and 3), each with the minimal lines; never listing .hive), none when the folder has one (kept, never overwritten: written with `wx`); asks for the name and email git lacks (`initProblem`), used only for what git still lacks once the repository exists (read there, conditional includes resolved) and saved with `git config --local`, never global (nothing entered and still none: it stops right after `git init` and the exclude, before the .gitignore or anything is staged, saying so); **a repository with no commit yet** (`resume`: one that stopped there, or made by hand; `HEAD` unborn) is taken on from there: everything but `git init`, its identity read from the repository itself; the dialog belongs to the Git tab (`gitInitFor`, `InitRepoHost`), so it stays open as the page under it changes, asks again after a failure, and goes on as **Make the first commit** with the fields the repository lacks; Branches & remotes of such a repository says it has no commits, with **Make first commit…**; and, inside another repository, a checkbox naming it, without which Initialise stays off. `git:init` checks it all again in main, then in order: `git init -b main`; `ensureHiveExcluded` (#345) and Hive's skill copies' exclude lines, refusing to go on, with nothing staged, unless git confirms it ignores .hive (a .gitignore rule bringing it back); the .gitignore; the identity; `git add -A`, then a check that nothing under .hive is staged (unstaging all and stopping if so); `git commit --allow-empty -m "Initial commit"` (hooks run); and a workspace refresh, so `isGitRepo`, the Git tab and worktrees work at once. One at a time per project (the Git view's queue). No undo: its reply says what it did ("Initialised a git repository on main, added a Node .gitignore and committed 42 files."), and a failure says which steps were done and where it stopped.
- **Hive's git commands never write the index as a side effect** (#212): they run beside the user's and agents' own git commands, and `git status` (and `git diff` against the working tree) otherwise refresh the index, taking `index.lock` for a moment, so a `git add` or `commit` at that moment failed with "index.lock: File exists". Every git command Hive runs (`git()`, `GIT_PREFIX` in `src/main/git.ts`) passes `--no-optional-locks` and `-c diff.autoRefreshIndex=false`; locks a command needs (commit, merge, reset, worktree add) are still taken. Status compares content in memory, so a file touched but unchanged still isn't listed; a worktree branch's changes since it left base (`diff --name-status <merge-base>`, which without a refresh lists stat-only files as modified) drop such a file only when git says exactly that it isn't changed: its working copy matches the index (status) and the index matches the merge base (`diff --cached`, mode included). A working copy that differs from an index that differs from base is kept (shown even if it was changed back by hand), never hidden.
- **Git itself** (#346, `gitTool.ts` in main and shared): whether git runs is kept apart from whether a folder is a repository. `git --version` on Hive's own PATH (a Start-menu launch can have another PATH than a terminal), with a 10 s timeout, at start and with Agent Setup's **Check again**, cached: `ok`, `missing`, `old` (older than **2.38**, `GIT_MIN`: `merge-tree --write-tree`, which checks a merge for conflicts first, recognises squash-merged branches and is what the merge-ready skill runs) or `unknown`, with the version and the executable (git.com or git.exe on PATH, as Windows starts it). Below **2.17** (`GIT_REQUIRED`: `--no-optional-locks` needs 2.15, `git worktree remove` 2.17) nothing of Hive's git runs, which counts as missing. `git()` reports a call that couldn't start git (ENOENT and the like, when its working folder exists: Node says ENOENT for a missing folder too) as `missing` with exit code -1, never 1, so no caller takes it for git's "no" (`check-ignore`'s "not ignored"); such a call marks git missing at once, and a call that runs while it is marked missing clears it and checks again (`git-tool` event, every window's store). **Agent Setup** shows a Git row under every provider's tab: the version and path; or **Git not found** / **Git x is older than 2.38**, what doesn't work and the fix (install or update Git for Windows, or add git.exe's folder to PATH, then Check again: no restart, as Check again reads the fresh PATH, #472), with **Git for Windows** (git-scm.com). No setting for git's path. The status bar shows **Git** (warning) when git is missing or unusable, **Git x** (caution) when old; either opens Agent Setup. Copy Diagnostics lists it. **Every git-dependent screen says "git missing" instead of "not a repository"** (`gitProblem`): Add Agent's New worktree ("Git isn't installed (or isn't on Hive's PATH)", `ProjectGitInfo.gitProblem`), the Board's Start in a worktree, adding a worktree agent (Agent API and cards too), the Changes tab (`GitStatus.gitProblem`: the problem, the fix, Agent Setup and Retry), the Merge dialog (the error in place of the merge, which can't be started), Remove All and template loads (the reason a worktree is kept, or the load refused), Remove Project. **Fail safe**: a git call that can't run, or fails, never counts as merged, clean or nothing to lose. `branchStatus` throws whenever git can't read the project folder's branch (`checkedOutBranch`: a failed read is not a detached HEAD, which counts against the base), count the branch or read the worktree's status (no more "nothing to merge" on a failure: the unmerged badges show nothing, the Merge dialog the error; merging refuses too), `worktreeCheck` is never removable then, and `removeCheckedWorktree` deletes nothing git can't run for. The Changes tab's status is "not a git repository" only when git says so: any other failed `git status` (a damaged index, a dubious owner), merge base or diff against an agent's base is an error the tab shows with Retry, never an empty list. A read that fails only because another git command is rewriting the index (`INDEX_BUSY`, "index file open failed" on Windows while a user's `git add` runs) is tried again briefly first (`gitReading`: 4 tries, 50 ms apart; the Changes tab's status and diff, worktrees' uncommitted counts, the Files tab's ignored entries). `.hive`'s exclude line is still written without git (file work), reported `unconfirmed` and logged, and stays excluded. Bundled skills keep to git that Agent Setup's row covers: merge-ready's `merge-tree --write-tree` needs 2.38, which the row names.
- **Ignored entries in the Files tab** (#222): `git check-ignore` exits 0 or 1; any other exit (128, "index file open failed" while another git command rewrites the index on Windows) is a failure, not "nothing ignored". A busy index is tried again (4 tries, 50 ms apart); outside a repository nothing is ignored. If git still can't say, the folder keeps its last known ignored entries (a memory of the 500 folders listed last) and is listed again 500 ms later (`files-changed`; at most 3 times in a row while it keeps failing). The failure is logged once per project.

---

## 9. Usage, compaction and memory

Each provider's adapter reads its own transcripts into one usage shape. Codex: rollout files under `$CODEX_HOME/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-…-<id>.jsonl` (`token_count` events for tokens, context window and plan limits; `session_index.jsonl` for titles). Claude Code: `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-path>/<session-id>.jsonl`, where the encoded path replaces every non-alphanumeric character of the project path with `-` (matched case-insensitively). A folder's sessions are found by place, whatever the folder is called (#493; a tool's path in a transcript shows relative to the project when it is inside it under another name, `shortPath`, #494): Claude Code names its transcripts' folder by Node's realpath of its cwd (a junction or symlink followed, an 8.3 short name kept), so Hive looks under that name, its own spelling and the real, long path, and last under folders named by an 8.3 spelling of the folder, which none of those gives: only names that may hold one (on its drive, with an encoded `~n`), each read once for the cwd its transcripts record (in their first 64 KB) while it is unchanged, and compared by place. It lists the sessions of all of them, restores a transcript under the first (where Claude Code looks on resume), and Repair copies a moved folder's data from each of them in that order (`copyPathData`, a copy per folder: the first one's file wins, a later one's that differs is kept and listed, in the preview too); Codex (`session_meta.cwd`) and Copilot (`workspace.yaml`'s `cwd`) record the cwd as given, and Hive compares it by real path (`placeMatcher`). Claude Code's transcripts don't carry the context window; for a running session it comes from the status line (`context_window.context_window_size`) and is added to the session's usage.

**Model choice** (global default, per project, per agent and for the Assistant): the models Claude Code reports (§10, models and capabilities), else the fallback list in Settings → Claude Code (as shipped: Latest aliases, pinned versions and older versions, hidden until requested); and a custom model ID. There is no 1M-context choice: current models (Fable, Opus 4.7+, Sonnet 5+) have the 1M window without asking, and Opus 4.6 and Sonnet 4.6 reach it only through a `[1m]` model ID, typed as a custom model (the placeholder says so). Saved `[1m]` choices from 0.2 still work and show as custom models. Availability for the account is only known when a session starts.

**Use 200K context (instead of 1M)** (providers with `capabilities.contextLimit`: Claude Code): global (Settings → Claude Code, off by default), per project (Inherit / On / Off), per agent (Add Agent and Agent Settings: the project's / On / Off, stored as `use200kContext` on the agent and cleared when its provider changes) and for the Assistant (Settings → Assistant and Assistant Settings). Resolved like the model: the agent's, else the project's, else the global setting. A session launched with it gets `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_1M_CONTEXT=1`, and a `[1m]` suffix is dropped from its model, since Claude Code rejects such a model as unrecognised when 1M is off (2.1.287). A change applies on the next start or restart (it's part of the launch signature, so running agents show Restart session).

**Metrics per session**
- Input, output, cache-write, cache-read tokens (assistant `message.usage`, de-duplicated by request id).
- Current context size: the last assistant entry's `input + cache_read + cache_creation`.
- Compactions from `system/compact_boundary` entries: trigger, `preTokens`, `postTokens`.
- Cache TTL detected from `cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens` vs `ephemeral_5m_input_tokens` (overridable in settings).
- Model, Claude Code version, session title (`ai-title` / `custom-title`), last activity.
- API-equivalent cost from `cost-state` entries (`totalCostUSD`), shown on the Overview as "API-equivalent cost". Claude Code writes them only at some turns' ends (hours can pass without one), so the requests after the last one get Hive's estimate on top, and the cost shows as partly estimated ("≈"). A provider that reports its cost (`capabilities.reportsCost`: Claude Code, Copilot) has **Settings → <provider> → Session costs** (#436, Darren's decisions): its reported cost, the default and the truth (it follows the CLI's own price table, even when that lags a price change, #442: Hive never recomputes it), or **My prices**, Hive's estimate from the table for every session it has a price for (the footer's live cost too, re-priced at once when the choice or a price changes, idle sessions included; the CLI's last report is kept, so going back shows it, as reported; days estimated, not shared out from the reports), the CLI's newest report (its status line's, else its transcript's, never recomputed) for a model without one, and with neither a price nor a report the cost is unknown (`costSource`, `usesOwnPrices`). No price page is fetched or scraped. For providers that don't report cost (Codex), Hive **estimates** it from a price table (per million tokens: input, cached input, cache write, output), shown with "≈". Hive ships the published API prices as editable defaults (checked 30 Sep 2026, GPT-6.1 Sol 5 Oct 2026, Haiku 5.5 8 Oct 2026; Claude's cache writes are 1.25× input and cache reads 0.1× unless listed; never fetched or scraped). Haiku 5.5's row is the prompts-up-to-100,000-tokens rate with five-minute cache writes: longer prompts (5× the price) and one-hour cache writes are not modelled, so its estimate is low for those sessions, and a cost Claude Code reports is kept and each provider's settings page has the table, labelled with the date they were checked: the user can change any price, add models, remove them (a removed shipped price is no longer used) and **Reset to defaults**. Only the user's changes are stored (`prices`, `pricesRemoved`), so Hive's later prices still reach models they never edited. A session whose model has no price has an **unknown** cost, never $0: the agent's footer shows **$?**, the Overview's running agents **Unknown**, and its cost totals (the summary, each provider's, the Workspace Overview's and the Assistant's, by agent and by day) and session details **Unknown** when no session in them has a price, or the known subtotal with **+ ?** ("≈ $1.20 + ?") when some haven't; the agent's footer and the running agents say (on hover) that Hive has no price for the model and to add one in Settings → <provider> → API prices. It is the API-equivalent value, not what a subscription charges.

**Keeping up with the CLIs**: a transcript of over 200 KB in which Hive finds no requests at all is taken as a format change it doesn't understand, and the user is told once per provider version. Sample transcripts per supported CLI version are unit-test fixtures (`tests/fixtures`).

**Overview tab**: first the project's cards at a glance (`TaskStrip` in `Board.tsx`, shared with the Workspace Overview, counts from `taskOverview()` in `shared/tasks.ts` for the project's cards only): On Hold, Todo, Doing, Waiting for review, Passed, Done, Stalled and Blocked (blocked not counting Done), from the store's board, so they follow every card change without a reload; each number opens the project's Tasks tab, and a project without cards shows "No cards for this project" with **Open Tasks** (the Workspace Overview's strip hides when the board is empty, and its numbers open the whole board). Then a period (Today, 7 days, 30 days, All time; All time by default) and a project summary across every provider (tokens, API-equivalent cost, sessions, prompts). Periods are **local calendar days** (Today since midnight; 7 and 30 days include today) and count only what happened in them: each session's usage is kept **by day** (`SessionUsage.days`, `shared/usageDays.ts`): requests, tokens, prompts and compactions on the day they happened; Codex's running totals by what they grew by each day. A day's cost is its share of the reported cost (each Claude Code cost report's increase is split between the days of the requests it covered, in proportion to Hive's estimate for them, so the days add up to the reported total) plus Hive's estimate for requests after the last report; for providers that don't report cost, Hive's estimate. A session counts in a period's sessions when it had activity in it. For 7 and 30 days a chart shows tokens per day (cost and prompts on hover). The day charts (both Overviews' and Performance's) share their look: each day or hour is a tile with a soft background (`.daily-col`, `--daily-slot`), 3 px apart, so a day without tokens still reads as its own column; Performance adds its hatching (not recorded) and fade (partly recorded) on top. Usage is read incrementally: each transcript keeps its parser and offset, and only lines added since are read (a long session's transcript can be over 100 MB). Then the agents running now (context against the model's window, cost), then one section per provider used (its totals and its plan limits: Claude Code's 5-hour and weekly, Codex's primary and secondary windows), a table by agent, and one agent's session details (its running session, else its most recent; the focused agent's until another is picked in the agent picker shown with more than one agent; compaction history as a data table, below). Its **compaction history** is a `DataTable` (`components/DataTable.tsx`, rules in `shared/tableView.ts`; #124): When (newest first by default; a text filter on the formatted date), Trigger (a choice), Before, After, Freed (sortable, biggest first); ten rows a page. A row (click, or Enter when focused) opens the Sessions tab on that session at that compaction's divider, its summary open (`openInSessionsTab(path, id, n)` → `transcript:compactions` gives the nth compaction's item id), when its transcript or Hive's backup is there; otherwise rows aren't clickable. The footer's context (`showInOverview`) scrolls to it (`#compaction-history`), else to the session details.
- **Search and filter boxes** (#433): every one is `SearchInput` (`components/ui.tsx`): the board's search, Settings' and Project Settings' search, the Files tab's Find, the Sessions tab's search, the sidebar's Filter projects and Filter skills, a data table's text filters, Tips, Keyboard Shortcuts and the command palette (`tests/searchBoxes.test.ts` fails for a plain `<input>` named for searching, filtering or finding). While it has text, a × (`IconButton` "Clear") sits inside it on the right (`.search-box`, which takes the box's place in the layout; the box keeps its size): clicking it clears the text as typing would and keeps the focus in the box. **Escape** with text clears it too; with the box empty, Escape does what it did before. In a dialog (`Modal` leaves an Escape to a clearable box with text, `escapeClearsSearch`) and in the palette, the first Escape clears the box and the next closes it. The Files and Sessions searches already cleared on Escape; the palette used to close at once.
- **Data tables** (`DataTable`): one component for tables that grow, with the look of Hive's other tables (`.table` in `.table-wrap`, Performance's too): a header click sorts (ascending, or descending first for dates and numbers; then the other way; then the default order; `aria-sort`, a chevron, the header a button), quick filters in a row under the headers (text contains, any case; a choice of the rows' values), and paging under it (rows per page 10/20/50/100, remembered per table in `ui.panes` as `table-rows:<id>`; "21–40 of 133"; first, previous, "Page n of m", next, last), hidden when one page of the fewest rows holds them all (so a bigger size can always be made smaller again). Rows can be selectable (`selection`, #249): a checkbox before each row, and one in the header for every row the filters let through, on every page. A choice filter can match rows with several values (`values`: a card's labels). Filters and sorting apply before paging; a filter or rows per page change goes back to page 1, and a page past the end shows the last. Only the page is rendered. No rows: a message instead of the table; no matches: a row saying so, with Clear filters. It scrolls sideways inside its pane at narrow widths. A clickable row (`onRowClick`) is a button: Enter or Space on the row opens it, but not on a control inside it, which does its own action (an archived card's **Unarchive**, #267). **Its sort is remembered** per table too (#250), in `ui.panes` as `table-sort:<id>:<column>` (1 ascending, 2 descending; one a table; the default order keeps none, and a column that no longer sorts is ignored; `rememberedSort()`/`sortPanes()` in `shared/tableView.ts`). A table can also have totals under its rows (`foot`, a tfoot: Storage's), a row's detail opened under it (`detail`, with `aria-expanded` on a clickable row: Compare's scenarios), a row's own class (`rowClassName`), a header's (i) (`headerTip`), a column's class (`className`), and filters shown only past a number of rows (`filterFrom`: small tables show none, or while one is set). **Where** (#250): the compaction history and archived cards; Settings and Project Settings → Keyboard Shortcuts (every command on one page, its edit controls kept); Performance's Hive tools (by characters), Agent API (by requests), Guidance at launch and Provider usage (unknown totals sort last; a latency past the histogram's last bound, shown "> 10 s", sorts as the slowest, `latencyOrder()`, #305); Compare's scenarios (worst first; a row opens its measures), an export comparison's measures (sortable by change) and the kept list; Storage (a project's, with its total under it; Clean Up…'s preview, a table per kind; Settings → Workspace's projects); the Workspace Overview's table by project (by tokens; a row opens the project's Overview); a project Overview's table by agent. **Left as they are**: About, the shortcut cheat sheet overlay, the Files tab's file details and a session's details (label and value lists), the editable price and model-fallback tables, hidden projects, a template's agents (a form), Markdown and CSV previews, and Compare's per-scenario measures inside an opened row, and the Assistant panel's **Done by the Assistant** (#301): its last 3 changes at a fixed height under its status line (#312, #399), in a side panel, where a table's headers, filter row and paging would take more room than its rows; and it lists only this run's changes (up to 200, in memory), so there is little to sort or page. It (and the session lists) update per **Settings → Sessions → Overview updates**: **Live** (default: as sessions change, at most every 15 seconds, only while shown), **Every minute**, or **Only on Refresh**; the Overview has a Refresh button. A session list reads each project and session file once.

**Workspace Overview** (activity bar, Ctrl+Shift+O): the same summary for the window's workspace. A period (Today, 7 days, 30 days, All time; **7 days** by default, kept while Hive runs), then a strip with the board's cards per column, stalled and blocked (each opens the board); the summary (tokens, API-equivalent cost, sessions, prompts) across every project **and the Assistant**; for 7 and 30 days a chart of tokens per day **stacked by project** (the six that used the most in the period, in name order so each keeps its colour while the set stays the same, the rest as "Other (n)"; a legend, and each day's numbers by project on hover; colours from a validated categorical order stepped for each theme); every running agent (the project's name and the agent's, click opens it; the Assistant's opens its panel); a sortable table **by project** (sessions, tokens, cost, share of the tokens, last active; the Assistant is its own row; click opens that project's Overview, or the Assistant view); and a section per provider with its totals and plan limits. Hidden and removed projects aren't counted (a note says how many). The sidebar lists each project with its cost for the period. It uses the project Overview's counting (`shared/usageTotals.ts`) and its update setting, and only while shown; main sends only what totals need (`workspace:usage`: each project's and the Assistant's Hive sessions, with deleted sessions' kept usage, no sessions started outside Hive and no transcript or backup checks), with usage from the usage cache (a transcript is read only where it grew). In a narrow window its period buttons go under the title and wrap, the summary cards stack, and only the table by project scrolls sideways (#252). Down to a few dozen pixels of page (the sidebar and the Assistant open in a small or zoomed window), both Overviews keep everything with less padding: words break rather than push the page sideways, and the board strip, the period buttons and the cards take a row each (#269). The project Overview's session details (model, CLI version, started, last activity) keep their labels whole and their values a readable width, and scroll in their own box (#307). Budgets, alerts, export and comparing periods are not in it.

**Re-cache estimate on resume**: if time since last activity exceeds the TTL, "Resume will re-cache ≈ N tokens" (N = current context size); otherwise "cache likely warm (≈ M min left)". Always labelled as an estimate. Resuming a session whose cache has expired with more than 20k tokens asks first: **Archive and Start Fresh** (archives it and starts a new session in the same agent) or **Resume**.

**Transcript backups**: while a session is live Hive brings its copy in `<Project>/.hive/sessions/` up to date at most once a minute while it changes, at the end of every turn (`Stop`) and when the session exits, so sessions survive Claude Code's own cleanup. Transcripts only grow, so while the copy is still the start of the transcript (same first and last 4 KB at its length) only the new bytes are appended; otherwise (the first copy, a rewritten transcript) the whole file is copied to a temp file and renamed into place (`syncCopy` in `fsutil.ts`, one at a time per copy). A failed backup is retried at the next look. Archiving moves the backup to `.hive/archive` and brings it up to date the same way.

**Memory tab**: grouped by provider. Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, the user `CLAUDE.md` and its auto-memory folder for the project. Codex: `AGENTS.md` and the user `AGENTS.md`. When a project's agents use more than one provider and they don't read the same instructions, **Share one AGENTS.md…** makes AGENTS.md the shared file: CLAUDE.md gets an `@AGENTS.md` import line (anything else in it stays, for Claude Code only); with no AGENTS.md yet, CLAUDE.md's content moves into it first, so nothing is lost. Providers declare the import line in their descriptor (`instructionsImport`); those that read AGENTS.md themselves need none.

**Usage cache**: each transcript's usage is worked out incrementally and kept in memory, and also in `usage-cache.json` in the profile (§4.3): per transcript path, its session, provider, size, modified time and the parser's result **before costs**. Costs (the provider's report, Hive's estimate, each day's share) are worked out from it with the current prices whenever it's shown, so price changes need nothing more. After a restart, a transcript with the same size and modified time is taken from the file without being read; one that changed while Hive was closed (a session continued outside Hive) is read again in full, since the parser's state isn't saved. The file is derived and disposable: written a few seconds after a change and at quit, ignored when damaged or written by another Hive version (each version starts afresh, as parsers change), and **Settings → Sessions → Clear the usage cache** deletes it. **Usage cache size** (5,000 transcripts; 100–50,000) bounds it, least recently used first; the parsers themselves (each holding a session's request history) stay limited to 200.

**Busy actions** (a shared pattern: `useBusy()`, `BusyButton` and `Modal`'s `busy`/`error`; `confirm({ run })`; `runOnce()` for buttons without a dialog): while an action runs its button shows a spinner and an "…ing" label, the dialog's other controls are disabled, Escape, × and clicks outside don't close it, and repeat clicks do nothing; a failure keeps the dialog open with the error (the button offers **Try Again** where it helps). Used by Start, Remove Project (its label says what it does: Hiding…, Removing from Hive…, Moving to the Recycle Bin…; no stages reported), the card's Save, Delete, Archive and Comment, Compact, Agent Settings, Stop All Agents, Delete note and Delete MCP server; Resume All and Remove Agent show a spinner on their button or tab. Questions (confirm, choose, prompt) render above every other dialog, and Escape closes only the topmost dialog.
**Dialogs dim the window buttons.** A backdrop (`.overlay`: a dialog, nested or not, or the command palette) dims the whole page, but Windows paints the native minimise, maximise/restore and close buttons over it (`titleBarOverlay`). So the page tells main how many backdrops are up (`useBackdrop()` keeps them per window, in order; `window:setBackdrops`), and main dims the buttons' background and symbols the same way: black at the backdrop's 45% once for each, as the page's layers stack (`shared/titleBar.ts`; a question over a card is two), back to the theme's colours when the last one goes. The same stack decides which backdrop Escape is for: the top one only, so Escape with the palette over a card closes the palette. Main keeps each window's theme colours and dimmed state (`main/titleBar.ts`), so a theme change while a dialog is up stays dimmed in the new theme's colours, each window is on its own, and a page that reloads starts undimmed. The buttons keep working, and Windows still highlights them on hover. The overlay exists on Windows and Linux only; elsewhere there is nothing to dim.

**Footer usage belongs to its conversation**: the usage an agent's footer shows (context, cost) is held with the project and session it was read for (`src/shared/liveUsage.ts`); after a switch it shows `– ctx` until the new conversation's own usage is read, never the previous one's, and a refresh that fails keeps the same conversation's numbers, faded, with a note in the tooltip.

**Transcript size**: a running agent's live state carries its transcript's size (`transcriptBytes`, from the 5-second look). The pane footer shows it next to the context count ("6.1 MB"), orange from **Settings → Sessions → Warn when a transcript is over** (20 MB; per-project override; **Never** turns it off; a 50 MB saved by 0.3.x or earlier, the old default, is moved to 20 MB once, config version 5). A long transcript slows the CLI and Hive (each turn, resume and transcript view reads more), and compacting doesn't shrink it, since the file keeps the whole history. Crossing the limit notifies once per conversation (a toast with **Hand Over to…**, and a desktop notification under the usual settings, no chime). Clicking the size opens **Hand Over to…** for the agent, with the agent itself chosen when its transcript is over the limit. The Assistant sees `transcriptMB` and `transcriptWarnMB` in `hive_agent_activity`. For the Assistant itself the advice is a new conversation, and the click does nothing. The Sessions tab (a project's, and the Assistant's) shows every session's transcript size beside its context (`transcriptBytes` in `session:list`, #671): a stat of the CLI's own transcript (Claude Code's, Codex's rollout, Copilot's events) each time the list loads (it opens, or a session changes), never a read; amber past the same limit, with a tooltip suggesting a handover (the Assistant: a new conversation); **—** when the file is missing or can't be read.

All parsing lives in the adapter, tolerates unknown fields, and degrades to "unavailable".

---

### 9.1 Performance metrics

Hive counts what its own parts cost, locally, per workspace (`metrics.ts`; the contract and units are in `shared/metrics.ts`), so a change to its guidance or API can be judged by what it does to real use. **On by default** (Settings → Sessions → **Record performance metrics**); turned off, nothing new is recorded and what was recorded stays until **Reset performance metrics**. Kept **30 days**: hourly buckets for 7 days, folded into daily ones after, in `.hive/metrics/metrics.json` (git-ignored, this machine's; at most 2 MB, the oldest buckets going first), saved 30 seconds after a change, when the workspace closes and when Hive quits. Retention is applied whenever the data is loaded or read, however long Hive was idle, and what expires is removed from the file too (a read that changes nothing writes nothing); a store holds at most 201 buckets whichever way the clock moves, and buckets dated more than a day ahead (a clock that was ahead) are dropped. A workspace's metrics file has one writer at a time across openings: a workspace reopened (by any window, its path in any case) while its last save is still going continues with that same store, so a Reset or new records can't be overwritten by the older save. The 2 MB cap is in UTF-8 bytes, even if only one bucket is left, so Hive never writes a file it would refuse. The saved file is untrusted input: a file over 2 MB isn't read (the read itself stops at the cap), and every bucket, series, counter and label in it is checked as it is loaded (what isn't valid is skipped).

- **What is measured, in exact units**: UTF-8 bytes; characters as UTF-16 code units (JavaScript string length); milliseconds on a monotonic clock (the wall clock only picks the bucket, so a clock set back lands in an earlier hour). **No token estimates**: provider token usage is only ever what the provider reported.
- **Agent API**: requests by route template (never the path asked for), method, caller role (agent, Assistant, script) and outcome (ok, client error, denied, server error, cancelled: a client that went away before the reply); count, a latency histogram, request body bytes as received (a partial or refused body as far as it came) and response body bytes as serialized (an aborted reply may not all have been sent; headers and transport not included). Requests refused before Hive knew whose they were, event streams (connections, events, bytes, time open) and requests in flight are process-wide, not any workspace's. The health check and the bridge's own reports aren't counted.
- **hive tools (MCP)**: the bridge reports each call's final text, the one the model got (characters and UTF-8 bytes), its time, outcome and mode (compact, or detail when the call asked for it), and once a session start the tool list as its CLI got it (bytes of the JSON). Reported with the session's own token (an agent's or the Assistant's), checked and bounded (at most 50 calls a report; a malformed one is refused, not stored), and never counted again as API traffic. Reports never hold the bridge up: one at a time, each with its own 3-second deadline, at most 500 calls waiting; a report that fails or times out drops what is still waiting, so a bridge whose CLI has gone exits within one deadline.
- **Guidance**: each launch's Hive-supplied parts, exact sizes, by provider and role, each part apart (`launchParts()` in `guidance.ts`): **core** (the session contract, once, whichever way the provider gets it), **custom** (what Hive adds for the project: the latest handover's pointer), the Assistant's **role** (who it is, the workspace, Control's rules) and its **persona** (text that isn't as expected is counted whole, never split by guesswork), the skills that reached the session, measured on the copies its CLI reads, not their sources (how many, their catalog entries' bytes, their bytes on disk; a model reads a skill's body only when it uses it, which isn't observable), how many skills asked for didn't reach it (too big, a link that can't be made, a failed copy, a broken header), and how many it got whose copy couldn't be measured (`skillsUnmeasured`: gone, unreadable, or a header that isn't a valid skill header; left out of the sizes, unknown rather than zero).
- **Skill service**: scans (timed), shared scans, hits, misses, invalidations, skills too big to check, and the files, bytes, header bytes and directory entries it read: the workspace's, not any project's.
- **Provider usage**: what the providers reported for sessions active in the range (as the Overview has it), summed per provider and whose sessions they were (project agents' or the Assistant's; scripts have none, and the report says so): each session's total, labelled as such, how many are running now, requests, compactions, and context as a snapshot (each session's last request: how many reported one, average, largest, the largest window), never summed. Its cells are one or two deliberate lines (#241, `CellLines` in `components/DataTable.tsx`): Sessions the count over "n running · n unknown", Context the average over "max of window", Cost "≈ $…" over "+ n unknown"; a table's numbers never wrap (`.table td.num`), and a table too wide for its section scrolls in its `.table-wrap`. A session with none reported is unknown, not zero: left out of the totals and counted as unknown cost too (a row whose sessions are all unknown shows unknown, not $0). The workspace's own work and the Assistant filter keep the Assistant's sessions only; a project scope never has them. Session history that can't be read (a project's or the Assistant's) is counted, never named (`providersUnreadable` of `providersHosts`): the totals are partial, or unknown if none could be read, and the page says so even when there is nothing else to show; the next read that works reports in full again.
- **Coverage**: when Hive observed the workspace (open in Hive, recording on, since the last reset), kept with the metrics as up to 500 `[start, end]` stretches, extended by each record and query and every 5 minutes while the workspace is open (an unref'd tick; saved with the next save, never a write of its own), so it is right to within about 5 minutes; a longer gap starts a new stretch. Known boundaries are never joined over, however short the gap: recording turned off ends the stretch at that moment (the setting's change, or a touch that finds it off), the last window holding the workspace closing ends it (another window reopening it while it saves starts a new one), and a store loaded from disk starts a new one at its first use. History evicted to keep the file under its cap or the bucket count (not by age) is unavailable, not idle: the store keeps `evictedThrough` (saved), observation before it is removed, and every report whose range reaches it says so (`coverage.evictedThrough`, the storage's own fact, no loss count). Expired with the buckets, cleared by Reset. A report has `coverage` (the range, the time observed in it, since when, how many stretches) and each trend point `observedMs`, so an unobserved slot reads as no data, not zero; rates are per hour observed.
- **Scope and attribution**: work is recorded in the workspace it started in (a workspace that is closing admits nothing new: its lifetime stays ended until it has fully closed): a request, launch, scan or read that is still going when its workspace closes (or its window opens another) is dropped, never recorded in the next one, and a metrics read that the workspace switches under is refused. An agent's work is its project's; the Assistant's, scripts' and the shared skill service's are the workspace's own, never spread over projects. A workspace query has each project's part and the workspace's own, which add up to its totals without counting anything twice; a project query has that project's part only (matched case-insensitively, as the API matches project names), and its `dropped` counts only that project's dropped measurements. Who lost measurements is itself bounded (as many owners as projects); past that a loss is untracked: a workspace report has that total (`droppedUntracked`), and a project report only says some losses couldn't be attributed (`lossesUnattributed`), with no count from other projects or the workspace, never a known zero. A project's own known losses are summed bucket by bucket. Project names are plain keys: a project called `constructor` or `__proto__` is like any other, and a name longer than 128 characters is kept under a bounded label of its own (its start, `|` and a hash of the whole name), matched the same way. `|` can't be in a project's name, so such a label never equals another project's name. A measurement that is only dropped (a cap was full) is still saved as a loss. An agent may read its own project's.
- **Bounds**: every label comes from a fixed set: the Agent API's route templates, the hive MCP server's own tools (a list a test keeps equal to the server's), the provider ids; anything else is "(other)". Per hourly bucket at most 100 projects and 2000 series, what doesn't fit dropped and counted (`dropped`). Nothing stores a prompt, a body, a token, a URL or a path. Recording a request costs about 0.3 µs; a bucket at its series cap is about 430 KB; the collector has no timers while idle.
- **Performance view** (activity bar, after Workspace Overview; `views/Performance.tsx`, selection and totals in `shared/metricsView.ts`): the workspace's metrics, with a scope filter (sidebar or **Scope**: the whole workspace, the workspace's own work, or one project, queried as that project's scope), and a **Performance** tab in each project (after Overview) fixed to its project (no workspace or other-project data reaches it: its query is the project scope). Ranges **24 hours, 7 days, 30 days**; filters by role (agents, Assistant, scripts; the tab has agents only) and provider (launches and provider usage; the rest isn't per provider), kept per view and project while Hive runs. Each filter sits with the sections it changes, never above ones it doesn't (#270): **Who** heads a framed group (`.perf-group`) of the cards, trend, tool, route, guidance and provider usage tables and the tool list; the skill service and Hive-wide numbers are below it, outside. **Provider** heads a group inside it holding only the guidance table and provider usage, the parts recorded per provider (a provider still chosen keeps the group, saying "Nothing from it", even when the scope or range has nothing at all, so it can be seen and changed, #294) (the guidance per launch card, the trend's launches and the tool list are every provider's). Role and own work go to Hive with the query (`queryFor()`: `role`, `own` for the workspace's own work), which narrows the parts, the trend and the provider usage alike; the page's query has no provider (its key neither), and `select()` applies the provider to those two sections, so the filter's choices (`providersIn(report, chosen)`) are every provider with data in the scope, range and role, plus the current one, and switching provider needs no query. The load, **Export** and **Keep current view** all use that query (`pageQuery()`), so what is saved is what the page loaded, every provider's (each per-provider row names its provider), and its launches, trend and totals agree with the cards whatever provider is picked; a kept view says "every provider". What a filter can't narrow is said (`filters.notFiltered`: the skill service, Hive-wide numbers and dropped measurements aren't per role). Cards (requests per hour observed, failure and cancelled shares apart, p50/p95 from the histogram, bytes sent and received, tool characters and bytes, guidance per launch), a trend (`trend` in the query: every hour of a range up to 2 days, else every day, at most 200 points, empty slots zero) of requests with failures, tables by tool (by characters), route (failed and cancelled apart), guidance (provider and role: per launch core, project, role, persona, skills, catalog, bytes on disk, not delivered or unmeasured) and provider usage (by provider and whose: sessions and running, tokens, reasoning shown as part of output, requests, context average and largest of the window, compactions, cost with ≈ when estimated and unknown sessions counted). Unobserved trend slots are hatched, partly observed ones shaded. The workspace scope adds the skill service and Hive-wide streams. Coverage (range, retention, how long Hive was recording in it and the gaps, dropped measurements, losses not attributed: shown for any project scope, from either entry) and what isn't measured are always shown, and a banner when recording is off. Refreshes every minute while shown and the window is visible, one request at a time, and a late reply for another scope or range is never shown; it reads through IPC (`metrics:query`), so looking at it records nothing. **Export** (`metrics:export`) saves the page's own query (scope, range, filters) as JSON (`schema: "hive-metrics/1"`, Hive's version, units, export time, range, filters, coverage, and the report with its trend) through a save dialog; Shift+click sanitizes it (project names become `project-N`, the same name the same number; no workspace path).
- **Compare** (#117; the Performance page's Now/Compare switch, `views/PerformanceCompare.tsx`, rules in `shared/benchmark.ts`, storage in `main/benchmarks.ts`): a baseline against a run, each a kept artifact of the page's scope. Sources: **Keep current view** (the page's own query as an export summary), **Import…** of a Performance export (`hive-metrics/1`, kept as its summary) or a scenario benchmark (`hive-benchmark/1`, written by `npm run scenarios`). Files are untrusted input, imported or kept alike (the same normalisers, a fresh bounded object, never the one read): read with a cap (at most 2 MB in UTF-8 bytes, however the file changes meanwhile), known measures only, bounded scenarios (100), samples (20), checks (60), skill revisions (60) and text; a series that isn't what it should be counts as none. Scopes: the whole workspace, **its own work** (the view with "Workspace's own work" chosen: its own list, never the whole workspace's) and each project (its tab, or the view with it chosen). A scenario benchmark is the whole workspace's only. A project's export must hold only that project's data (one also holding other projects' or the workspace's is refused, never summed); a workspace export's project part only after the page asks (sanitized ones, own-work ones and ones without that project are refused, and why); an own-work export must hold no project's. Up to 20 kept per workspace in `.hive/metrics/benchmarks` (an index and one file each); each scope remembers its baseline and run (selections of scopes with nothing kept are dropped). Every operation is bound to the workspace it started in: its folder and lifetime are taken before any await (a dialog, a read, the queue), and one whose workspace closed or switched meanwhile is refused, never written into the next; a project-part answer is bound to that workspace, scope and lifetime, used once, within 10 minutes. The index is the only authority and records every change in progress: an entry being added (`adding`) or removed (`removing`), with its change's id. Keeping is (1) the index records the new entry as adding and the evicted ones (the oldest unpinned, when full; refused if all are pinned) as removing, (2) the file is written, (3) the new entry becomes kept; then each evicted file is removed and its entry dropped only once the file is gone. Remove is the same: recorded as removing (off the list), the file removed, then the entry dropped; a removal whose file can't be removed stays recorded, is retried on every read (also after a restart), is said, and never comes back to the list. The index holds at most 40 entries in all (kept, being added, being removed): the reader's bound, which every index Hive writes is checked against first, and a keep that would pass it (removals piling up while their files stay in use) is refused, saying why, until they finish. Renames are retried while Windows holds a file for a moment, the workspace checked before every attempt, so a retry after it closed or switched commits, moves or sets aside nothing. Every write is staged and renamed into place only after the workspace is checked again, so a workspace that closes or switches during a write commits nothing. Every read first validates the whole index, then reconciles it with the folder, deleting only what that validated index proves: an add whose change never finished is rolled back (its file goes, what it evicted is kept again), and recorded removals are retried. A file the index doesn't account for (unreadable, or valid but unlisted) is never deleted: it is moved once to `quarantine/`, named by when it came there (time, then a sequence for the same moment), and the newest 100 so named are kept (the oldest by that time go, whatever their ids; other files there are left alone) and said, and an entry whose file is gone is dropped. A damaged index (not JSON, not an index, over 512 KB, an entry invalid or repeated, too many entries) is never acted on: it is set aside as `index.damaged-<time>.json` (the newest 10 kept) and rebuilt by adopting every valid kept file with what the old index still said about it (pin, when kept); invalid files and valid ones past the limit go to quarantine, so the rebuilt index accounts for every file and is stable afterwards; the page says it was recovered. An index that can't be read for another reason (permissions, I/O) is an error and nothing is written. A kept file whose scope doesn't match its index entry is refused. Comparable only when they measured the same thing: the same kind and scope; for benchmarks the same fixtures version, provider, fake or model, and for model trials the same model, effort and mode (CLI versions differ: a note); for exports the same filters and some recorded time on both. Otherwise **Not comparable**, with why, numbers shown. Benchmarks compare scenario by scenario on usable samples: completed, correctness known (a sample whose checks all skipped is unknown, never passed) and not stale (left-out ones said). Each measure has mean, min, max and n, the change and whether it is within the spread; a measure some sample didn't measure is **unknown** (no zero-filled totals: context and guidance exist only when every part is known) and isn't compared. Correctness first, check by check, the check list being authoritative (its counts are used; summary counts that disagree with it, a check listed twice, an entry that isn't a check, or more checks than can be read leave the sample's correctness unknown, and any failed check in it fails the sample): a check that passed or ran less often, or failed more often (one the baseline never had included), in the run is a regression (a fake's declared skips are skipped on both sides, so they aren't), shown with its counts and the run's failed checks; a regression is *smaller but failing* (less context) or *worse*. Otherwise more failed calls, tool errors, API failures or cancellations, more calls or retries, or more than 1% more context is *worse*; less context is *better*, else *same*; a scenario with a partial measurement (recording off or unknown, measurements dropped or missing) or an unknown failure measure is *incomplete*, not judged on size. Comparable also needs both runs' sources unchanged while they ran. Provider usage only between model trials (a fake's is simulated: said), only when every usable sample on both sides reported that field (unknown stays unknown, a resumed session's is the conversation's), cost only when both have one (≈ said when Hive estimated it), reasoning its own row. The skill revisions each session was given are compared, and a difference (an edited skill) said. The page shows each side's provenance; while comparing it hides the live range and filters, which only shape **Keep current view**. Exports compare per hour recorded (guidance per launch as is), noting partial data and that real use isn't a controlled workload. A total that any damaged piece of the export adds to (a series that is missing, isn't a list, is too long to read, or has an invalid number) is unknown, said, and not compared; never zero. A late answer for another pair or scope is never shown.
- **Scenario benchmarks** (`tests/scenarios`, developer tooling): each run writes `benchmark.json` with every scenario's samples: each check (passed, failed, skipped), failed checks, incomplete with a path-free reason, stale guidance, resumed, the guidance and skill revisions the session was given, the measurement's coverage (recording, dropped, what made it partial), and measures: Hive's metrics before the launch and after the turn, the harness's own requests left out (context in parts with bytes and characters, tool replies, calls and repeated calls, failures, API traffic, delivered and undelivered skills, skill service work, time, and the session's provider usage as reported: a field it didn't report is left out). Fixtures v5 adds controlled pairs: **list-compact** / **list-detail** (the same cards, the compact list against the explicit detail form, the same required correctness) and **skill-unchanged** / **skill-changed** (a skill edited inside the measured window, `beforeLaunch`). Both fake providers act the scenarios out (`fake`, `fake-codex`); model trials are opt-in and record their model, setup and authorization. Model trials keep to `--budget` (a finite amount above 0): a trial that reports no cost makes the spend unknown, so no further trial starts unless `--allow-unknown-cost` is given, and the spend is the reported subtotal plus the unpriced trials, unknown in all (never $0). `--repeat N` (default 1), `--save-baseline NAME` (the new one read first and swapped in atomically; the older one archived under a name of its own, never overwritten, also when saved twice in a second; 5 kept), each run in a results folder of its own, the newest 30 kept.
- **Cost per card and per lane** (#602, Darren's decision of 11 Oct; `shared/cardCosts.ts`, `main/cardCosts.ts`, `components/CardCost.tsx`, `views/PerformanceCards.tsx`). Usage is the card's while an agent **held** it: its builder (`TaskCard.agent`) while it is in Doing, its reviewer (`TaskCard.review`) from `review: "start"` to the verdict. Cards keep their holds (`TaskCard.held`: agent id, name, role, from, to; the newest 100), updated whenever a card is saved (`trackHolds` in `saveCard`), whoever changed it; a card from before #602 gets them from its history the first time (`holdsFromHistory`: "Given to", "Taken from", column moves, "Started reviewing" and verdicts; agents by name, the card's own agent and reviewer by id). The holds are left out of the Agent API's card views (lean replies). Each provider's parser also gives the session's usage **over time** (`SessionUsage.timeline`, `shared/usageTimeline.ts`): rows of a second (about one a request: Claude Code's requests, Codex's `token_count` increases, Copilot's requests), merged into 10, 100… second steps past 20,000 rows (`boundTimeline`: each step's charges merged into one, their sum, so a session with a charge per request is bounded too and no charge is lost; a hold taking part of a merged charge gets ≈; charges no request refers to are left out), with the **charges** the provider reported and which requests each covers (`reports`; a row's report index, or none): Claude Code's running total, each increase covering the requests since the last; Copilot's credits at each checkpoint, covering the requests since the one before (a checkpoint that charged nothing more covers its requests at $0). Copilot gives tokens only when a launch exits: until then its requests' tokens are **unknown** (`TOKENS_UNKNOWN`), and then the launch's total is spread over its requests (`TOKENS_SPREAD`, ≈). The timeline is kept in usage-cache.json with the parser's result (an entry without a valid one is read again) and removed before pricing, so it never travels with a session's usage. A hold's usage is its agent's sessions' rows inside it (a row partly inside counts in part). Its cost: a charge is split only between the requests it covers (by Hive's estimate for their tokens, else their tokens, their requests, or equally), so a later card never changes an earlier one's; exact where all of a charge's requests are in the hold, ≈ where the hold takes only some; requests no charge covers are Hive's estimate (≈), and with the user's own prices (#436) everything is; no price, or no tokens to price yet, is unknown. Tokens are tracked apart from cost: unknown tokens are never shown as 0 ("tokens unknown", "+ unknown"), and shares Hive worked out are ≈. While the agent held several cards at once, what it used then is shared between them equally (`sharedSpans`, ≈), so nothing counts twice. A hold's sessions are its agent's (by id; by name for one from history, also matching sessions whose record kept that name); a deleted session's kept usage has no timeline and is **unknown** where it was active in a hold, as is a hold whose agent has no session Hive knows of. Estimates are marked ≈ and unknown parts shown as such (`costText`: "Unknown", "+ ?"), never $0. **Where it shows**: the card's panel ("cost: ≈ $1.84 · 2 rounds", rounds as `cardRounds`: none before it first went to Review, then each failed review and the round under way or passed; the builder's and reviewer's parts and tokens on hover); the **lane's hover** (its top line in the Progress panel: the builder's and reviewer's usage from the lane's start to its end or now, split into on cards (holding any card) and **overhead** (holding none: merges, watching, compactions), and the cost per finished card); and Performance's sortable **Cards** table (cards held in the range, each card's whole cost as Provider usage counts whole sessions; a project's scope only its cards; none for the workspace's own work; outside Who's box, since it is agents' work of every provider). Read on request (`costs:cards`, `costs:lanes`), nothing stored beyond the holds. Money by default (#436's prices), tokens on hover.
- **Safeguard stops** (#606): each session's stops by model, read from its transcript with its usage (`SessionUsage.safeguards`), summed per provider (Providers' **Safeguard stops** column, "not detected" for Copilot) and per project, agent and model (`MetricsReport.safeguards`, the **Safeguard stops** table below Providers; the Assistant as itself). A sanitized export names agents agent-1, agent-2… as it does projects. A stop seen only on screen (the prompt about one that left no transcript record) is counted from the session's record (`screenStops`, added by `usage()` unless the transcript recorded it in the same turn), so it counts during the launch and after a restart. Each row is the agent's name now (its name then once it is gone: `SessionRecord.agentName`, `KeptUsage.agentName`).
- **Not measured**, and said in every report (`notMeasured`): provider tokens per tool or guidance part, provider network traffic (Hive isn't a proxy), context a CLI adds itself, whether a model read a skill, and HTTP headers.

### 9.2 Developer console

What each CLI actually sends Hive, next to what Hive made of it (#366: the CLIs are the most likely thing to break Hive, and this shows what happens instead of ad-hoc probes). For power users and Hive's developers. Built in three parts: the capture layer and the agents' read-only tool (#636), the console window (#637: Help → Developer Console and a shortcut, its own window, hidden until "Show developer features"), and recording to a file, Copy Diagnostics and contract tests (#638).

- **Sources** (`devConsoleSources.ts`): each agent's session (a project's agents and the Assistant), whether it runs or not (capture can start before it launches), and **Hive itself** in a workspace. A session's events: its hooks (with the reply a PreToolUse got), status lines, transcript reads (the newest records and the details read from them, plan limits included), the terminal markers Hive reacts to (the title asking for attention, the startup question, the prompt showing, the footer's mode), the lines Hive types into it, its launch (the CLI, its version, the option names it was given from a fixed list of the CLIs' own, and how many other arguments: an option's value, inline or not, can be a prompt, so none is kept) and exit, and its own Agent API calls. Hive's: Agent API calls (route template, caller, status, time, sizes; never a body), refused hooks, merge slot claims, releases and changes, watches waking agents, and Progress runs as they start, step on and end.
- **Each event is paired** with what Hive made of it: a hook with the event Hive took it for, the status that followed and the actions it ran; a status line or transcript read with the details read from it; a terminal marker with the status it set. A one-line summary in Hive's own words goes with it.
- **Off unless the console is open** (`devConsole.ts`, #366 decision 4): a source is captured from the first console window watching it to the last; before and after, a capture point returns at once without building its event, and nothing is kept. Readers (agents' and the Assistant's hive tools) start nothing: they read what an open console has, and are told when it captures nothing. Each source keeps its newest **500 events or 1 MB**, in memory only; at most 40 sources a window and 50 windows; an event is never over 32 KB (its payloads cut to their outline, then to `<cut>`; names and summaries cut too); what waits to be sent to a window is bounded (500 events or 1 MB, the oldest dropped and counted in `dropped`).
- **Redacted at capture** (`shared/devConsole.ts`): payloads are copied field by field, and a field's context follows everything under it. A secret or sign-in field (tokens, authorization, cookies, logins, device and user codes) is `<redacted>` whole; under a text field (prompts, messages, replies, commands, titles, questions) no string is kept; elsewhere a string is kept only at a path listed for that kind of payload (`HOOK_FIELDS`, `STATUSLINE_FIELDS`, `TRANSCRIPT_FIELDS`, `DETAILS_FIELDS` and each capture point's own: a hook's envelope and its tool input's file path, a status line's model, a transcript record's type, model and tool names, Hive's own fields) and only when it looks like an identifier, a path or a time: any other string, one word or many, is `<text: N chars>`, so a tool's arguments (a search query, a URL), an unknown record and an unlisted id, name or model stay hidden. Field names are matched by their words, however spelt (`idToken`, `api-key`, `Prompt`); a text field named as an envelope (a transcript record's `message`) keeps only the listed fields under it. Anything kept that looks like a token or key is masked too (`redact.ts`). Summaries and names are built from such safe values only (a Progress run's title, someone's text, stays hidden in its payload). Bounded: 40 items a list, 60 fields an object (names cut to 64 characters), 6 levels. A subscription may include text, the user's recordings (#638) and the console's windows while **Developer console: show text** is on (#699, below): it alone is sent a copy with text kept, secrets still masked; agents never.
- **Showing text** (#699): **Settings → Advanced → Developer console: show prompt, reply and command text** (`hive.console.showText`, off by default, a value of the advanced registry, §9.3) makes main subscribe the console's windows with text (`console:subscribe` takes no opt-in from the page: main decides from the setting), and a change applies to open windows at once (`DevConsole.setIncludeText`; turned off, what waits to be sent is dropped and the window reads its events again, redacted, so no text stays on screen). Only what is captured from then on has text: while a source has a subscriber with text, its events with text are kept in a second ring (same bounds, dropped with the last such subscriber), which only those subscribers read (`eventsFor`: a window's resume after a pause); the redacted ring stays the only one readers see. While it is on the window shows an **Unredacted** banner and its status bar says so, and the recording box starts ticked. **Never configurable:** secret and sign-in fields are masked whole as always, and the text kept goes through `maskText` (`redact.ts`). Its main rule (the Assistant's, after #699 round 3, replacing three rounds of a value scanner that let fragments through): **from a credential's name to the end of the string, everything is masked**, with no parsing of quotes, escapes, continuations or options: after a command-line option naming a credential and its separator (`--api-key k`, `--token=t`), a key naming one and its `:` or `=` (`token=t`, `password: p`, `Authorization: Bearer t`, `$env:API_KEY = k`, a key whose quotes are escaped as in a JSON string inside another), `-u user:…`, or `Bearer ` (`credentialStart`, the Assistant's rule after round 5: no regex, a single pass of direct character checks: each run of name characters, letters, digits, `_`, `-` and `.`, is read once and holds a credential's word once its spaces, dots, underscores and dashes are taken out (`GITHUB_TOKEN`, `csrftoken`, `x-api-key`), then the characters after it are checked for a key's closing quote and `:` or `=`, or an option's whitespace; a quoted name, whatever is in it (`'api key':`, `"token[0]":`, `"password/value":`), is read up to 100 characters ahead to the next quote of either kind and tested whole, `-u`'s user:password up to 256 after its quote; one that reaches its limit without a clear end (no closing quote, `:` or space) is masked from there whatever it holds, a quoted name once what was read names a credential (#705 round 3: no exact budget decides whether a password shows)). Losing what follows a secret (the flags after it, a URL's other parameters) is the accepted cost. A quoted name counts the same (the Assistant's rule after round 4): a JSON or Python key naming a credential at any depth, in a list, in malformed or embedded JSON, spelt with spaces or dots too (`"password":`, `'api key':`, `"api.key":`), masks everything after it to the end of the string, the JSON's other fields too, so no container can hide one (round 4 found a field-by-field JSON masking that an ordinary object or list swallowed). A payload Hive holds as an object is masked by its keys instead (`redactPayload`'s field rules), not by this. Then: known token and key formats, JWTs, OAuth and signed-URL codes in a URL (`code=`, `state=`, `otc=`, `sig=`, `ticket=`, `nonce=`), long hex and base64 runs, email addresses, and sign-in codes no name announces: a device code standing alone (`ABCD-EFGH`) and a code after the words a sign-in uses (`enter QX7PL29KD`, `code: …`). Every pattern left starts only where its match can start (a word's or a run's start: a JWT only where its run starts, a base64 run not after a `+` or `=`), has no two quantifiers next to each other that can read the same characters, so a failed match never reads the same run again and masking is linear; the log's user-text marks stop at the next opening mark too. Unit tests bound it on every whitespace class, on long input shaped to make a backtracking pattern slow, and on a sweep of every pair of the characters the patterns care about and every credential prefix followed by long runs (200,000 characters each, where a quadratic pattern takes minutes; #699 round 5 found the credential-name regex's quoted-name escapes and `-u` lookahead, JWT, base64 and user-text marks quadratic, round 4 the email and sign-in-code patterns; since round 6 credential names use no regex at all), and on backslashes and quotes at 10k, 100k and 1M characters. A report goes through it line by line (Copy Diagnostics' log: a credential masks the rest of its line, and of the lines a backtick or backslash continuation carries it onto). It errs towards masking. In the console the text is cut to its cap (2,000 characters) first and then masked (the Assistant's rule after round 5: masking never reads more than is kept, and nothing past the cap is kept); a word the cut goes through is dropped whole, so no part of a secret too short to be recognised is kept. And it is the **last step over the whole event**, text kept or not (`makeEvent`: the event's name and summary, every payload string and every field name; the size bound after it only cuts), so a secret is masked wherever it sits, not only in the fields known to hold one; a source's label is masked too. Cookie headers (every cookie), and a device login's or signed URL's `otc=`, `sig=`, `ticket=`, `nonce=` are masked as well. A pattern it lacks goes into `redact.ts`, so the log and Copy Diagnostics get it too. **Agents' `hive_console` stays redacted** whatever it says (#366 decision 1: agents' access is read-only, redacted and bounded; the Assistant reads every agent of its workspace, so following the setting would hand it their prompts); a recording with text is still never attached to Copy Diagnostics, and Copy Diagnostics lists the setting among the advanced overrides by its id.
- **Agents and the Assistant** read it through `hive_console` (Agent API `/v1/console/…`), only while **Settings → Agent API → Let agents read the developer console** is on (off by default; read-only to the Assistant; the tool is offered from a session's next start). A project agent reads its own session; the Assistant its workspace's agents, its own session and Hive's events there; a source a caller may not read is refused as if it didn't exist. Replies are a line per event (the newest 30 the first time, then oldest first after `since`), with up to 3 events in full on request; sources come 50 a page, each with `select`, the exact arguments that read it (the agent's id and its project's name), apart from its display label. The console's own reads aren't events.
- **The window** (`consoleWindow.ts`, `ConsoleApp.tsx`, #637; #366 decisions 1–3): hidden until **Settings → Advanced → Show developer features** is on (off by default; drawn first in Settings → Advanced's list since #367, under the same key): until then **Help → Developer Console** (Ctrl+Alt+J) is in no menu, the palette or Tips, the shortcut does nothing and `console:open` is refused; turning it off closes open consoles. Its own window like Chrome DevTools, one per Hive window on the workspace that window shows (opening it again brings it forward; with no workspace it is refused), remembering its size and position (`consoleWindow` in `config.json`). It closes with that window, and when that window leaves its workspace (another workspace opened in it, or the workspace closed): its capture ends with it, and opened again it shows the new workspace. Any window's subscription ends when it leaves the workspace it subscribed in, and `console:snapshot` reads only sources the window's current workspace may read. It may call only the console's channels and `settings:get` (for its theme). A **source picker** (Hive and the Assistant, then each project's agents) watches one source at a time, which starts its capture; picking none or closing the window ends it. The list shows each event's time, type, name and summary; picking one shows its **raw payload beside what Hive made of it**, as trees whose nodes over 1.5 KB start collapsed. **Filters**: event type (the types present, each on or off), provider and **search** (name, summary or payload, Ctrl+F); **pause** holds the list (capture goes on; the indicator counts what came) and resume fills it in from the ring; **clear** empties the list through the newest event received, including any held while paused, which never come back (a resume's snapshot starts after it); a **Live / Paused / Not watching** indicator; events not delivered (a burst over the window's queue) are counted in its status bar. The window keeps its source's newest 2,000 events. No recording yet (#638).
