- **Recording** (`consoleRecorder.ts`, #638; #366 decision 4): **Record** in the console writes the watched source's events from then on to `console-recordings\console-<date>-<time>-<ms>[-<n>]-<kind>.jsonl` in Hive's profile folder (reserved with an exclusive open; the newest is the one that started last, by its name's time and number): a header line (format, Hive's version, the source's kind, label and provider, whether text is kept, and each CLI's installed version beside the version this release was tested with), one line per event as the console captured it, and an end line (events, lost, why it ended). **Redacted by default**, as capture is; ticking **Include prompt and reply text** keeps text in that recording only (a subscription of its own with `includeText`; tokens and sign-ins stay masked), and the box starts unticked each time (ticked while the console shows text, #699). One recording per window, of a source it watches and only while it does: a window's starts and stops run one at a time, and a stop, a close, another source or another workspace cancels a start still on its way (checked again after each of its awaits; Hive logs a start that doesn't happen). It stops when the user stops it, the window watches another source, closes or leaves its workspace, or at 50 MB or 100,000 events; what was captured until that moment, even still queued, is written before the end line, and nothing after. Every write is written whole (however many writes it takes) and counted only once it is in the file; a header, event, end line or close that fails ends the recording as **failed**, with the reason, the file kept as written. The bar shows its progress, then the file's name and **Show in folder** (only Hive's own recordings, by name), or **Recording failed** and why.
- **Copy Diagnostics** (#638) offers the newest recording, unticked: attached, it adds a line about it (source kind, provider, Hive and CLI versions, events, how it ended) and its newest 200 events (at most 64 KB of UTF-8), every string redacted again as the log is (folders, names, secrets). A recording with text is never attached; the dialog says so.
- **Fixtures and contract tests** (`shared/consoleFixtures.mts`, #366 decision 5): `npm run console-fixture` turns a redacted recording into a fixture in `tests/fixtures/console/` (the CLI's events and Hive's reading of them, in order, absolute paths as `<path>`; a recording with text is refused). Real-tier **contract suites** record a scripted turn through the console and compare it with the fixture (`compareContract`): a hook, status line, launch or exit no longer arriving, or a **recognition marker** (`prompt shown`, `footer seen`: noted once a launch the first time Hive recognises the CLI's prompt or footer, whatever the agent's status, `screenRecognition.ts`; the suite waits for each the fixture has before its turn) no longer arriving, which means the CLI's screen changed, a hook's, status line's, launch's or exit's field gone or retyped, or a reading of the fixture's Hive no longer makes (a hook's event kind, status and actions; a marker's, launch's or exit's status, mode and flags, where both runs have it; text and what varies between runs left out) fails; Hive's status markers (`ready`, `footer mode`) come and go with timing (a transcript can make an agent ready before its prompt draws), so they are never required; new events, fields and readings are flagged. Transcript reads hold whichever records were newest, so only a new one is flagged. The first is `contract-copilot` (the real Copilot CLI offline against the stand-in model); tests/e2e/README.md and tests/fixtures/console/README.md say how they run and how a fixture is made.
- **The window's IPC** (`api.ts`): `console:open` (from a Hive window), `console:sources` (the window's workspace), `console:options` (`showText`; changes come as `console-options` to the console windows), `console:subscribe` (replaces the window's list and returns what each source has so far), `console:snapshot`, and `console:record`, `console:stopRecording`, `console:recordings` and `console:showRecording`; live events come as `console-events` and a recording's progress as `console-recording`, to that window only. `app:diagnostics` takes `{ recording }`.

### 9.3 Advanced settings

What Hive relies on that a CLI update can break, and timeouts and limits for power users, as named, documented defaults that can be overridden without a Hive release (#367; import and export are #683). The CLIs are what most often breaks Hive; the developer console (§9.2) shows what changed, and an override fixes it the same day. Remote updates of the defaults are not fetched (revisit after 0.7.0).

- **The defaults** (`shared/advanced.ts`, `ADVANCED_DEFS`): each has an id (`<hive|provider>.<what>.<which>`, as advanced.json names it), a title, what it is for and what goes wrong when it's wrong, its kind and default, and who takes a change (at once, or sessions started afterwards). **Data only, never code**: a regular expression's source (its flags are part of the default), text found as it is, a field name, a list of names, or a whole number in a range. From the adapter audit:
  - **Sign-in and trust**: each CLI's folder trust question (`claude-code.trust.prompt`, `codex.trust.prompt`, `copilot.trust.prompt`); the refused sign-in: Claude Code's StopFailure error (`claude-code.signIn.stopError`), Codex's error message in its session file (`codex.signIn.expired`), Copilot's session error type and message and its ErrorOccurred hook's message (`copilot.signIn.*`).
  - **Readiness**: Codex's composer placeholder (ready, and an idle input line, `codex.ready.placeholder`) and Copilot's footer (`copilot.ready.footer`).
  - **Field names**: Claude Code's plan limits in its status line (`claude-code.statusLine.rateLimits`, `.fiveHour`, `.weekly`, `.usedPercent`) and Codex's in its session file (`codex.rateLimits.field`, `.usedPercent`, `.windowMinutes`). Hive reads no rate-limit **labels** from a CLI (limits are identified by these fields), and no sign-in prompt from the screen (#309 reads hooks, session files and the CLIs' status commands).
  - **Codex's terminal title items** (`codex.title.items`).
  - **Turn screens** (#685): what Hive reads from a screen when it missed a turn's hooks (#648): Claude Code's line saying the last turn is done (`claude-code.turnScreen.done`, "✻ Worked for 12s · done 16:52"), Codex's Working line (`codex.turnScreen.working`); and Codex's hint that it queues what is typed (`codex.queue.hint`, "tab to queue message"), which keys Hive types wait out. Not overridable: the input line's markers and Claude Code's spinner line (structure, not text), and its "esc to interrupt" (no recording of 2.1.296 shows it).
  - **Codex's /permissions menu labels** (#685, #396): the preset Hive picks by its label (`codex.permissions.readOnly`, `.ask`, `.approveForMe`, `.fullAccess`).
  - **Safeguard stops** (#606, #685): Claude Code's transcript note (`claude-code.safeguard.note`) and its question's open dialog (`claude-code.safeguard.prompt`), defaults from `SAFEGUARD_DEFAULTS`, recorded from HB3's stop. Claude Code's record subtypes and Codex's reroute reasons and policy error kinds wait for a real recording (#697: names in a CLI's binary aren't one), and then a list of codes is checked against its samples by exact membership.
  - **Footer modes** (#696): how Hive reads an agent's permission mode from its CLI's footer on screen, which it also watches to switch modes with Shift+Tab: one pattern per mode, each a whole regular expression for that mode's footer (Claude Code's `claude-code.footer.manual`, `.acceptEdits`, `.plan`, `.auto`, `.bypassPermissions`, `.dontAsk`, "⏸ plan mode on (shift+tab to cycle)"; Copilot's `copilot.footer.ask`, `.allowAll`, `.plan`, `.autopilot`, "Plan · Manual Approval"). The mode whose pattern is found last on the screen wins. Copilot's Accept edits has none: its footer is Ask's, so a session launched in Accept edits is taken to be in it. The defaults are tables in shared code (`CLAUDE_FOOTER_MODES`, `COPILOT_FOOTER_MODES`), and the adapters pass the settings' values to the shared parsers. Each is checked against a screen recorded in its mode (`tests/fixtures/advanced/footer-*`), and must not find the other modes' screens.
  - **Timeouts**: each CLI's hook timeouts, the hooks' and before a tool's (`<provider>.hooks.timeoutSeconds`, `.preToolUseTimeoutSeconds`; Codex holds its interrupt and session-end hooks to 3 s, and a change is part of the trust hash Hive gives Codex, so it needs no review there); Codex's timeout for hive's tools (`codex.mcp.toolTimeoutSeconds`, 900 s); the grace a stopped Copilot gets to exit by itself before Hive kills it (`copilot.exit.graceSeconds`, 10 s, 3–60, #727; read at each stop, so it applies at once, and quitting or closing a workspace waits past it, §Copilot agents); the hive MCP server's reply timeout (`hive.mcp.replySeconds`, 300 s, #371) and the longest card and agents waits (`hive.wait.cardMaxSeconds` 840, `hive.wait.agentsMaxSeconds` 600), always kept a minute under Codex's tool timeout. The MCP server gets them in its environment at launch (`HIVE_REPLY_SECONDS`, `HIVE_WAIT_MAX_SECONDS`, `HIVE_AGENT_WAIT_MAX_SECONDS`), and its tool descriptions name them.
  - **The Progress panel's lanes shown** before its lanes scroll (`hive.progress.lanesShown`, 4; the dragged height still wins, per workspace).
  - **Show developer features** (#636, `hive.showDeveloperFeatures`) is first in the list. It keeps its settings key (`settings.advanced.showDeveloperFeatures`, the catalogue's `advanced.showDeveloperFeatures`) and is kept in advanced.json too: the two are one value (`bindAdvancedSettings`): a change of the setting from Settings or the Assistant is written to the file first (`updateSettingsChecked`), and refused, nothing changed, when the file can't take it (unreadable, or edited since Hive read it); Reset writes it after; a hand edit of the file changes the setting, and at the first start a setting already on is written to the file.
  - **Developer console: show prompt, reply and command text** (#699, `hive.console.showText`, off) follows it: the developer console's windows show text, secrets still masked (§9.2).
  - **Agents per project** (#562, `hive.agents.perProject`, 12; 1–24, Darren's 11 Oct decision: four pages of six, more should be another project; a workspace can set its own): adding an agent (Add Agent, quick add, a card's new agent, the Agent API, a template's agent) and loading a template keep to it, the main process checking it again under the project's lock (a limit lowered meanwhile refuses the change); the window greys Add Agent out at it. Lowering it removes no agent: a project with more keeps them and can't add another. The template editor holds as many agents as a project of its window's workspace can have; a template file holds up to 24. Above 12, its row in Settings → Advanced has a note on what more agents cost, in the machine's figures: the memory the running agents' processes take now (each launch's job, its processes' working sets, measured by the process tracker's `memory` request; roughly 200–500 MB each when none runs or it can't tell) and the computer's memory and free memory, and that they all share the plans' usage limits. **Audited** for 24: pages (four of six; more than six pages a compact pager), the agents table (#529: tighter rows above 12, fits a 700-high window), the agent watch (`AGENT_WATCH_MAX_AGENTS`, 32), templates (above), unique names (#702, unbounded). Fine as they are: Ctrl+1…9 (agents 10 and up never had one; Ctrl+Alt+] / [ and the pages reach every agent), role length (`ROLE_MAX` is characters, not agents), the Overview, inbox, tray and banners (bounded lists with "and N more"), workspace-wide bounds (open progress runs 30, kept leftover launches 100, watches 500: per workspace, already beyond one project of 12), a quiet restart's carried terminal output (20, taken back at once), the Progress panel's lanes (`hive.progress.lanesShown`, up to 24), and the e2e lanes (10 runners at once on a machine, bounded by the ports below 49152; heavy runs queue for 2 slots; waiting for a lane is #707). Tested at 24 (e2e `agents24`: 24 fake Claude Code agents started, 23 reporting runs to the Progress panel while the 24th watches them all, and the note measuring them; `pages`: pages, the pager, the agents table and a template of 24; `tests/agentLimit.test.ts`).
  - The heavy test-slot count stays in the test folder's config (#589); no other HIVE_* variable is a user setting (they are test hooks or session plumbing).
- **Settings → Advanced** lists them about:config style: grouped (Hive, then each CLI), filtered by its own search and **Modified only** and by Settings' search and Modified only; each shows its id, what it is for, the value, a modified mark with the default beside it, and **Reset**. A value that doesn't check out is refused there; one in the file that doesn't is listed as not valid, and Hive uses the default (logged once).
- **advanced.json** (Hive's profile folder) holds the same values: `{ "format": 1, "values": { id: value }, "madeFor": { id: { "version", "value" } } }`, written in order. **Open advanced.json** opens it in the user's editor (creating it); Hive follows the file and reads it again when it changes (an editor's save that replaces it too). A file it can't read (not JSON, not an object, a newer format) keeps the values read before, says why in the list, and changes from Settings are refused until it's fixed (they would overwrite it). A change from Settings while the file was edited since Hive read it is refused, and the edit read. Hive's own changes to the file go one at a time, each from the state the last one left, so two CLI versions learnt at once both reach it. Ids this Hive doesn't know are kept and listed, not used.
- **App-wide**, with a **workspace's own** value only where one makes sense (`workspace: true`: the lanes shown, and agents per project, #562), in `workspace.json` (`advanced`), set in the list's **This workspace** box; empty uses Settings'.
- **Validation**: a pattern must compile (with its flags), be one line of at most 500 characters and not match empty text (it would match any screen); text one line of at most 200; a field name `[A-Za-z_]\w*`; names `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*` (1 to 10, a list or comma-separated); numbers whole and in range.
- **CLI versions** (#367 decision 4): every value of a CLI's is CLI-facing, what Hive reads from it and what it gives it (hook timeouts, title items, tool timeout, Copilot's exit grace). Its override records the CLI version installed when it was set (or when Hive first learns it). A value edited by hand is made for the version installed when Hive reads it, and Hive writes that record back to the file (unless the file changed again meanwhile), so a restart after the CLI updates still flags it. Once another version is installed, the list flags it (**Made for Claude Code 2.1.296; 2.1.300 is installed now**) with **Keep for 2.1.300**.
- **Tested against recordings** (#367 decision 6): each CLI-facing default carries samples from recordings (`tests/fixtures/advanced`, recorded with test homes, no sign-in; and the earlier recordings): for a value Hive reads, the text or fields the CLI sent; for one Hive gives it, the CLI taking it (its hooks arriving, Codex listing the tool timeout it read, its title spinner, Copilot writing its totals and exiting by itself when Hive stopped it). The unit tests check each sample is in its fixture (named with, or saying, the CLI version), that the default reads it, that the adapters do with the defaults, and that an override takes effect (Codex's plan limits read under renamed fields once the overrides name them). The developer console's own recordings redact screen text, so the screens are recorded apart. An override is tried on the same samples: one that no longer finds them is kept (the CLI may have changed) and says what it misses.
- **Reset all settings** (Settings → Advanced) clears advanced.json's overrides too (ids this Hive doesn't know are kept), before Hive's settings; it is refused while advanced.json can't be read. A workspace's own values are kept.
- **Copy Diagnostics** lists the overrides by id (never their values), the workspaces' own values as a count, which aren't valid, which were made for another CLI version, and unknown ids.
- **The Assistant's settings catalogue** has each as `advanced.<id>` (and `workspace.advanced.<id>` for a workspace's own), read, explained and (with Change settings) changed like any other setting, null putting the default back.
- **IPC**: `advanced:state`, `advanced:set` (scope app or workspace; null removes), `advanced:keep`, `advanced:open`; the `advanced-changed` event to every window.

## 10. Provider framework

A provider is two pieces:

- **Descriptor** (`src/shared/providers.ts`, static data both processes use): id, product and company names, icon, permission modes (with danger flags and labels), effort levels, model groups, capabilities (fixed session IDs, live mode switch by cycle or menu, Plan toggle, prompt-cache TTL, reports cost, compact focus, 200K context limit, image paste, the attachment kinds it reads itself), terminal keys the CLI reserves, instructions file and import line, setup notes.
- **Adapter** (`src/main/providers/<id>/`, `ProviderAdapter`): locate the CLI and check readiness (login and which one with its account, `AgentInstallInfo.authMethod`/`account`; sandbox; short `notes` Agent Setup shows), setup tasks, launch (`prepareLaunch`, `buildCommand`), hook normalisation and file-lock replies, live details (status line or transcript tail), mode-switch keys, transcripts (path, restore, list, usage, conversation parser, images, export), memory sources, allowed files and skill folders, the project's own MCP servers.

**Models and capabilities from the CLIs** (#125). The CLI is the source of truth wherever it can say; where it can't, the lists are manual (never scraped or fetched) and every one is editable in Settings, so nobody waits for a Hive release.
- **Where they come from.** An adapter's `listModels` asks the installed CLI for its models and what each can do, at start and on every refresh (Check now, a changed CLI path), with a timeout, never blocking start. Claude Code: the Agent SDK's `initialize` control request (`claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --no-session-persistence --strict-mcp-config` in a folder of Hive's own, in the sessions' environment), which sends no prompt and uses no tokens; the CLI is closed as soon as it answers. Only `models` (and `unavailable_models`) is read: `account` and the reply's other personal fields are never read, logged or stored. Each model gives its value, resolved id, name, `supportedEffortLevels` and `supportsAutoMode`; the pickers (and the Agent API's model list) show an alias with the model it stands for, "Opus (Opus 5.5)" (#248), a pinned version as the CLI names it; the `default` entry isn't offered (it is "Claude Code default") and its resolved model is the CLI's default model. Codex: `codex debug models` (hidden models left out; `supported_reasoning_levels`, `default_reasoning_level`). The replies are the CLIs' protocols, not documented commands, so they are read defensively: unknown fields ignored, a missing or odd field falls back for that field, a reply without models is a failure (logged without personal data). Per-model flags are trusted only when the reply uses them at all: then a Claude model without effort levels has none (Haiku), and one without `supportsAutoMode` no Auto. Contract tests run against recorded replies in `tests/fixtures` (personal fields removed), which the fake CLIs answer with too.
- **Cache.** The last good catalog per provider is kept in config.json (`modelCatalogs`), keyed by the CLI version, so pickers fill at once on start while the fresh read runs ("as it last said; asking again"); a different version's is not used. Only the latest refresh's answer is kept.
- **Pickers.** Models: the CLI's (unavailable ones shown, not offered), else the fallback. Effort pickers (global, project, agent, Assistant) offer the levels of the model that would run (`models.ts` `modelCaps`); without the CLI's word, the effort fallback. A saved effort the model doesn't take stays chosen and is shown as such ("Extra high (not offered with Opus 4.6)", with a warning), never changed behind the user's back. A model without effort (Haiku) says so.
- **Default effort.** The footer and the pickers' Inherit option show the model's own default when nothing is set or reported: "Medium (default)". It is the CLI's config (Codex's `model_reasoning_effort`), else its catalog (Codex's `default_reasoning_level`), else the effort a session started without one reported for that model (Claude Code's status line; `observedDefaultEffort`, the newest 50 models per provider). Unknown: nothing shown, as before.
- **Fallbacks in Settings → each provider:** **Models (fallback)**, used only when the CLI can't be asked (not installed, too old, an unexpected reply), with where the models come from now ("From Claude Code 2.1.289", "Fallback (Claude Code couldn't be asked)"); **Effort levels (fallback)**, used when the CLI doesn't report a model's own (its names also name the CLI's levels, in the pickers and the agent's footer alike); and the prices (§9), which list the CLI's models too (#436: a listed model without a price is a row marked "No price: enter one"; a priced one the CLI no longer lists stays, greyed, "not in <provider>'s list"; a provider whose cost is only estimated (no `reportsCost`) says so, with a link to its `pricingUrl`). Each can be edited (add, remove, rename; models marked Older) and has **Reset to defaults**. The lists are folded while the CLI's answer is in use and they are Hive's own. Defaults stay in code (the descriptor's `modelGroups` and `effortLevels`, `SHIPPED_PRICES`); only an edited list is stored (`modelFallback`, `effortFallback`), so improved shipped defaults reach users who never edited them.

The registry (`src/main/providers/index.ts`) lists the adapters; the rest of Hive (sessions, Overview, settings pages, status bar, Agent Setup, Sessions tab) works from descriptors and adapters without naming a provider. Adding a provider means a descriptor, an adapter, an icon and a price table. Skills and MCP are stored in a Hive-neutral form; adapters translate them at launch.

---

## 11. Provider installation and updates

The same rules apply to every provider's CLI; the details below are Claude Code's. Codex: installed with OpenAI's installer (`irm https://chatgpt.com/codex/install.ps1 | iex`) into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\OpenAI\Codex`, signed in with a ChatGPT plan or an API key, plus the one-time sandbox setup (§6.4). **Agent Setup** (Help menu) has a tab per provider with its install, sign-in, sandbox and update steps, and the readiness banner links to it for enabled providers that need something.

**A fresh PATH** (#472, `freshPath.ts`): installers add their folder to the user's or the machine's PATH in the registry, which running processes don't see. Before Hive looks for the CLIs (`providerService.refresh`: at start, after an install, update, sign-in or setup task, Check again, a failed launch) and before Check again's git check, it reads the machine's then the user's PATH from the registry (PowerShell's `[Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable`, expanded; `reg query` where PowerShell can't run) and appends the folders its own PATH lacks, in that order: its own keep their order and come first, nothing is removed, one read at a time. `where.exe`, the CLIs' checks and every session started from then on (`childEnv` copies Hive's environment) get it; running sessions keep theirs. **Before Hive starts a process of its own** (#821) it reads it again (`refreshPathBeforeStart`): an agent's launch (`sessions.start`, before a worktree's setup command too), a provider task's terminal (install, update, sign-in, setup) and **Open External Terminal Here** (`wt.exe`, else `cmd`), so what was installed outside Hive since it started reaches them with no Check again. That wait is bounded (2 s, `FRESH_WAIT_MS`): a slower read lets the start go on with Hive's PATH as it is and counts for the next one; launches at the same time share one read. A Windows Terminal already running opens the new window in its own process, with the environment it has (or reloads from Windows, its *reloadEnvironmentVariables* setting): Hive can't give that one its PATH. No provider is named: every adapter's `PATH` candidate gains it. After an install task, Agent Setup says the CLI was found and needs no restart (agents already running get the new PATH when they restart), or, when it still isn't found, that the install ended without Hive finding it (Check again, or set its path in Settings). Test copies (unpackaged, with a test profile) never read the registry: `HIVE_TEST_REGISTRY_PATH` stands in for it (none by default; the `freshpath` suite changes it while Hive runs, as an installer would).

**GitHub Copilot** (`copilot`, #403 spike on CLI 1.0.93; readiness #452, `providers/copilot/install.ts`, which the adapter delegates `locate`, `readiness` and the install, update and sign-in tasks to):
- **Installed** with WinGet (`winget install --id GitHub.Copilot --exact --source winget`, the **Install** task; WinGet links `copilot.exe` in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Links`) or npm (`@github/copilot`, a `.cmd` launcher). Discovery: settings path → `PATH` → WinGet's Links → npm global. The version is `copilot --version` (its "GitHub Copilot CLI 1.0.93." without the full stop), read with `COPILOT_AUTO_UPDATE=false` as its agents run: the installed executable, never a version `copilot update` downloaded into its home. So **Update** goes through the installer it came from (`winget upgrade …`, `npm install -g @github/copilot@latest`); any other install gets no Update button, only a note to update it the way it was installed. The latest version is npm's.
- **Signed in, and how**, in the CLI's own order (`copilot login --help`), for the environment its agents get (Hive's, without `GH_TOKEN` and `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which other tools set and Copilot launches leave out, `COPILOT_ENV_STRIP`; `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN`, set for Copilot alone, is kept, #450): a token variable (`COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN`; the other two only outside Hive), else a **stored login** (`copilot login`: the token is in Windows Credential Manager, keyed by account, and `loggedInUsers`/`lastLoggedInUser` in the CLI's `config.json`, under `COPILOT_HOME` else `~/.copilot`, name it), else the **GitHub CLI's** login (`gh auth status --active --hostname github.com --json hosts`: its account and `state`). Hive only checks that a token variable is set, reads only the login names from `config.json` (a JSON file after a `//` comment line), and never asks gh for the token. `authMethod` is the variable's name, "Copilot login" or "GitHub CLI", and `account` the login (a token's isn't known; an Enterprise host follows it in brackets). Agent Setup's chip: **Signed in as octocat (Copilot login)**. Signed out: **Sign in** runs `copilot login` in a visible terminal that the user signs in with (Hive types nothing; tests never run it), and the detail names `copilot login --device-code`, the gh fallback (`gh auth login`), and any token variable set in Hive's environment that agents don't get.
- **Plan note** (`notes`): "Copilot Free uses Auto only; choosing a model needs a paid plan." (the CLI can't say an account's plan). Usage is in AI credits (1 AIC = $0.01). Copilot Pro's "third-party agents (Claude Code and Codex)" are GitHub's cloud agents (Agent HQ), not the local CLIs: a Copilot plan doesn't sign in Hive's Claude Code or Codex.
- **Tested with** (#365) works as for the others: Copilot is in `REAL_CLIS` with its real suite (`copilot`, the installed CLI offline against a stand-in model, #453), and its `tested-clis.json` entry comes from the release's real-tier run (`npm run tested-clis`), first in 0.4.2. Until a release names it, Agent Setup shows no Tested line for Copilot.
- **Agents** (#450; how in ARCHITECTURE, GitHub Copilot): turned on in Settings → Providers like the others. Launched with a session id Hive picks (`--session-id`, `--resume`), a launch plugin of HTTP hooks (statuses, the file-lock reply), the workspace's MCP servers and the hive server with its instructions (the user's and the project's own servers and Copilot's built-in ones turned off), and Hive's skills as the `.agents/skills` copies it shares with Codex; Hive never writes Copilot's config. Modes: **Accept edits** (the default, #468: Ask with writes under the agent's folder approved, `--allow-tool=write(<folder>/**)`; where the folder's path has `(` or `)` it approves no edits, since Copilot refuses parentheses in its rules and any stand-in would also match a look-alike folder, and Hive says so once when such an agent starts), **Ask**, **Plan**, **Allow all** (`--allow-all`; no opt-in since #490, Darren's decision: Copilot needs one low-interaction mode, and its description says plainly that it approves every tool, path and web request, in its folder and outside it), and the danger mode **Autopilot**, unlocked by the opt-in, which names the danger modes it unlocks (**Enable the Autopilot option**, from the descriptor's list, #461; turning it off reverts projects and agents using it to Inherit). Autopilot is Allow all and more: on its first turn Copilot 1.0.93 sets *Permission policy: Allow All for this session* (the footer turns to Autopilot · Allow All), whether launched with `--autopilot` or switched to live, and leaving it keeps Allow All (Interactive · Allow All, read as Allow all), so after it Ask and Accept edits need a restart (#490's probes, offline in a test home); the footer says which (Accept edits and Ask look the same there, so the launch decides), Shift+Tab cycles to Plan and Autopilot and back live, switching between Accept edits, Ask and Allow all needs a restart. The hive server's tools never ask, in any mode (`--allow-tool=hive`; the Assistant's only those its Control settings trust), as for the other CLIs: the Agent API enforces what an agent may do. Its folder-trust dialog is the startup question. It reads the project's `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.github/copilot-instructions.md`, and runs a project's `.claude/settings.json` hooks. Sessions, the conversation and usage come from `COPILOT_HOME/session-state/<id>` (#451); cost is Copilot's own figure in AI credits. Copilot writes a session's token totals (`session.shutdown`) only as it exits by itself, so Hive ends it as a person would (#465): Stop, closing its workspace, quitting Hive and a restart type Ctrl+C three times, 400 ms apart (cancelling a turn or a dialog, then exiting), and kill it only if it is still running 10 s later (#716: under load it took up to 2.4 s to exit by itself, and 2.5 s killed it, losing its totals; the grace is Settings → Advanced's `copilot.exit.graceSeconds`, #727). Quitting, closing a workspace and removing a project wait past that grace for it to end. Models: Copilot's ACP session (`listModels`, only while Copilot is on); Free has Auto only.

**Tested CLI versions** (#365): each release ships `resources/tested-clis.json`, the version of each provider's CLI its real e2e tier ran against: `{ "<provider id>": { "version", "testedAt", "record" } }`, `record` being the run record's code. It is never typed by hand, and the versions are what Hive itself selected: each suite's test copies of Hive note the CLI their detection chose (the standalone one, never an editor extension's copy), its path and version, in a log the runner gives the suite (`HIVE_TEST_CLI_LOG`, carried into the test Hive; `noteSelectedCli` in `testedClis.ts`, unpackaged test copies only), in the CLI homes the suite gave it. The runner starts no CLI of its own; it keeps each suite's CLIs in the run record (a "Real CLIs (as Hive selected them)" line, and per suite in `run-record.json` beside `run-record.md`), and `npm run tested-clis` (`scripts/testedClis.mjs`) replaces a provider's entry only from a valid record in which every real suite of that CLI ran, passed and says which version its Hive used, all the same one. Between releases it may lack Claude Code's entry (its real suites run in the default home until #368, so only the release's run writes it); `npm run release` refuses a manifest with an entry missing, from uncommitted changes, or from a commit that differs from HEAD in anything but the manifest (RELEASING.md). Main reads it on each provider check (`testedClis.ts`; `HIVE_TEST_TESTED_CLIS` replaces it in unpackaged builds, for tests) and compares the installed version by the adapter's `isNewer`: `AgentInstallInfo.tested` (`same`, `newer`, `older`, `unknown` when the CLI gave no version), null when the release names none. **Agent Setup** shows, under the CLI found, "Tested with X · installed Y": a tick when the same; newer: "This version came out after this Hive release was tested. Most updates work; if something behaves oddly, report it (Copy Diagnostics)."; older: "Older than tested; consider updating."; unknown: that Hive couldn't tell (`shared/testedClis.ts`). Nothing is blocked. **Copy Diagnostics** lists both versions per CLI, and the Agent API's `/v1/providers` (`hive_list_providers`, the Assistant's) gives `tested: { version, installed }`.

- Not bundled (proprietary; redistribution would need Anthropic's permission).
- **The standalone CLI is required** (Claude Code and Codex alike). Copies bundled with editor extensions (VS Code, Cursor, Windsurf) are never used, even if set manually: they move on every extension update and can't be updated with `claude update`. If only an extension is present, the setup dialog explains that the CLI is still needed.
- Discovery: settings path → `PATH` → `~/.local/bin/claude.exe` → npm global.
- **`.cmd` launchers** (npm's `codex.cmd`, say) aren't run through cmd.exe when Hive can tell what they run: a Node CLI's launcher that does nothing else, matched whole (npm's cmd-shim template, with its `node.exe` beside it or `node` on PATH, or the bare one-line `@node "%~dp0cli.js" %*`), is started as that node and script directly; one that sets anything, adds its own arguments or Node flags, or runs another interpreter is not, since a direct start would leave that out (`shimTarget` in `providers/common.ts`), so its command line can be as long as an .exe's (32,767 characters) and the first task and session name are passed as they are. cmd.exe takes at most 8,191 characters, less than the Hive Assistant's Codex launch (its hooks, instructions and hive server, about 9.5K). Any other `.cmd`/`.bat` still goes through cmd.exe, with its first task and name flattened to one plain line, and a launch too long for it is refused before it starts, saying to point Settings at the CLI's `.exe`.
- Not found → first-run dialog offers a one-click install using Anthropic's official installer, run in a visible terminal.
- On launch (if enabled): read `claude --version`, compare with the latest published version, offer **Update** (`claude update`) in a visible terminal. Never updates while sessions are live.
- Auth is handled by each CLI itself. Hive never touches credentials, and never edits a CLI's own config files.

### 11.1 Hive's own updates

- **electron-updater** against GitHub Releases of `Suremise/hive-desktop` (`publish:` in `electron-builder.yml`). It reads `latest.yml`, downloads the NSIS installer (differentially when a `.blockmap` is available) and checks its SHA-512 before installing. Only published releases of a public repository are seen (drafts are not); no token ships with the app. With no published release, checks report "No release of Hive has been published yet". 0.1.0 (29 Sep 2026) was the first published release.
- **Settings → Updates**: *Check for updates automatically* (on: 30 s after start, then every 6 hours), *Download updates automatically* (on), *Install updates* — **Automatically, when Hive quits** (default) or **Manually, with Restart and Update** — and *Include pre-releases* (off). Hive never installs while it is running: sessions are never cut off by an update.
- **Restart and Update** goes through the normal quit flow (the quit dialog when agents are working, including *wait for them*), then installs silently and reopens Hive. Cancelling the quit cancels the update.
- **Where it shows**: the status bar's version item becomes *Hive Desktop X available*, *Downloading Hive Desktop X… N%* or *Restart to update to X* (click: update dialog); a notification when a download is ready; the update dialog (version, date, size, progress, release notes from the GitHub release, **Download**, **Skip This Version**, **Later**, **Restart and Update**, **On GitHub**); **Help → Check for Updates…** (always shows the result: up to date, available, or the error); the About panel and Settings → Updates (status and **Check Now**); the tray menu (*Restart to Update*). After an update, a notification links to the release notes.
- **Skip This Version** stops automatic checks offering that version; a manual check still shows it.
- Development builds never check (the status bar says so); tests point `HIVE_UPDATE_FEED` at a local feed, with their own download cache, and never run what they download.
- **Install folder permissions** (#431): Electron refuses to start (a deliberate crash, nothing on screen) when its sandbox can't read its folder: a DACL with an entry for an AppContainer package SID but none for ALL APPLICATION PACKAGES (`S-1-15-2-1`). The installer (`build/installer.nsh`, `customInstall`, run by updates too) grants `*S-1-15-2-1:(OI)(CI)(RX)` on `$INSTDIR`, inherited by what it holds. At start, before Electron's check (which runs after the main script's first synchronous pass), `installDir.ts` reads the SDDL (`icacls /save`, ~20 ms) of the file Electron checks (`icudtl.dat`; the folder's if it isn't there) and, in that condition, grants the entry the same way, then reads it again; if that file doesn't inherit it from the folder, it also gets `*S-1-15-2-1:(RX)` of its own (that one file, read only). If it still lacks it, or can't be read again, Hive tells the user and quits: a PowerShell Windows Forms window (Electron's other dialogs need `ready`) naming the folder, or the checked file when that is what still lacks the entry, with the PowerShell command (each argument a single-quoted literal: the folder grant, then the file's), **Copy Command** and **Close**; if PowerShell can't show it, Electron's `dialog.showErrorBox` (which works before `ready`) with the same text. Permissions it can't read at all leave the start as it was. Only the installed (packaged) app changes its folder: a development or test copy runs from `node_modules`' Electron, which it never changes (it tells the user instead), and tests point it at a test folder of their own (`HIVE_TEST_INSTALL_DIR`). The sandbox is never turned off.
- **The name** (#443): the app is displayed as **Hive Desktop** (`productName`; `shared/appName.ts`): the window title and title bar ("Hive Desktop Dev" for dev builds, also their Windows app ID's name), About, the status bar's version and the update items and notifications that name a version, the tray tooltip, native dialog titles, the installer, the Start menu and desktop shortcuts (`shortcutName`), Installed apps (`Hive Desktop <version>`) and the GitHub release title. Sentences and labels where the short name reads naturally say **Hive** ("Hive couldn't…", Quit Hive, Hive Assistant, Hive skills), and so does everything agents and data see: the `hive` MCP server and tools, the Agent API's `app`, `TERM_PROGRAM`, card history's author, metrics exports. Nothing stored moves: the app ID `com.hive.desktop` (taskbar pins, notification settings, the uninstall entry), the profile `%APPDATA%\Hive` (set by main, since Electron would take `productName`), the install folder `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Hive` and `Hive.exe` (from `executableName`), the installer's file name `Hive-Setup-<version>.exe` and the update feed. An update renames the old shortcuts (electron-builder's installer keeps them under the new name). `tests/appName.test.ts` pins the folders.
- Releasing: `npm run release` uploads a draft release (installer, `latest.yml`, blockmap) with the `gh` login; it is published by hand on GitHub. See `RELEASING.md`.

---

## 12. Agent API

Local HTTP API on `127.0.0.1:<port>` (default 47821), bearer-token auth. The workspace token is in `%APPDATA%/Hive/agent-api.json` (Settings → Agent API) for scripts; each agent launch gets a token of its own instead (`agent-api/<hash>.json` in the profile, passed as `HIVE_API_TOKEN`, ended when the agent stops), from which the API knows the calling agent and its project, and the Assistant has its own per launch. Errors use 400 (bad input), 401, 403 (not allowed, e.g. an agent asking for another project's cards), 404, 409 (conflicts with the current state) and 500 (unexpected). Endpoints under `/v1` for app status (with the API's version, who the caller is to Hive and the guidance and skill revisions), workspace, projects, sessions, usage, shared notes, the task board, skills (a lean listing; one Hive skill's files, paths checked whole and inside its folder), MCP, notifications, progress (long runs for the Progress panel, §13), a server-sent events stream and `/v1/docs/agent-api` (the running version's own reference). A project agent lists only its own project's skills; a project's listing leaves out the Hive skills for the Assistant alone, as its Skills tab does. An agent's activity is clipped (prompt and reply to 300 and 500 characters, the last 3 tool calls; with detail 2,000, 3,000 and 10) and says what it cut (`clipped`, `toolCalls`). Full reference: `docs/AGENT_API.md`.

**Apps that show Hive** (#620, for Apiary; API contract version 3). Three parts, all read-only:

- **Viewer tokens**: made and revoked by the user in Settings → Agent API → Viewer tokens, each named for its app (unique names, at most 20), kept in `%APPDATA%/Hive/agent-api-viewers.json` beside the workspace token (`viewerTokens.ts`). Scope: every open workspace, as the workspace token sees it; read-only. A viewer's every request but a GET is refused (403) before its body is read or its route looked up, so no write (nor a wait, nor a progress report) is reached; it gets the event stream like the workspace token. `GET /v1/status` gives its caller as `role: viewer`. They work while the Agent API is on. Revoking one refuses its next request and ends its open streams. Its requests are counted under their own role (`viewer`, Performance's "Viewer apps").
- **The discovery file**: `agent-api-discovery.json` in Hive's profile (`apiDiscovery.ts`): `{ app, version, api, url, port, pid, startedAt }`, **never a token**. Written whenever the Agent API starts listening, removed when it is off or fails to listen, and on quit (in the quit flow, and synchronously at `will-quit` for the other ways out), only if this process wrote it. A crash leaves it naming a gone `pid`: clients check `/v1/health`.
- **The event stream's events** (`apiEvents.ts`, unit tested): besides the session, workspace, notes, skills, board and lane events, `progress-changed` (the runs, as `GET /v1/progress`), `merge-slots-changed`, `notice` and `notice-resolved`, `assistant-activity` and `agent-added`. **Nothing private, to any token** (the workspace token's stream included): every payload is built from an allowlist of fields, never passed through, so a field added to an event later stays inside Hive until it is listed. An agent's state (`publicSessionState`) goes without its status message (the CLI's words), a question's, review's or sign-in's text, the line Hive typed and it hasn't taken, and what queued messages say (only who from and when); a session's exit without its failure's output (`failedToStart` only); a notice without its body (an agent's last reply, its question); an Assistant action without what it quotes (`AssistantAction.brief`: the line without the prompt or message it gave, or a setting's values). **An Assistant action's fields** (#763, Darren 14 Oct: Apiary walks the Assistant around the office and mustn't parse English that i18n will translate): every action, done or refused, carries a `kind` from a fixed set (`ACTIVITY_KINDS` in `shared/assistantActivity.ts`: `start-task`, `prompt-agent`, `message-agent`, `cancel-messages`, `start-agent`, `stop-agent`, `add-agent`, `update-agent`, `hand-over`, `create-task`, `update-task`, `update-tasks`, `reorder-tasks`, `comment-task`, `lane-start`, `lane-join`, `lane-end`, `create-project`, `activate-project`, `deactivate-project`, `setting`, `revert-setting`) and its targets where they apply (`project` by name, `agent` and `toAgent` as `{ id, name }`, `card` or `cards`, `lane`, `column` after a move, `settingId`, never a value), in the panel's list and through `activityFields`' allow-list on the stream; a refusal is `ok: false` with the kind it tried. A refusal's `error` on the stream is Hive's own short reason (`AssistantAction.reason`: fixed words for the boundary's refusals, else `publicReason` of the status the API answered with), never the error's text, which can quote what was sent (a setting's value, a prompt); the panel keeps the whole message. The stream's `text` is never an error's either: a change whose line carries one gives a `brief` in Hive's own words (an agent added whose start failed: "Added B2 to hive; it didn't start", without the reuse note, git's words), and request values in a refusal's line are the names Hive resolved (the agent a card was to start on), and lane ids, columns, projects and setting ids are sent only when they are one (`activityFields`). Targets Hive knows before the change are named on a refusal too: the existing agent a card is started on or given (`activityTargets.knownAgent`, which never throws), a lane's builder or joining reviewer; a card's action names the agent it is with afterwards (`cardAgent`), a batch per card. The record functions (`record`, `admit`, `refuseUnlessAllowed`, `assistantChange`) take the act as a required argument, so no change is recorded without a kind; targets known only afterwards (a new card's number, a new agent) come back with the change's line. A batch records each card with `update-tasks`. `text` stays for people. `api.version` 5. The Assistant's waits and notifications aren't actions (nothing is listed), so they have no kind. A notice is sent whenever Hive tells the user, as a banner or a Windows notification (`tellListeners`, no window, marked `windows`), not when notifications are off. A project agent's token gets only its project's sessions, notices, runs, slots and added agents, and no Assistant actions.
- **`GET /v1/progress`**: the Progress panel's runs (`progress:list`) in their public form (`publicRun`, shared with `progress-changed`): who, what title, how far, how long, its state, whether it waits; never the command line (arguments can hold a token or a prompt), the step's name, what it waits for, the summary or the log's path. **Titles (Darren's rule, 11 Oct):** a run's title leaves Hive only when it was set on purpose (`hive-progress --title`, a reporter's or script's own title, Hive's own runners'). A title generated from a command line never does: such a run is `"a command"` with `titleFromCommand: true` and its structured fields (source, step/total, time left, state); no command line is trimmed or redacted to make a label. The wrapper says the title is its command line when it starts the run (`titleFromCommand`, kept on the run and in Recent's history), since title and command are cut to different lengths (120 and 200) and can't be compared whole; a run without the flag (from before) whose title begins as its command does counts too. A project agent's own project's runs only.

Not yet: the terminal screen route and jumping to an agent's pane (with Apiary v2).

---

## 13. UI

- **Title bar** with menu bar (File, Edit, View, Project, Session, Help), the Always on Top pin, native window controls.
- **Activity bar**: Projects, Task Board, Shared Notes, Skills, Templates (#127), MCP Servers, Hive Assistant; Docs and Settings at the bottom.
- **Sidebar** per activity: project list (active toggle, status dot, warning for bypass, context menu), notes tree, skill/MCP lists with toggles.
- **Compact project list**: the Projects sidebar collapses to a 48 px rail — one tile per project with its initials ("web-dashboard" → WD, "hive" → Hi) and its combined status dot, working-on projects first, then a divider and the others. Hover shows the name, status, branch and each agent's state; click selects, double-click starts a session, right-click gives the usual project menu. Toggle it with the chevron in the list header or on the rail, **Ctrl+Alt+B**, or by dragging the sidebar edge below 120 px (dragging out again restores the earlier width). Only the Projects view compacts; Notes, Skills and MCP always show at full width. Kept in the app config (`ui.sidebarCompact`). Ctrl+B still hides the sidebar entirely.
- **Context percentage**: the window is only ever what the CLI reports for the session (Claude Code's status line, Codex's rollout), never taken from a model table, since Claude Code's 1M window depends on the model and the plan; until it's known, tokens only (`contextPercent()` in `shared/defaults.ts`, also used by the Overview's running agents). **The context figure** (`SessionUsage.contextTokens`) is the last request's input (cache included) plus its output (thinking included), which stays in the context: what the CLI compacts on (#154; before, Claude Code's showed the input only, so a turn adding 70–90K of thinking made a compaction look early). `contextInputTokens` and `lastOutputTokens` keep the two parts (absent from usage read by older versions); right after a compaction the figure is what it left. The footer's tooltip and the Overview's Context card give the parts, and where the CLI compacts by itself: `autoCompactAt()` from the descriptor's `autoCompactReserve` (Claude Code: 33K below the window, 20K kept for output and a 13K buffer: about 167K of 200K, 967K of 1M; Codex: not known, not shown), an estimate labelled as the default (#242, `autoCompactLine()`). At launch the adapter's `autoCompact()` reads how the CLI was set to compact by itself into `LiveSessionState.autoCompact` ({ window: tokens | 'off' | null, percent?, source }), from what Hive passes and the CLI's settings files, never changing them (Claude Code, `providers/claude/autoCompact.ts`, as code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config and env-vars say: `DISABLE_COMPACT` or `DISABLE_AUTO_COMPACT` on (`1`, `true`, `yes`, `on`, any case) turns it off over everything; else `autoCompactEnabled: false` in the highest settings scope that sets it turns it off (not when `DISABLE_AUTO_COMPACT` is set off, which overrides the setting); else `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` (a plain count), else `--autocompact` in the agent's arguments ("auto": the tuned default), else the highest settings scope with a window (managed, the command line's (the last `--settings` in the agent's own arguments: a file relative to its folder, or inline JSON), the folder's `.claude/settings.local.json`, `.claude/settings.json`, the config folder's `settings.json`), its `modelSettings.<model>.autoCompactWindow` for the model the session runs as before its top-level `autoCompactWindow`; windows clamp to 100K–1M; `CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE` only brings it earlier). **Only the files Claude Code reads** (#333, checked with 2.1.292 in a test home, a SessionStart hook in each file under `--init-only`): managed settings and the last `--settings` always; of the user's, the project's and the local file, those the last `--setting-sources` names (either form, a comma-separated list of `user`, `project`, `local`, names trimmed, an empty list none), or none with `--restricted`, whatever `--setting-sources` says (`settingSources()`). **Files are read within Claude Code's bounds**: a stat first, then only a regular file of at most 2 MiB, no further than the limit (Claude Code refuses a larger `--settings`, or a folder), so a device name, a pipe or a huge file never holds up the main process. **What Hive can't determine makes the answer an estimate**, said with any answer (a window, off, or the default): a file it couldn't read (not a regular file, over the limit, an error reading it), a `--setting-sources` Claude Code refuses (another name, an empty entry, no value: it doesn't start), and `--safe-mode` or `--bare`, which may stop Claude Code reading some settings files (`SettingsScope.unsure`). It is read again when the status line reports another model, for that model alone (until then the choice's resolved id, then the alias, in that order; within a model its exact name before other spellings of it). The variables are read from the environment Claude Code ends up with (#273): the launch's, with each scope's `env` block over it, the higher scope winning (checked with Claude Code 2.1.291 in a test home: a settings `env` beats the launch environment, the project's the user's, a `--settings` the project's, and of several `--settings` only the last is read; the source then says where, e.g. "CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW (env in Claude Code's settings.json)"). The tooltip then says the default for the window, the window that was set and where (capped at the model's window), or that auto-compaction is off; for a session not running, "by default (its settings can change this)". A default is labelled an estimate (`AutoCompactSetting.estimate`): policies Hive can't read, Windows registry policies or an organisation's server-managed settings, could change it. Each compaction records the last request before it (`lastInputTokens`, `lastOutputTokens`); the compaction history's **Last turn** column shows that output, and says "turn added 72.4k output" (hover: from what to what) when it is 20K or more and at least half the jump from the input (`turnPushedCompaction()`). Its tooltip gives the tokens, the window and the compactions, then, after an empty line, "Click to view compaction history" (a click opens the session in the Overview; not on the Assistant's footer, where a click opens nothing).
- **The project header's actions** (#216): **Active** · **Resume Agent / Resume All Agents (n)** (while a stopped agent has a session to resume, or a running one an expired sign-in stopped, which it tells to carry on, #309; "Resume (n)" under 860 px) · **Stop Agent / Stop (n)** (while any runs; it lists them and asks) · **Start New / Start New (n)** · **Archive and Start New / Archive and Start New (n)** · **Remove Agent / Remove All (n)** (#291, below) · **⋯** (**Explorer**, **Terminal**, **Project Settings**, **Remove Project…**). **The counts** (#275), with more than one agent (Resume and Stop: more than one to act on), are exactly the agents the action acts on, the ones its question lists (`batchCounts()` in `shared/startAll.ts`): Stop the running agents, Resume the stopped ones with a conversation to resume, Start New every agent, Archive and Start New those with a session to archive; they follow agents as they start and stop, the tooltips say it in full ("Stop the 3 running agents"), and the Session menu and palette entries carry the same count ("Start New (6)…", "Archive and Start New (5)…"; the latter only while some agent has a session to archive). As the header narrows, its actions give way in steps (each keeps its name, count included, in the tooltip and for screen readers, and its count beside its icon): under 860 px the two new ones are icons; under 640 px Resume, Stop and the Active switch are icons too and Remove All moves to the top of ⋯ and the status drops "· n of m agents"; under 460 px the two new ones move to the top of ⋯. The title shrinks (its name cut with "…") before any action does, and a header narrower than even its icons (a main area squeezed by both side panels) wraps them onto more rows, so every action stays inside the header and clickable at Hive's narrowest window. **Start New (All)** asks once, listing every agent with what it is doing and, for a running one, what stopping it costs, as the quit dialog says it (will be interrupted, background tasks will stop, waiting for you, its card loop pauses); confirmed, running agents are stopped and every agent starts a fresh session (their old conversations stay resumable from Sessions). **Archive and Start New (All)** (shown while some agent has a session) asks once, listing every agent that has a session to archive (a running agent's current one, else the one its Resume would open — its last one, if not archived yet — as the agent's own **Archive and Start New…** does); confirmed, those are stopped, their sessions archived (`.hive/archive`) and each starts a fresh one. Both go one agent at a time (`shared/startAll.ts`), report the agents that couldn't be archived or started in one notice with the reasons, and are in the **Session** menu and the command palette (`session.startNewAll`, `session.archiveAll`); Cancel changes nothing. The title bar's **Project** menu lists every tab, in the strip's order, from the same list (`shared/projectTabs.ts`), besides Explorer and Terminal.
- **Action colours** (#344): each session action has one colour wherever it appears, in both themes: **Stop** red, **Remove** violet (Remove Agent, Remove All; apart from Stop's red), **Start New / New Session** green, **Archive and Start New** teal, **Resume** the accent amber. They are tokens in `app.css` (`--act-<action>` for the hue, `--act-<action>-fg` for the label and icon), worn as `btn act-<action>`: a light wash of the hue for the header's and panes' buttons (and their narrow icon forms), filled (`solid`) where the action is the main choice (an empty pane's New Session, the ended bar's Resume, the Assistant's Start) and on the confirm button of the question that asks before it (Stop, Remove, Start New, Archive and Start New: `ConfirmRequest.action`); menu entries (`MenuEntry.action`) colour their icon, and hovered or chosen from the keyboard take the action's wash instead of the accent; the narrow forms' counts are filled. A disabled action isn't faded: no wash, a dashed edge in its hue and a muted label that stays readable. A question's choices take an action too (`ChoiceRequest` choices: the expired-cache Resume or Archive and Start Fresh), and the stop-first question before New Session, Resume or Remove takes the colour of what follows. Every label (counts, hovered menu entries and disabled labels included) has WCAG AA contrast (4.5:1), resting and hovered, on every surface, checked by `tests/actionColours.test.ts`; focus is a 2px ring in the action's colour; labels and icons stay, so colour is never the only cue. Delete and Discard (work that's lost) keep the danger red. The command palette and title-bar menus have no icons, so no colours. **The primary button and the tinted Add Agent** (#360) meet AA too, resting and hovered: `.btn.primary` fills with `--accent-fill` (dark: the accent with its dark label, 8.0:1; light: the accent's amber darkened only as far as AA needs for the white label, #b16105, 4.6:1, hovered `--accent-strong` 5.0:1), and `.btn.tint-amber` (Add Agent in an empty project) takes the tinted Resume's label, `--act-resume-fg`, on its wash (the same tests). **Menus** (#362): an entry hovered or chosen from the keyboard, and the command palette's chosen row, fill with the same `--accent-fill`; the label, icon and shortcut are `--accent-fg`, and its second line, a greyed (`muted`) entry's label and Recent's ✕ take the label's colour, so every part reads at AA on the fill in both themes (before, a second line was 1.6:1 in light and 1.3:1 in dark). A session action's entry keeps its own wash (#344). **The status bar and count badges** (#384): the light bar is `--accent-fill` with its white label (4.6:1), hovered and its standing-out items (caution, update) a darker wash; the dark bar keeps the accent with its dark label, its standing-out items a lighter wash (a darker one brought the dark label to 3.3:1): `--status-hover`, `--status-caution`, `--status-raised`. A secondary part ("5h 55%") is no longer faded (it fell to 4.4:1 in dark, 2.6:1 in light). The update-ready chip's label is `--act-resume-fg` on its light chip; a warning item stays white on red (6.5:1). The activity bar's and a project's count badges fill with `--accent-fill`. The `contrast` e2e suite measures them all as drawn, and `tests/actionColours.test.ts` the tokens.
- **Text and icon contrast** (#421): the text greys and the accent as text reach WCAG AA (4.5:1) on every background text sits on, in both themes: the editor, the sidebar, dialogs, inputs, hovered and selected rows. `--fg-muted` (light #555555, dark #b0b0b0) and `--fg-faint` (light #676767, dark #a0a0a0) keep their order under `--fg`. The accent as text or an icon on those backgrounds, and on its own tint over them (`--accent-soft`: a badge, an active button), is `--accent-ink` (light #8f4605, dark #f8ad30), and text *on* the amber fill stays `--accent-fg`. Decoration and disabled items keep the old faint grey, `--fg-decor` (a disabled menu entry, an empty view's large icon): no contrast is asked of them. Status dots reach 3:1 (icons) at every moment: the working dot is `--dot-accent` (light the stronger amber), and the background-tasks dot a solid dimmer amber, `--dot-background`, rather than the accent at 60% opacity; the busy dots' `pulse` changes their shade away from the background (`filter: brightness(var(--pulse-shade))`: 0.65 in light, 1.4 in dark) instead of fading them to 35%. The `contrast` e2e suite measures every pair from the theme's tokens, the tint and the pulse's height too, and fails if the pulse changes opacity.
- **Agent templates** (#126): a template is a project's agents and layout under a name: per agent its name, `role`, provider (as it runs now), model, effort, permission mode, 200K context and whether it has its own worktree, and the project's one `layout` (#134); never sessions, `lastSessionId`, worktree paths and branches, `needsSetup` or the Assistant's persona. A `version` (1) lets the format grow; one made by a newer Hive is refused, not half read. Kept as one JSON file per template (`writeKeptJson`/`readKeptJson`: a damaged one is recovered from its `.bak`) in `<workspace>/.hive/templates` (**workspace**: every project) or `<project>/.hive/templates` (**project**: private, the project's `.hive` is kept out of git); the file name comes from the name (`build-and-review.json`; another name with the same file name gets `-2`), and a name in both scopes is listed twice with its scope. Files are read as untrusted (`shared/templates.ts` `readTemplate`: 1–24 agents (#562; loading one checks it fits the project's Agents per project, in its plan and again under the lock as it publishes) with unique names, providers' ids, settings as CLIs name them; a template that can't be used is listed with why; a provider this Hive doesn't know, from a newer Hive, is kept and flagged, and loading it, or adding that agent, is refused like a provider that isn't installed). The agent strip has two template controls (#286): **Template ▾** (**Save Template…**: name, and a tick for the workspace scope, saving over a name asks to replace; a separator; then the templates to load, by scope) and Add Agent's **▾ → Add Agent from Template** (each usable template's agents under its name and scope; one agent, a name any agent of the workspace has numbered, "Builder 2", #702). Template ▾ is labelled from 720 px of strip, else its icon, the agent tabs giving way first. Menus (`ContextMenu`, every menu in Hive) work from the keyboard: ↑ ↓ Home End move, Enter chooses, → opens an entry that leads to another menu (Add Agent from Template), and a menu opened from the keyboard (Enter on its button, or the palette) starts on its first entry. The palette's **Load Template…** and **Add Agent from Template…** open those menus on the project's Session tab (`stripMenus.ts`); **Save Agents as Template…** saves directly. **Loading replaces every agent** after a confirmation listing who goes and who comes (#268, `shared/templateLoad.ts`, in a box that scrolls when long). **Its agents' names are chosen there** (#702, `TemplateLoad.tsx`, Darren's decision of 12 Oct): each created agent's name in the template (`templateNames`, a reference never changed by edits) beside a field for the name it gets; a name another project's agent has (the plan's `taken`; this project's agents don't count, as the load replaces them, so a template loads into its own project unchanged, reusing those agents' worktrees), one used twice or an empty one is marked, saying what it clashes with, and **Load template** stays greyed out (`dialogBlocked` in the store, which the confirm's button follows) until none is. No naming patterns or prefixes are filled in. The plan is asked for again with the names (`templates:plan`'s `names`) once typing pauses, as worktree reuse, branches and "replaces" follow the names; the load sends them (`templates:load`'s `names`), checked again, and against other projects holding the names lock just before it is published (a name taken while it staged refuses it and the staged worktrees go). In the dialog: a removed agent a created one of the same name (any case) replaces is marked so; each created agent shows its provider and its settings in short, model · effort · mode as its footer will, what it leaves to the project or Hive shown as what that resolves to, "(default)"; #511: as a table (`TemplatePlan.tsx` from `templateLoadView`, the dialog's `content`, wide): Agent (role under it, "new" when it replaces one of its name), Coding agent (a badge), Model, Effort, Mode ("(default)" muted), Where ("project folder", "own worktree hive/x", "reuses hive/x", the folder and base on hover), in the template's order, the Removed list above it with each removed agent's worktree note, the notes below; no sideways scrolling, the box scrolls down; `templateLoadDetail` keeps the same as text; a new worktree shows its branch and folder **in this project** (`templatePlan`'s `worktrees`, from `newWorktreePlace`, which adding the agent uses too: a branch of this project's repository and a folder under the workspace's worktree location and this project's name, numbered `-2` when taken, by the plan's earlier ones too), and its base; and, when there are new worktrees and the project has a worktree setup command, that each runs it before its agent first starts. A template holds only whether an agent has its own worktree, never a path or branch, so loading one made in another project never reuses or points at that project's worktrees. **A clean worktree of the same name is worked in again** (#289, `reusableWorktree` in `templates.ts`) rather than a new `-2`: the worktree of the agent of that name being replaced, else one Hive made for that name that git lists in this project's worktree location and no agent works in (`hive/<workspace>/<project>/<name>`, or `hive/<name>` from before #703, in its folder, or one numbered when it was made: an earlier load's, kept when its agent was replaced; of several clean ones the unnumbered one, else none). Only when git lists it on that branch and nothing in it is uncommitted (strict, as Remove Agent checks); its branch is left as it is (not brought up to the project's branch). Setup runs again only if it never ran there: an agent replaced before its first session, or removed then (Remove Agent or Remove All keeping its worktree, #319), leaves its worktree on the project's `setupPending` list (project.json, at most 50, taken off when an agent works in it again or it is removed), and the agent working in it again, or one given it by Add Agent → Existing worktree, runs the worktree setup command first; one whose setup ran doesn't. The dialog says "reuses its worktree on hive/claude in … (clean; its branch is left as it is)", and the removed agent's line that its worktree goes on with the new one; one that matches but can't be reused gets a new worktree, the dialog saying why ("new: hive/claude has 1 uncommitted file"). The load makes the plan afresh, so a worktree that changed after the dialog gets a new one; nothing is removed. **Add Agent from Template** does the same for the name it adds under, never a worktree an agent works in (checked again under the project file's lock), and says so in a notice. **The replaced agents' old worktrees** (those no created agent works in again) are each checked with `worktreeCheck` (#291's: git lists it on its branch, the branch fully merged into the repository's main branch, a squash merge counting, nothing uncommitted; strict). The dialog's line for each says whether it is merged and clean or why it is kept ("2 commits not merged into main"), and when any are, a tick box, **off by default**, "Also remove the old worktree(s) and their branches (merged into main and clean): …" (`ConfirmRequest.check`, whose answer reaches the dialog's action before it runs). Ticked, the load removes them with their branches only once it is published (a refused load removes nothing), and only a removed agent's worktree, listed in the dialog, that no agent works in by then, checked again at that moment (`worktreeCheck`: still merged into the main branch the user was shown, and clean; a main branch that moved on while the dialog was open doesn't keep a worktree that is still merged) and removed through `removeCheckedWorktree` with that branch: if either branch moved after that check, or the worktree has files written since it, it is kept. Each is reserved before it is checked (`reserveForRemoval` in `projectAgents.ts`, which records when each removal started and ended on one counter), and whether an agent works in it is read under the project file's lock after that. Whatever gives an agent a worktree (adding an agent, a load) takes a mark (`claimMark`) before it looks at the project's worktrees and, under the same lock, refuses one whose removal went on at any time since (`removedSince`) or whose folder is gone. So an agent given it first keeps it, and one that saw it before or while it was removed is refused ("That worktree is being removed, or was just removed"), however long it took: no agent is ever left in a removed worktree. A notice says what went with its branch, what went while its branch stayed (a ref moved in the last step: `branchKept`, #313), and what was kept, why (`oldWorktreesNotice` in `templateLoad.ts`); it is refused while an agent runs or is starting, a removed worktree agent has uncommitted work (or git can't say whether it has: then it isn't known to be safe), a provider the template needs is off or not installed (Agent Setup or Settings offered), or the template has worktree agents and the project isn't on a git branch (#702 found this was why templates were unavailable in a project without git; the reason now names the agents and the case: git can't run, the folder isn't a repository, or no branch to start from (detached, or no commit yet), with the way out: make it a repository with a first commit, or edit the template to put them in the project folder; a template of project-folder agents loads anywhere); and if the agents changed since they were shown (the confirmation carries their ids). One load per project at a time, and **all or nothing**: the project's agents can't start for the whole load (a fence on its starts, `sessions.fenceStarts`, lifted however it ends); the new agents and their worktrees are staged first without touching `project.json`, then published in one write under the project file's lock, only if the agents are still those shown and none runs or is starting. If an agent can't be created, or the agents changed meanwhile (one added by the Assistant, say), the staged worktrees go and the project stays as it is now: nothing half loaded is ever seen, and no change made meanwhile is lost. Removed agents keep their worktrees and branches (to merge or reuse), their conversations stay in Sessions, and their open cards go back as Remove Agent's **Move them back** does. No agent or Assistant tools for templates yet (they come with Planning). **Templates view and tab (#127):** the **Templates** view (activity bar, next to Skills; `components/Templates.tsx`) lists the workspace's templates, then each project's under its name, with a filter (All, Workspace, a project; kept while Hive runs); a project's **Templates** tab (after Skills) lists its own and the workspace's, each with its scope. Selecting one shows its description (optional, #271, at most 500 characters; one that isn't text or is too long is left out when read), its agents (name, role, coding agent, model and 200K context, effort, mode, worktree or the project folder) and layout. Actions: **Edit…** (#271: in its place, `TemplateEditor` in `Templates.tsx`: its name, description and layout (automatic or a chosen one); its agents added (**Add Agent…**), copied, moved up or down (their order is a project's: pages hold them in turn) and removed, at most 12; each edited in the **Agent Settings dialog** itself (`AgentSettingsModal` in `AgentDialogs.tsx`, shared with a project's agents, in its template mode, `TemplateAgentDialog`: a choice of its own worktree or the project folder in place of the worktree's details, no running session, role suggestions from the template's agents, names unique within the template; an empty choice stays empty, following the project it is loaded into, shown with the tab's project's or Hive's defaults); an agent whose coding agent this Hive doesn't know can be moved or removed, not edited. **Save Template** sends the draft to `templates:update` (`updateTemplate`), which reads it as untrusted (`readTemplate`, as an import) and writes it in its file under the place's lock (`changing`), refused if another template there has its name or the template's `savedAt` isn't the one the editor opened (a save meanwhile, from a project or another window, is never overwritten). The editor keeps the template as it opened it, whatever the list shows later: the draft is compared with that, and that revision is the one a save expects; when the list shows a newer one, the editor says the template was saved again and offers **Discard and Reload**, keeping the draft until then; its place stays (Duplicate… moves it). Cancel asks before throwing changes away. **An agent's Save saves the template** (#510: Save means save): when the agent's dialog saves, the editor saves the whole draft at once (its other changes too), unless the draft can't be saved yet (no name, or the template was saved again meanwhile: then it stays in the draft, as before), and stays open, comparing with what it saved; the list showing the revision it replaced until it reads it again isn't a save meanwhile. The draft is kept in the window (`templateEdits` in the store, by workspace and template) until it is saved or discarded: leaving the editor another way (another template, another view, the project's tab) keeps it, the list marks that template **Not saved** and the editor says **Changes not saved**, and the template opens in the editor again with them; an editor left with nothing changed keeps nothing. A draft with changes is one of the window's unsaved edits too (`editorDrafts`, listed by its file, `.hive/templates/<file>`): quitting, closing the window or the workspace, switching workspace and Reload Window ask about it as they do for files, even with quit confirmation off; Save there saves it as Save Template does (refused, as that is, when the template changed meanwhile: then Hive stays open with the draft), and Discard drops it. While a save runs (Save Template's or an agent's), every field and button of the editor is disabled, so no change is made that the save didn't take, and no agent's Save is accepted that isn't saved; Save Template closes the editor only when nothing changed since it began. The editor's Cancel reads Close when nothing is changed. Projects loaded from it earlier are never touched);  **Load into Project…** (the view asks which; the tab loads into its project; a project's template can load into any project, `from`), **Rename…** (in its place; a name taken there is refused), **Duplicate…** (into the workspace or any project, " (2)" for a taken name), **Delete** (file and `.bak` to the Recycle Bin), **Export…** (a save dialog, `<name>.hive-template.json`; the file is `exportable()`: version, name, savedAt, layout and agents only, so no paths, ids, sessions or names of people) and **Import…** (an open dialog; the file, at most 256 KB, is read as untrusted and refused with `readTemplate`'s reason before anything is asked or written; then where to keep it, the workspace or a project; a name already there asks **Replace** or **Keep Both**, " (2)"; one write, so nothing is half imported). Every change to a place's templates (save, import, duplicate, rename, delete) runs locked against the others for that folder (`changing()`), from any project or window, with its clash check and the choice of name and file inside: two at once never take the same file or replace each other unasked. The dialog for a place is a choice with a drop-down (`ChoiceRequest.select`). The lists refresh when this window changes a template (`templatesVersion`). Agent Settings has **Role** (free text, suggesting builder, reviewer and the project's roles), shown in the agent's tab tooltip.
- **Agents** (Session tab): a strip above the terminal lists the project's agents (status dot, provider icon, name, the worktree icon for worktree agents, lock count) and the **Add Agent** split button (Configure Agent and Add…, the dialog; ▾: Configure Agent and Add…, Add Agent from Template); a project without agents shows "No agents yet" with Add Agent, New Session (adds one and starts it) and All Sessions (its single empty pane draws nothing over it), and — once there is more than one agent — the layout: **one at a time** (the strip switches agents), **two columns**, **three columns**, a **2×2 grid** or a **3×2 grid**. **Pages** (#134): up to twelve agents (24 with Agents per project, #562), and **one layout for the project**: automatic (the one that shows its agents, up to the 3×2 grid) until one is chosen, which then stays as agents are added; choosing the one that shows the agents makes it automatic again. A page holds as many agents as the layout has panes, so every agent on a page has a pane: **pages = agents ÷ panes** (6 agents in a grid of four: 4 + 2; 4 in three columns: 3 + 1; 12 in the grid of six: 6 + 6), the last page's spare panes empty (Add Agent). One at a time (Single) is one page of every agent, its tabs choosing who is shown, so it has no page buttons. The strip's right-hand group is, left to right, Template ▾, the page buttons and the layouts (#292: the pages beside the layouts they follow from; the Assistant's strip has no Template ▾).
