With more than one page, numbered buttons in the strip switch pages (Ctrl+Alt+PageDown / PageUp), each with a dot for that page's most urgent agent and its agents in the tooltip; more than six pages (more than 12 agents in two columns, #562) show a compact pager instead, **‹ 3 / 12 ▾ ›**: ‹ and › go to the previous and next page (round from the last to the first; each a page button, with that page's dot, its tooltip and its drop target, held over while dragging to step through the pages), and the middle opens a menu of every page with its agents' names, a dot on it when another page has an urgent agent; a line in the strip marks where each page starts (and thin lines separate neighbouring agent tabs, none beside the focused one), and the strip scrolls sideways (mouse wheel too) keeping the focused agent in view. Changing the layout re-pages at once. A page's panes show its agents in their order; clicking an agent on another page goes to that page. Going to a page focuses the agent last focused there. Stored per project as `layout` (§4); the per-page `layouts` of earlier versions are read as page 1's (else automatic). Every agent pane, with one agent or several, has a **header** (status, provider, name, the worktree icon, card, locks; then Compact and Stop (red) while running, or Resume, Resume a Session… and New Session; Merge… for a worktree; ⋯ with Archive and Start New… (while running), Hand Over to…, Agent Settings, Review Changes, Remove, Discard) and a **footer** (model · effort, the permission mode badge; on the right the running **session** (its icon), context used, as tokens and the percentage of the context window ("84k · 42%"; tokens only while the window isn't known) — amber over the Compact threshold —, the transcript's size and the session's cost; a click on the cost (or "$?") opens the Overview on that agent's session, scrolled to its details, as an anchor: `showInOverview(path, agentId, 'session')`, where the context lands on its compaction history; its tooltip ends, after an empty line, with "Click to see this session's details in the Overview" (#122; not on the Assistant's footer, whose clicks open nothing). Short of room (`useFooterFit` in `AgentPanes.tsx`: worked out from what every item takes in full, and back to more text only with 2 px to spare, so a footer on the edge never flips), items give up their text for their icon in turn, each before its text would be cut, never a truncated "1…": the transcript size, then the context's tokens (its percentage stays), then the whole context; their tooltips keep the figures and the text stays for screen readers. Then the permission mode's label shortens with an ellipsis (its icon and caret always show), then the model's, and only then is the cost cut off at the right. Compact, Stop and Merge… are always icon buttons (as in the Assistant's header; Merge…'s count stays beside its icon, its name and what it merges are in its tooltip and accessible name); the other header buttons are labelled from 565 px of pane width, icons from 340 px, and otherwise all are only in ⋯. The mode follows the header's width both ways (a pane, window, sidebar or layout change, or a pane shown again after being hidden), with no memory of an earlier mode. Short of room, the agent's details give way to the buttons, never the reverse: the card's title first (down to `#n`), then the status and name (with an ellipsis; each has the full text on hover). **A worktree is only its icon** in the agent's tab and header (#287), which never shrinks: its tooltip (on hover, and on keyboard focus: it is focusable, `Tooltip`'s `focus`) gives the branch, the folder, the base and what is left to merge, and its accessible name is "Worktree: <branch> in <folder>"; agents in the project folder have none. A tooltip inside another's (the icon in a tab) hides the outer one while it shows. So each mode only needs room for its widest set of buttons, a stopped worktree agent's with a four-digit Merge… count, which both widths are set to fit (`tests/e2e/paneheader.cjs` measures it). An icon button widens for its count (Merge…'s commits, or • for uncommitted files alone) rather than letting it spill out. While a compaction runs, Compact shows a turning spinner (the Assistant's too), disabled, until the compaction ends, fails or the agent stops. The **project header** is the project's only: name and combined status; Active, Explorer, Terminal, **Resume All Agents** (while any stopped agent has a session to resume; resumes those one after another, never restarts a running agent, asks first only when some prompt caches have expired, and reports each agent that couldn't resume with the reason while the rest still resume; also in the project's right-click menu when it has several agents), **Stop All Agents** (while any runs; always confirms, listing each agent and its state) and ⋯ (Changes, Project Settings, Remove Project…; agents are added from the agent strip); below 860 px Explorer and Terminal show icons only. The rule for new controls: a button when it is used often, the ⋯ menu otherwise. **Resume** is a split button in pane placeholders and the empty-session card (in the agent header it is Resume plus Resume a Session…): the main part resumes the agent's own session — its last one, else the latest it ran in its folder, skipping archived ones and any open in another agent (worked out in main as `AgentInfo.resume`) — and is disabled with an explanation when there is none; **▾** opens a picker of the 10 most recent sessions from the agent's folder with when each was last active, which agent last ran it and an expired-cache warning; sessions open in another agent are greyed and clicking one shows that agent. **The session** shows in the agent's footer as just its icon, right-aligned before the context, at every width and whatever its name: hover for its name, when it started and its ID (its accessible name is "Session: <name>"), click to read it in the Sessions tab, right-click to **Rename…** it (as the Sessions tab's Rename). The name is the **latest rename**, in Hive or in the CLI (Claude Code's `/rename`, Codex's thread name): Hive passes its name to the CLI at launch (`--name`), so it records the CLI's name each time the session is renamed in Hive (`titleAtRename` in `sessions.json`), and once the CLI's name differs from that, the CLI's is the newer one. Resuming such a session makes the CLI's name Hive's, so the launch passes it back rather than the old one. Without a rename the CLI's own title shows; Hive's automatic name ("<project> · <agent> · <date>", which Claude Code keeps as its title) counts as no name, and then the time the session started shows ("2026-10-04 14:05", in the user's date and time format; names the user typed are never changed): its record's `createdAt`, which a new session's record takes from its launch's start (`LiveSessionState.sessionCreatedAt`), so the footer and the Sessions tab show the same minute, and a resumed session keeps its time rather than the resume's (#630; "Running since" in the tooltip is the launch's). The same name is used in the Sessions tab, the session pickers and the agent strip's tooltips (`sessionLabel()` in `shared/defaults.ts`). The Assistant's footer leaves the name out. The agent strip's tooltips say what each agent is running or what Resume would open. Clicking a pane or its terminal focuses that agent; keyboard shortcuts, the Session menu, Ctrl+V images and Insert into Session act on the focused agent. Clicking an agent that isn't on screen puts it in the focused pane, or goes to its page. Empty panes offer Add Agent. Ctrl+Alt+] / Ctrl+Alt+[ cycle agents (across pages). The sidebar keeps **one dot per project** (the most urgent agent's state: needs input, then working…), with a count when several agents run; the project header badge does the same, with each agent's status on hover. Notifications, the quit dialog and the tray name the agent when a project has several. There is no limit on running sessions across projects. Only visible terminals use WebGL, within a budget of 8 contexts per window (Chromium drops the oldest context beyond about 16, which could be a terminal on screen): hidden terminals keep theirs for 30 seconds so switching back is instant, or give it up at once when a visible terminal needs one; the rest draw with xterm's DOM renderer. A terminal giving WebGL up (hidden too long, or ended: each new session re-creates its agent's terminal) also loses its context at once (`WEBGL_lose_context`): disposing the renderer alone left it alive until garbage collection, so Start New (All) on many agents pushed the window past Chromium's limit and the oldest context, the Assistant's, was dropped (#293). A terminal whose context is lost anyway draws with the DOM and is fitted again, the DOM's cells being wider.
- **Session terminal**: Ctrl+V pastes text; if the clipboard holds only an image, Hive saves it to `.hive/attachments/<session id>/` (`session:pasteImage`) and pastes the path, which Claude Code attaches. Files dropped on the terminal (or inserted from the Files or Attachments tab) are **attachments** (§ Attachments). Right-click pastes text only. **A program's own copy** (#736): a CLI that selects text with the mouse itself copies it by writing an OSC 52 sequence to the terminal (Copilot: a drag over its screen, then Ctrl+C; Claude Code sends one beside its own copy; Codex copies without one). Hive puts the text on the clipboard when the terminal has the focus in the focused window and had the user's key or mouse button in the last 10 s (leaving the window forgets that input, so coming back alone allows nothing), up to 1 MB, and never answers an OSC 52 read: a program can't read the clipboard. Refusals are logged (each kind at most once a minute a terminal). Shift+drag still makes Hive's own selection, which Ctrl+C and right-click copy. (Claude Code's own Alt+V image paste still works, but its image is not kept by Hive.) **File links**: file paths in an agent's or the Assistant's terminal (`src/a.ts`, `src\a.ts`, absolute, `./` and `../`, with `:line`, `:line:col`, `(line,col)` or Python's `", line N`) are links when they lead to a file that exists in a workspace project or agent worktree; **Ctrl+click** opens the file in that project's Files tab (the worktree's folder for a worktree) with the cursor on the line, a Markdown or other previewed file switching to Split or Edit. A plain click stays a click (select, focus), and web links still open on a plain click. Relative paths are from the agent's folder (its worktree or project); the Assistant's are from the workspace folder. Names may have brackets and braces (`app/[slug]/page.tsx`); a path wrapped in brackets (`[src/a.ts]`) links without them. A quoted path (`"…"`, `'…'`, `` `…` ``) may have spaces, and a location inside the quotes (`"src/a.ts:12"`); quoted text that looks like a path is one path, so when it isn't a file no piece of it is linked (not `file.ts` in `"missing file.ts"`, nor `src/a.ts` in `"Fix src/a.ts"`). Unquoted, a path with spaces is tried as a run of words (from a word with a folder in it, or one plain word just before one) and its longest beginning that is a file is linked; a run that starts with an absolute path and isn't a file as a whole links none of its pieces. Paths outside the workspace's projects and worktrees, folders, `.git` and missing files aren't linked. Existence is checked in main (`files:linkFiles`, within a project or worktree root) only for the lines being hovered, remembered for 5 s; it returns each file's path as spelled on disk, which the link opens (Windows ignores case, the Files tree doesn't). A path wrapped across two lines or redrawn mid-word isn't matched (`shared/fileLinks.ts`). **Card links** (#440): a card number (`#12`) in any Hive terminal is a link when it is a card on the window's board (archived too); hovering shows its title and column as they are now, and **Ctrl+click** opens the card dialog in that window, like a file link. Not linked: inside a longer word, path or HTML entity (`abc#12`, `a/#12`, `&#12;`), hex or a version (`#fff`, `#12ab`, `#12.5`), a leading zero, more than seven digits, or a URL's fragment (`shared/cardLinks.ts`).
- **Terminal size and scroll** (#247, `TerminalView.tsx`): `fitTerminal()` is the only place a terminal is fitted to its pane and its size sent to the CLI (`pty:resize`). Never while it is hidden (its own view, or an ancestor not displayed: Settings open, another tab, project or page) nor while it measures under 20 × 5, so a hidden terminal keeps its last size and the CLI isn't told it is a few characters wide; it is fitted once its layout settles (100 ms after the last change: a resize, the terminal appearance settings, being shown again), and main passes on only a size that changed. A terminal following its output (at the bottom; only the user's own wheel, keys or scroll bar change that) is held at the bottom for a second after each fit and after being shown again, since xterm syncs its scroll position a frame or more late; one the user scrolled up in keeps its line.
  **From the process's start** (#486): until it is fitted, a terminal is its process's size (`pty:size`), not xterm's 80 × 24, so what a CLI draws before the fit (its first screens, or everything while its pane is hidden) lands where it drew it; output drawn into a smaller grid had left Copilot's screen with stacked frames and rules cut short until the window was resized. Main keeps the size the window last gave each terminal (`startSize` in `ptyHost.ts`, also one given before its process exists), and a process started in it (a restart, a mode switch that restarts) starts at that size rather than 120 × 32. These kept sizes (and a quiet exit's output, kept for the process that continues in the terminal) are bounded (#489): at most 200 sizes and 20 outputs of stopped terminals, the least recently set dropped first (a running terminal's are always kept on top of these, and trimmed once it stops); a removed agent's are forgotten at once, and all of a removed or deleted project's (`forgetPtys`), a hidden project's kept. A provider whose full-screen interface a resize while it starts can leave at the wrong width (`capabilities.startupSizeRefresh`: Copilot) gets a **size refresh** (`refreshPty`: one row shorter for 80 ms, then its size, or the size the window gave meanwhile), as resizing the window by hand would, half a second after its first status, again once it is ready, and after Hive switches its mode live (Shift+Tab or a menu; a switch that restarts it is a new start). Claude Code and Codex redraw their own way at a resize and don't get it. A mode whose CLI names it differently on its own screen says why in the mode badge's tooltip while a session runs in it (`ModeOption.cliNote`: Copilot's Accept edits, whose own footer still says Manual Approval, as Accept edits is its Ask with Hive's allow rule for edits in the agent's folder).
- **Progress panel** (`components/Progress.tsx`, `main/progress.ts`, `main/progressService.ts`, `shared/progress.ts`): long runs agents report through the Agent API (`/v1/progress`; a run is the calling agent's by its token, the Assistant's or a script's; only its owner, or a workspace-token script, updates or finishes it). A panel on the right, left of the Assistant's when both are open, shown and folded with **Ctrl+Alt+P** (`progress.toggle`), open state per workspace (`ui.panes`, `progress-open:<path>`), width draggable. Folded it is a 28 px strip with a small vertical bar per shown run (the newest six) and **+N** for the rest (#142: red if one of them failed, else amber if one is stale; hover lists them, up to 12); the strip's accessible name counts every run by state ("8 runs: 6 running, 1 failed, 1 stopped reporting"); it never opens by itself. Each run: agent and provider icon (the Assistant's mark for its runs, #399), project, title (command on hover), a bar (moving when it has no steps), "n of m: step", elapsed and time left (`estimateMs` is the time left as of the last report). Every line of a run stays inside its box: one line with an ellipsis (title, step, status, agent and project), the failure's summary wrapping; the panel never scrolls sideways (#220). Passed runs show for 10 s, then move to **Recent**. Failed and stale ones stay until the user has **seen** them (`progress:seen`: the panel open, the window focused, a failed or stale run with `seenAt` null; main sets each one's `seenAt` and clears the taskbar's red), then 10 s more, then move to Recent too (`isListed`, `isRecent` in `shared/progress.ts`), marked red; the folded strip drops a seen one at once (`inStrip`), so its red clears with the taskbar's. A new report (back to running) or a failure makes it unseen again. Dismiss moves one to Recent at once. **Honest states** (#549, `runStatusLine`, `barLook`): a run reported `waiting` (`waiting`, `waitingSince`) shows what it waits for as its step and "waiting n min" (after how long it ran first, when it ran over 5 s), never a time left, with a still, hatched bar (`aria-valuetext` "waiting"; the strip's bar the same); while it waits it has no `estimateMs` (the time that was left is held, `heldEstimateMs`, and given back from when it stops waiting, so the wait isn't counted; an estimate sent while waiting is held too) and never goes stale (`isOverdue`). A running run past its estimate says "finishing" for a minute (`FINISHING_MS`), then "longer than expected", never "0 s left" (less than a second left counts as reached). A **stopped** run (`state: 'stopped'`) is neither passed nor failed, muted (⊘), listed for 10 s like a passed run, then under Recent: `stopReason` `interrupted` (a finish with `stopped: true`: "Stopped after n min") or `gone` (its reporter's `pid`, sent at the start, is no longer a process while the run is open, running or stale, checked with the stale sweep every 15 s: "Ended without a report after n min"); neither marks a failure unseen. A reused pid reads as alive: such a run goes stale as before, never ends wrongly. **Recent** (#352) shows the newest 10 ended runs (`RECENT_RUNS`), then **Show all n**: unfolded, every kept run in a box of the same height (the 10 rows'), scrolling inside, so the panel around it doesn't move; **Show fewer** folds it back to the top. The fold is kept per workspace (`ui.panes`, `progress-recent-all:<path>`). The list is `ShowAllList` (`components/ShowAllList.tsx`), the shared "last few, Show all n, fixed-height scroll" pattern (#312's Done by the Assistant uses it too): a real button with `aria-expanded`, the unfolded box a named, focusable region. The agent's name on a run is a button that shows its agent (the Assistant's panel for the Assistant's), and says so: the pane (or panel) flashes for 1.2 s and its terminal takes the keyboard (`flashPane`; retried for up to a second while a switched-to project's terminals appear; this focus, and a terminal's own focus a frame after it is shown, apply only while nothing newer chose where the keyboard goes: no key or pointer press, no other agent shown, and no other control that took it meanwhile, `deferredFocus.ts`, #327), so showing an agent already on screen visibly does something (#220: the reported "does nothing"). A click elsewhere on a run, or on a Recent row, or the chevron right-aligned at the end of every run and Recent row (after a Recent row's time; down while closed, up while open; a button, "Show/Hide details for <run>", `aria-expanded`; #251), opens its **details** inline (one at a time; Escape closes), as `runDetails()` in `shared/progress.ts` lists them: title, command (Copy command), agent and project, provider, started and ended (`formatDateTime`), how long against `expectedMs` (the first estimate's total), steps (done of total, last step), state and why, `exitCode`, `logPath`, then the summary, and **Show the agent**, or why not (a script's run; an agent removed; a project gone). **Copy details** copies them all as plain text, a `Label: value` line each and the summary last (`runDetailsText`). **Open log** and **Show in folder** (`progress:openLog`, `progress:showLog`) take the run's id, not a path: main opens only the `logPath` that run reported with its finish, a full path to an existing file, wherever it is (an e2e record is under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`), without widening `guardFile` for the window's own file calls. Open log opens only text logs and reports (`.md`, `.txt`, `.log`, `.json`, `.csv`, `.xml`, `.html`…) in their default app, never a program; anything fails with a toast saying why (no log, not a full path, gone, not a text log). A **filter** at the top of the panel (#251; `activeFilter`, `filterRuns`, `filterChoices`, `agentChoices` in `shared/progress.ts`): **All projects**, each project with runs (by name), **Assistant and scripts** (runs with no project); a project with runs from more than one agent adds an agent filter. It applies to the listed runs and Recent alike (Recent is the filtered runs' last 10, and Show all n counts and lists every kept run the filter shows), is kept per workspace (`ui.progressFilter`, by workspace path in lower case, the newest 100), and the folded strip shows and counts only those runs, with a filter mark and "<project> only: …; n in all" in its name. Hidden while every run is in one project; a choice whose project (or agent) has no runs left falls back to all (or the whole project). The taskbar and seeing failures still cover every run. Stale: running with no report for longer than expected (a step's share of the time left + 2 min; the estimate + 2 min; 10 min with no estimate), or its agent's session stopped (checked every 15 s); a new report revives it. Open runs live in memory per workspace and go when it closes or its window opens another (`WorkspaceService.closingListeners`; a report whose caller lookup outlived the workspace is refused): at most 30 open per workspace whoever reported them (`429` beyond). Ended runs (#352): the newest 200 per workspace (`RECENT_KEPT`; the oldest ended go first, open ones never), also kept in `<userData>/progress-history/<hash of the workspace path>.json` (saved 2 s after a run ends, at once when the workspace closes, and when Hive quits; loaded the first time its window asks, merged with runs reported meanwhile; read as untrusted input, `keptRuns()`: malformed entries dropped, text capped, each counted as seen so it is under Recent); runs open at a restart aren't kept. A save before the file has loaded merges the file in first, and reads and writes go one at a time. Turning the panel off forgets the kept files too; 5 open per owner, field lengths capped, every field checked before anything changes, updates merged to at most four window events a second. An update without `estimateMs` keeps the deadline. `total` is set at the start or, on a run started without one, once by an update (#145; again is `409`; a step past it is lowered to it). A dismissed stale run has ended (out of status, taskbar and limits; `409` to reports). The window showing the workspace gets `progress-changed` events and a **taskbar progress bar** (`setProgressBar`): the combined steps of running runs, indeterminate when none has steps, error mode (red) after a failure until the panel is looked at (open, window focused). An agent's open run is in its API status as `progress`, and in the short project listing (`hive_list_projects`) as a few words. **Settings → General → Progress panel** (on by default) turns off the panel, strip and taskbar; reports are then accepted and ignored (`ignored: true`), also one whose caller was still being looked up when the setting changed (`admitReport`: the setting and its generation are checked again after the lookup, with no wait before the change).
- **Merge card and lanes** (#484, Darren's revision 1 of the panel; `components/ProgressMerge.tsx`, `components/ProgressLanes.tsx`, `shared/laneBar.ts`). Top to bottom the panel is: a **merge card** per merge going on: a slot whose holder has taken it (`holder.taken`; the user's own merge counts), not a turn given but not claimed yet nor a line alone (every project's; the project's name when the workspace has more than one), a divider (with a card only), the runs and Recent as before, and the **lanes**, pinned to the bottom. Neither shows without a merge or lanes, so a solo user's panel is as before. **The merge card** is in its own colour, teal (`--merge`, `--merge-ink`; no status uses it; dark `#2aa9bb`, light `#0e7f8e`): the holder (`holderText`: "B1 is merging #484", "You are merging"), the holder's branch from its worktree and the slot's (`hive/b1 → main`; "into main" for an agent in the project folder or the user), the holder's current step (its newest open Progress run in that project: "Full test set · 113 of 146: mergeslot"), "n min so far · n min left" (the warning colour once over its time), "Waiting: …", and **Release** (the Overview's confirm, `releaseSlot`). Its chevron folds it to its first line with the minutes so far, remembered per workspace (`ui.panes`, `progress-merge-fold:<path>`). On a short panel the merge cards take at most a third of it and the lanes give way (their bar always shown; at most half before #569, 40% before #512), each scrolling inside, and the runs keep at least 72 px. **Lanes** are #483's (`LaneInfo`, `lanes:list` and `lanes-changed`, the store's `lanes`) through one adapter (`renderer/src/laneSource.ts`), which maps them to what the panel reads (`Lane`: id, the project's path from its folder name, builder and reviewer, name, card numbers in order, state active/paused/ended/abandoned; a lane of a project not in the workspace is left out). Each card's segment is read from the board as it is now, not from the lane, except in an ended or abandoned lane: its segments are its cards as it left them (`Lane.frozen`, `frozenSegment`, #512), muted and never animated, with "…, when the lane ended" in the tooltip, no failed mark (the lane keeps no verdicts) and no current card; an `unrecorded` card is a dashed outline, "not recorded: the lane ended before Hive kept where its cards were", and the lane's count reads "–/n". The panel's filter applies (a project's lanes; an agent's: builder or reviewer). A header bar, **Lanes** with the count (a button, `aria-expanded`), folds the section to itself at the bottom (`progress-lanes-fold:<path>`); open, the list shows **up to four lanes**, then scrolls (#569, Darren: six (#512) was too many), and with fewer it is only as tall as they are. **Its top edge is a handle** (`.progress-lanes-edge`, a WAI-ARIA window splitter: `role=separator`, focusable, `aria-valuenow` the lanes shown now, `aria-valuemax` the most that fit, `aria-valuetext` "5 of 7 lanes shown"): dragging it up shows more lanes and down fewer, snapping to whole lanes (the list's `max-height` is the lanes shown times a lane's measured height with its margin, so the next lane starts at the edge), from one to as many as there are, and no further up than leaves the runs above 120 px (`RUNS_KEEP_PX`); double-click goes back to four; focused, Up and Down change it by a lane, Home and End to one and to all that fit. **A panel too short for the choice** (a smaller window, zoom, a merge card) shows the whole lanes that fit while the runs keep 120 px, and the splitter says that number and that maximum; the choice is kept for when there is room again (the room, the list's height plus the runs', and a lane's height are measured with a `ResizeObserver`; `laneRowsNow`). A drag or key sets the choice to what it then shows. The choice is a number of lanes (`chosenLaneRows`, `lanesShown`, `maxLaneRows`, `laneRowsNow`, `draggedLaneRows`, `keyedLaneRows` in `shared/laneBar.ts`), kept per workspace (`ui.panes`, `progress-lanes-rows:<path>`; none is the default), and folding keeps it; the handle isn't there while folded. **Order** (`panelOrder`, #512, #568, Darren's decisions of 11 Oct): running lanes (active and paused) first, then ended and abandoned ones in the order they finished (`endedAt`), the latest last, so the finished ones read oldest first and a lane that finishes always drops to the very bottom. Without a move the running lanes are as #512 had them: active ones, then paused ones, newest first in each. **The user can put the running lanes in their own order** (only running lanes move): dragging a row (HTML5 drag, `application/x-hive-lane`; the top half of another running lane drops before it, the bottom half before the next running one, or after the last; an accent line, `drop-before`/`drop-after`, shows where, none where it would stay; held near the list's top or bottom edge, the list scrolls every frame, as the board's columns do (`shared/edgeScroll.ts`), and the line follows what scrolls under the pointer), focused (a running lane is a focusable `role=group` named by its pair and name) **Alt+Up** and **Alt+Down** a place (not past either end, said as such), or the row's context menu: **Move Up**, **Move Down** and **Usual Order** (forgets the order). Each move is said to screen readers in a status region ("B8 / R1 (one more) moved: 3 of 5 running lanes"), and the moved lane keeps the focus. The order is the running lanes' ids as they then show (`droppedLaneOrder`, `nudgedLaneOrder`), kept per workspace in Hive's settings (`ui.laneOrder`, by workspace path in lower case; `ui:setLaneOrder` changes this window's workspace's only, on what is saved now, at most 100 ids, `keptLaneOrder`), and survives restarts. Lanes it doesn't name (started since) go on top, newest first whether active or paused (a new lane paused at once on a blocked card is still on top), and the others keep its order below them; a lane that pauses or runs again keeps its place; a finished lane leaves it (its id is dropped at the next move). The same order is every lane list's (`useLanes`): the agents tables (#529) follow it too. A lane is a header (builder / reviewer, its name, the state word for paused, ended "merged" and abandoned, the current card: the one being worked (`currentCard`, #512), and finished/total, finished being Passed or Done; ended and abandoned lanes dimmed) and a **segmented bar**: one equal-width segment per card in the lane's order (`laneCards`), each a button (click: `taskOpen`; tooltip and accessible name: "#405 Built-in loop wakes · Review, round 2", `cardSegment`). **Its marks explain themselves** (#706, Darren had to ask what the red line meant): under that line the tooltip has a line per mark (`CardSegment.notes`), and the accessible name ends with them (`segmentName`): a failed round's underline "#650: review failed (round 4): waiting for round 5", then "Reviewer: " and the first line of the comment its reviewer gave with that verdict (`failedVerdictGist`: by the verdict's author, within 10 s of it, Markdown bold and heading marks dropped), and the hatching's "#7: blocked: <reason>" or "#7: On Hold: <reason>" (an On Hold card with none: "waiting for you to move it on"), the reason's first line, both cut at 160 characters. A card failed and blocked has both; a finished card with a stale reason has none. An ended lane's segment keeps its blocked or On Hold reason as a note (no verdicts, so no underline note). Segment states, colour and pattern both (colour-blind safe, both themes): todo an empty track with an edge, doing `--info` diagonal stripes moving (still with reduced motion on), review an amber (`--warning`) outline on its tint, passed `--success` with a light inner edge, done `--success` dimmed and flat, blocked (a blocked reason, or On Hold: waiting for the user) `--error` cross-hatch; a failed round (`reviewRounds`: the latest verdict in its history "Review failed" with no "Returned for review", "Moved to Review" or "Started reviewing" after it, in Doing or Review) underlines the segment in `--error`. Rounds count the card's failed reviews: in Doing or Review it is in round failures + 1 (the failed one while failed), a finished one "Passed in round n" past round 1. Segments are at least 12 px with 2 px gaps (`segmentsThatFit` on the panel's width); a lane with more cards than fit folds its finished cards into one "✓ n" segment at the left, and if that isn't enough, its last cards into "+n" (`fitSegments`; their tooltips list the cards). Cards not on the board (deleted) read as todo, "not on the board".
- **Progress reporters** (#137; `main/progressReporters/`, `tests/progressReport.mts`): **`hive-progress [--title T] -- <command>`** reports any command as the calling agent's run. Hive writes a `.cmd` and an sh script into `<profile>/bin`, first on every session's `PATH`, which start the unpacked `hive-progress.js` with Hive's executable as Node. Output and exit code pass through, and the command gets the caller's environment (the shims' `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`, which runs the wrapper, is swapped back for the caller's own, kept in `HIVE_PROGRESS_RUN_AS_NODE`, so an Electron app it runs starts as an app); finish is ok on exit code 0 (else `exit code N`), and **stopped** (`stopped: true`, summary `interrupted`) when the command ended by an interrupt (`STATUS_CONTROL_C_EXIT` 0xC000013A, or 130) or `hive-progress` was sent a stop signal (Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Break, the console closing, SIGTERM: the command has 5 s to end by itself, a second signal ends it at once). Told to stop, the run is stopped however the command then ends (a server shutting down cleanly exits 0; its exit code is passed on), and a stopped run is never timed. Ending it ends what it started too (`endTree`: on Windows, where a `.cmd` or npm command is cmd with the server under it, the tree is listed with start times and each process ended through a handle checked against its start time, never one that started before the parent its id names, which `taskkill /T` would end (#655); the command alone if that fails), and the run ends 2 s after the command exits even if something still holds its output. Run by the shims it is Electron as Node, with no console, and its command has a hidden console of its own, so a Ctrl+C typed in the agent's terminal reaches neither, and an agent stopping it ends the process tree, which nothing can see: that run is ended by Hive when its pid is gone (#549). The entry ignores SIGINT itself. A `##hive-progress wait=<what>` line reports `waiting` (the run starts at once, without waiting for a total), `wait=` (empty) its end; the time waited is left out of the command's timing. The estimate is the median of the last five passing runs of that command line in that folder (timings under a hash in `<profile>/progress/timings.json`, never the project). Step lines `##hive-progress step=N total=M name=…` (N the step starting, from 1) are taken out of the output and reported (`step` N−1 finished, `stepName`); the run starts at the first step line with a total, or after 2 s; a run started without one sends the first total printed later once (`PATCH`, #145), and later totals are ignored. Silent and harmless outside Hive, unreachable, refused, or `HIVE_PROGRESS=0`. **Agents use it unasked** for commands and background tasks likely to take over 30 s: the session contract (`progressRule` in `shared/hiveGuidance.ts`) says so while **Settings → General → Agents show long commands in the Progress panel** (`general.progressCommands`, on by default) and the Progress panel are both on, and otherwise to use it only when the user asks; the setting is greyed out while the panel is off (#167). The hive MCP server gets the choice as `HIVE_PROGRESS_COMMANDS`, Codex the same text in its developer instructions; it applies to sessions started afterwards. The command gets `HIVE_PROGRESS_WRAPPED=1`: a reporter inside it (another `hive-progress`, Hive's own runners) then reports no run of its own (`progressTarget` is null) and Hive's runners print step lines for the wrapper's run instead, so a wrapped `npm test` is one row (its total, printed once Vitest has found the files, comes late if that takes over 2 s). Hive's own runners report through `tests/progressReport.mts`: a heavy run waiting for a test slot (or the Codex test home) reports `waiting` (its own row, or wait lines under `hive-progress`, and a `wait=` line when it gets the slot); `npm run e2e` a step per suite with per-suite estimates (`%LOCALAPPDATA%/hive-test/progress-timings.json`; `--no-progress`), `npm test` a Vitest reporter added to Vitest's own reporters, a step per file; output unchanged; the e2e runner and the scenario harness give the suites, their test Hives and the wrappers they start only an allowlisted environment (`tests/e2e/runContext.cjs`), so no session or wrapper variable (`HIVE_*`, `HIVE_PROGRESS_WRAPPED`) reaches them (#202, #203).
- **Project view tabs**: Session (terminal), Overview (usage, compaction, re-cache), Performance (its metrics), Tasks (its board cards, §6.6), Sessions (list and transcript viewer), Files (file browser), Attachments (what was pasted or dropped into sessions; its id `images`, and its command `project.tab.images`, are from before #387, kept so tab settings and shortcuts carry over), Changes (git diff), Memory, Skills, Templates (#127), MCP, Settings (project overrides). When their labels don't fit the strip, every tab shows its icon only except the active one, which keeps its label if that fits (otherwise it is an icon too, and the strip scrolls sideways with the active tab kept in view when even the icons don't fit). The strip measures each tab with and without its label (`ProjectTabStrip`), so the fit follows its width (window, sidebar, panels) both ways; it goes back to a roomier fit only with 12 px to spare, so it doesn't flicker at the switching point. Icon-only tabs are narrower, keep their order, height and tooltip (name and shortcut), and their label for screen readers. At the window's minimum width (900 px) with the sidebar shown, all thirteen fit as icons (`tests/e2e/tabstrip.cjs`).
- **Files tab**: tree of the project folder with create (file/folder, inline name, `a/b/c` creates intermediate folders), rename (F2), delete to the Recycle Bin (Del, confirmed), cut/copy/paste and duplicate, multi-select, drag to move (Ctrl copies), drop from Explorer to copy in, drag onto the Session tab to paste paths, open in the default app, reveal, copy path. Git status colours; git-ignored entries and `.hive` dimmed; `.git` hidden. Folders load on expand; a recursive `fs.watch` keeps the tree live while the tab is open (changes inside `node_modules` aren't followed; while files keep changing, the tree still updates at least once a second). Moving several entries checks every destination first, so a name already taken moves none. A paste, duplicate, drop, rename or new file selects what it made when it ends, only if the selection hasn't changed since it started (counted per change, `selectionSeq`): a row clicked meanwhile keeps the selection, so the next key acts on it, not on what was pasted (#641). Find searches the project (`git ls-files`, so ignored files are excluded). Selecting a file opens it in the right pane as an **editor** (Monaco, Ctrl+S saves, UTF-8 with BOM preserved, 5 MB limit, binary files refused). Previews come from a registry in `FileView.tsx`: Markdown (Preview/Split/Edit, front matter hidden, Monaco-coloured code blocks, relative images and links), CSV/TSV table, HTML in a no-permission sandboxed iframe, SVG as an image, images and PDF (Chromium viewer) through `hive-img:`. Unsaved edits are kept as in-memory drafts across file and tab switches (marked ● in the tree). The other editors (shared notes, skills, instruction and memory files, MCP server definitions) keep theirs the same way across view and file changes, are included wherever unsaved files are asked about (quit, reload, closing or switching the workspace), and saving one whose file changed on disk since it was loaded asks before overwriting it. Deleting a shared note, skill, persona or MCP server (all to the Recycle Bin) drops its unsaved edits, so saving them can't bring it back; a note with unsaved edits can't be renamed until they are saved or discarded. Typing while a save is in progress keeps the newer text as a draft. Renaming or moving a file or folder in the tab carries its drafts along; deleting warns that unsaved changes will be lost; quitting always asks while any file has unsaved edits (listed, with **Save them** or **Discard the changes**; a file that changed on disk is never overwritten, and Hive stays open), and **Reload Window** and switching or closing the workspace offer **Save All** first. Drafts are not kept across a crash (hot exit is a possible later addition). Saves carry the file's modified time and are refused if it changed on disk; the user chooses Reload or Overwrite. Clean files follow changes on disk. Third-party preview plugins are deferred.
- **Dates and times** (`shared/dates.ts`): every date and time Hive shows goes through one formatter (`formatDate`, `formatTime`, `formatDateTime`, `formatWhen` for "14:05 today, else the date too", `formatWeekdayTime`), following **Settings → General → Date format** (`general.dateFormat`: `ymd` 2026-10-04, the default; `dmy`; `mdy`; `system`, Windows' regional format's short date: `app.getSystemLocale()`, given to the window with the language, never the language Hive shows, #767) and **Time format** (`general.timeFormat`: `24h`, the default, or `12h` "2:05 PM"). Each process holds the current choice (`setDateStyle`: main from its settings, the renderer from a store subscription made before any component), and components that show dates re-render when it changes (`useDateStyle()`). It applies to every date and time a person reads: sessions named by when they started (`sessionLabel`) and Hive's automatic session names (`autoName`, which still count as no name), the session lists and transcripts, Markdown exports (the title, "exported from Hive" and each message), the Overview's session details (Started, Last activity) and compaction history, "Running since" and a watch's deadline in the footer, "last updated" notes, a card's history, the Assistant's activity list, the Files tab's Modified and image times, the Performance views, an update's release date and plan-usage resets. File names, keys and what agents are told keep a fixed yyyy-mm-dd. Names users typed are never changed. Relative times ("2 hours ago") stay, with the full date and time on hover in the Sessions tab; the charts' day axes keep their short "Mon 5 Oct" labels.
- **Language** (#767; `shared/i18n/`, `main/i18n.ts`, `renderer/i18n.ts`): user-visible text is a message, `msg({ id, defaultMessage, description })` (`shared/i18n/msg.ts`, which imports nothing, so data modules agent scripts reach can hold messages), shown with `tr()` (or `tRich()` for markup) in the current language by FormatJS (`@formatjs/intl`, ICU MessageFormat, pinned exact); numbers, money, relative times, dates and lists go through `fmt`, in the language's locale. Text is translated when shown, never at module load. **Translations** are `locales/<code>/<area>.json` (id → ICU message), found by the loader (`shared/i18n/catalogues.ts`, which only main imports); `locales/en/<area>.json` is the English extracted from the code, with each message's note for translators, and `shared/i18n/values/<area>.d.ts` types each message's placeholders for `tr()`. A message a language lacks, or whose translation can't be formatted (a renamed placeholder, broken ICU), shows its English, and the problem is logged once. **Languages** (`LANGUAGES`, `shared/i18n/languages.ts`): English and seven drafted by AI (de, es, fr, ja, pl, pt-BR, zh-CN), each with its own name, its AI mark and note in its own words, and its `dir`. Every language can be chosen (its untranslated text shows in English), but Automatic picks one only once it has a translations folder, so Windows alone never gives a language that is still all English. **Choosing** (before ready, since Chromium takes its language only at start): a forced language (the `--lang` start option, or `HIVE_LANG` in unpackaged builds; it may be the pseudo-locale or a language without translations, and wins over the setting for that run), else **Settings → General → Language** (`general.language`: `auto`, the default, or a language's code; read from config.json directly, `config.savedLanguage()`), else the first of Windows' display languages (`app.getPreferredSystemLanguages()`) that Hive has translations for, matched exactly, then by language (de-AT → de, pt-PT → pt-BR; Chinese only Simplified: zh, zh-CN, zh-SG, zh-Hans, never zh-TW, zh-HK, zh-MO or zh-Hant), else English. The locale for formatting is the Windows tag that matched (en-GB, pl-PL), else the code; Chromium starts with it as `--lang` (the pseudo-locale as en-US). Main loads the chosen translations once and gives them to each window with `app:info` (`AppInfo.language`: the code, the locale, the language Chromium started in, what Automatic picks, the languages with translations, Windows' regional format, the forced language and the messages); the window applies them before its first render (`initLanguage`, awaited in `main.tsx`) and sets `<html lang dir>`. **Switching** is live for Hive's own text: a changed setting switches main (the latest request wins: a load still running when another request comes, even one back to the language shown, is dropped) and sends `language-changed` to every window and developer console, and `App` renders again on `useLanguage()`. Chromium's own parts (the PDF viewer, date pickers) follow at the next start, and the picker says so; it also says when a forced language wins. **The picker**: *Automatic (Windows: <language>)*, then each of Hive's languages by its own name, an AI-drafted one marked short ("Polski (AI)"), with the chosen one's AI note in its own words under the list; in any language but English the row's title adds its English ("Język (Language)", the catalogue row's `text.englishTitle`). **Agent-facing text stays English**: hive-mcp.js and hive-progress.js never import `shared/i18n` beyond `msg.ts` (a unit test walks their imports); the settings catalogue keeps its English for the Assistant's tools, while a row's `text` messages are what Settings shows (and searches, with the English); the Assistant sets `general.language` as a select (Automatic, or a language's English name or code). **The pseudo-locale** `en-XA` (start option or `HIVE_LANG` only): each message accented, about 30% longer and in brackets, its placeholders, plurals and tags kept, so text not converted yet shows plain. **Tests pin English**: `hiveEnv()` sets `HIVE_LANG=en`, so suites find the window by its English text and dates and numbers format alike on any Windows. **The installer** speaks Hive's eight languages (`installerLanguages` in `electron-builder.yml`), following Windows' display language without asking.
- **Sessions tab** (a tree since #239, like the Files tree): the project's sessions (Hive's, external, optionally archived) on the left as **provider → agent → session → its sub-sessions** (`shared/sessionTree.ts`), newest first; selecting one shows its transcript read-only on the right, under a **Ran in …** line. A session sits under the agent whose id it recorded, under that agent's current name; never an agent added later with the same name or worktree folder. Sessions record their agent's name too (`agentName`; older records don't have it), so a removed agent's branch is "Coder (removed)", and older ones' "Removed agent". Sessions without a recorded agent (adopted, external, or from 0.1) go under **Not from an agent**. Each session row has a badge saying where it ran (`sessionOrigin`): "Project folder" or "Worktree · hive/reviewer", with the full folder and the agent's id on hover (the **Ran in** line names the agent too); external sessions keep their *external* badge and the Assistant's conversations have neither a location nor an agent level (provider → conversation).
  - **Sub-sessions**: sessions a CLI started for another one, not conversations of their own: Codex's **guardian reviews** (in Approve for me it starts one to judge each action, saved as its own rollout) and other sub-agent sessions. The provider's adapter reads it from the transcript's first line, cached by file and mtime (Codex: `source.subagent`, `thread_source` other than `user`, `parent_thread_id`; Claude Code: `isSidechain` with the starting `sessionId`), as `SessionListItem.sub` (`{ parentId, kind }`, kept on the record when adopted; records adopted before this are checked from their transcript, or Hive's copy of it, and the result kept on them: when listed, and once per run when their project's agents are read, so resume targets never pick one). Besides the project folder, sub-sessions are looked for in the other folders its sessions ran in (agents' worktrees, folders sessions recorded, the Assistant's workspace folder), where only sub-sessions of listed sessions are taken; a session's transcript is looked for in the same folders when it is read, searched, exported, checked or adopted (`providerTranscript`). A Claude Code sub-agent's own transcript (its first message `isSidechain`) is read in full: only within a conversation are sidechain entries left out (`SidechainFilter`). They are shown, not hidden: each nests under the session that started it, which says how many ("3 guardian reviews"); one whose session isn't listed stays under its own agent, marked. Archived sessions take their sub-sessions with them. A sub-session is never an agent's resume target or last session, and never in a Resume picker (`resumeRecord` and the pickers skip `sub`).
  - **Counts and folding**: every branch shows its count (sessions, then "+ n" sub-sessions; the tooltip spells it out). Branches start folded except the focused agent's (the Assistant's: its provider's) and the branches down to the session shown; what the user opens or folds is remembered per project (`ui.sessionsTree`, by project path in lower case: up to 300 branches per project and 200 projects; saved one project at a time like the Skills tab's, #245). **Expand All** / **Collapse All** in the header and on every branch.
  - **Keyboard like Files**: ↑/↓, Home/End move (moving onto a session shows its transcript, onto a match opens it there), → opens a branch or goes into it, ← folds it or goes to the branch above, Enter folds or opens a branch, F2 renames, Delete deletes (a branch: Delete All).
  - **Branch actions** (right-click, or a row's ⋯): **Expand All**, **Collapse All**, **Archive All… (n)**, **Unarchive All (n)** (when archived ones show), **Delete All… (n)**, on every level (provider, agent, a session with its sub-sessions). One confirmation names the count and branch ("Delete 4 sessions under Claude (Claude Code)?"); `session:bulk` then does each session as one at a time would (all or nothing per session) and skips those in use, and the result says how many were done and which were skipped and why ("Deleted 2 sessions; 2 skipped: 1 running, 1 in use"). Archive All includes only Hive's sessions (an external one has nothing of Hive's to keep; an external sub-session goes with its archived session). Usage totals stay as they are.
  - **Can't resume**: a session that can't be resumed has a muted **can't resume** marker (a sub-session: its kind's badge) in the tree and over its transcript, and its Resume buttons and menu item are disabled with the reason as their tooltip (`shared/sessionResume.ts`): a sub-session; its provider turned off or not installed; no transcript left anywhere (neither the CLI's nor Hive's backup); for the Assistant, a conversation from another provider than it runs now; no agent can run it (a worktree no agent of its provider uses, or no room for another agent). A running one shows **Show** instead. A removed agent's session that another agent can resume says so ("Its agent Coder was removed: it resumes in Helper."). Where a session ran is separate from who resumes it now: **Resume in …** names that agent, and its menu gives each agent's location (project folder, or worktree and branch), with "running, its current session stops first" where it applies. The transcript is read from Claude Code's file or Hive's backup. Your messages and Claude's replies (Markdown) are shown in full; thinking and each tool call (one-line summary: the command's description, the file, the pattern…) collapse, with input and result on expand. Tool input/output over 4,000 characters is shortened, with **Show all**. Subagent work appears as its Agent tool call with the final report. Each compaction is an inline divider with trigger, size before and after, the first request's real size after it (including instructions and tools) and the collapsible summary. Slash commands and `!` commands show with their output. Pasted images show as thumbnails (the base64 stored in the transcript, loaded lazily) and open in a viewer that also shows where the image came from. The view opens at the latest message and loads the latest 200 items; earlier ones load as you scroll up (or click), keeping your place, and a search hit further back loads from just before it. A running session is followed (2-second poll of the appended bytes, while you are at the bottom) only with **Follow** switched on (off by default: Settings → Sessions → Follow running sessions); otherwise **Refresh** loads what is new. Search (Ctrl+F) looks through every session: names, dates, providers, agents and transcript text. It filters the tree to the sessions that match (and the sessions of matching sub-sessions), opens their branches, and lists each session's transcript matches under it; the line under the box counts sessions and matches. Clicking a match opens that transcript, expands the item and highlights the matches. Per-message Copy, **Export as Markdown** (tool calls, thinking and summaries as `<details>`), **Resume** for sessions that are not running — **Resume in ▾** to choose the agent when several work in the session's folder (by default the agent that last ran it, or another one that isn't running) — and **Show** for a running one, which opens that agent's terminal; plus Rename (a tag icon: the pencil means editing contents), Archive, Delete and Adopt. Each session row shows Rename, Delete and ⋯ on hover. Parsing is incremental and cached per transcript in main (`transcripts.ts`, `agents/conversation.ts`); blocks off screen use `content-visibility: auto`.
- **Attachments** (#387, Darren's decisions of 7 Oct 2026): any file can be attached to an agent's or the Assistant's prompt, by a paste (a clipboard image), a drop on its terminal, or Insert into Session. Every attachment reaches the CLI the same way, **as its path in the prompt** (a type the CLI doesn't read itself is not refused). Main decides per file (`session:planAttach`, then `session:attach`, `shared/attachments.ts`): a file from **outside the session's folder** (`state.cwd`: the project or worktree; the Assistant's is the workspace) is copied into the host's `.hive/attachments/<session id>/` (`run-<runId>` until the provider names the session, moved when it does), named `<time>_<its name>`, and the copy's path pasted; so is every **image**, from anywhere, so the Attachments tab keeps what was shown; a file **inside** the session's folder, one already in the attachments folder, and a folder are pasted as they are. Files over **20 MB** (`LARGE_ATTACHMENT_BYTES`) are confirmed first (one dialog for all of a drop's large files; cancelled, nothing is pasted or copied). Nothing attached is ever run: Hive copies it and pastes its path, and the Attachments tab opens in its program only images and PDFs. **Kinds** by extension: image, PDF, text (text, logs, data, code) and file (anything else). Each provider's descriptor lists the kinds its CLI reads **itself** (`capabilities.attachments`), checked against the installed CLIs in test homes (`tests/fixtures/attachments/matrix.json`, a unit test holds the descriptors to it): Claude Code images, PDFs and text (2.1.296: its Read tool returns a PDF as a document); Codex images and text (0.162.1: a PDF only as raw bytes through a shell); Copilot images and text (1.0.95: its view tool returns a PDF's raw bytes). **Chips** (`AttachmentChips`): while the prompt is written, a chip per attachment over the terminal's top right, its kind's icon, name and label ("PDF", or "PDF · by path" where the CLI doesn't read that kind itself; the tooltip says which and the path); × dismisses one (the path stays), and Enter in that terminal clears them. **Old folder** (`attachmentsFolder.ts`): a host's `.hive/images` is moved to `.hive/attachments` the first time anything uses the folder in a run (renamed whole, else file by file into the same session folders, a taken name getting " (moved)", recorded in `.hive/attachments/.moved.json` before the file moves (if the record can't be written, or is there but can't be read, which is never overwritten, the file stays at its old path, where old links find it first); only real files move, between real folders of the host's own; what can't move stays for the next run), and paths into the old folder still lead to the moved files in Hive: the `hive-img:` protocol and file links (`files:linkFiles`) resolve a gone old path through `movedAttachment()` (the renamed file when the move renamed it, else the same name under `attachments`). **Containment** (decision 4): every write and move into the attachments folder goes through `ownAttachmentsFolder()`, which checks that `.hive`, `attachments` and the session folder are real folders of the host's own (no junction or symbolic link, really inside the host) and refuses otherwise; new files are created exclusively (never over a file, or a link left in its place).
- **Attachments tab**: what was pasted or dropped into sessions (`.hive/attachments`), grouped by session, archived sessions last, under a one-line description of what it holds ("Files pasted or dropped into this project's agents' sessions, by session."; the Assistant's: "…into the Assistant, by conversation.", #255). Images as thumbnails, other files as their kind's icon and name; with more than one kind, filter buttons (All, Images, PDFs, Text, Other, each with its count; a filter whose kind is gone shows all again). Viewer with keyboard navigation: an image, else the file previewed read-only with the Files tab's previewers (`ReadOnlyPreview`: PDF, Markdown, CSV, and text otherwise, from `attachments:read`, up to 2 MB; a file of kind file, a binary or a larger one says there's no preview); insert into session, copy path, reveal, copy image (images), open (images and PDFs), delete to the Recycle Bin, and a group's name opens its session's transcript. Deleting (one attachment, or a session's group at once: `attachments:trashGroup`, all or none, whatever the filter shows) has the checks of deleting sessions (§ lifecycle, In use): the session is reserved and must not be running (`sessions.whileStopped`: it can't resume and paste meanwhile; Delete is greyed out for a running session's attachments, in the menu, the group's bin and the viewer, whose Delete says why and follows the one shown and its session starting or stopping, #258), none goes if another program has one open, and only what Hive keeps there: a `.hive/attachments` folder, group folder or file that is a link (junction or symbolic link) elsewhere is neither listed nor deleted through. Images and PDFs are served to the renderer through the `hive-img:` protocol, restricted to image and PDF files inside the workspace.
- **Resizable panes**: the sidebar, the list beside the Sessions, Files, Changes and Memory tabs and the Docs view, and the Markdown/HTML split view have a drag handle (orange on hover). Double-click resets to the default. Sizes are kept per pane, not per project, in the app config (`ui.panes`); list widths are limited so the main area keeps at least 320 px, and the split stays between 20% and 80%. However little room the side panels leave the main area, nothing in it paints over them (#227: `.main-area` clips, and so does each agent pane, so a narrow pane's buttons stay off its neighbour): the empty project page and the pane placeholders start at their edge and scroll in their own area when they don't fit (`safe center`), and under 280 px the project page keeps only its title, warnings and buttons.
- **Settings page**: JetBrains/VS Code style — searchable, category tree, every setting has a tooltip. **Project Settings** uses the same layout with its own categories (Claude Code, Sessions, Agents & Worktrees, Advanced) and search; overridden values are marked.
- **Files and Changes tabs** get a selector (Project folder / each worktree agent) when agents work in worktrees. For a worktree, Changes lists everything that differs from where its branch left the base branch (its commits and uncommitted edits, `git diff <merge-base>` plus untracked files), diffs against that point, and offers Merge.
- **Command palette** (Ctrl+Shift+P; **Go to Project**, Ctrl+P, lists projects only), toasts and notification centre.
- **Tips** (`shared/tips.ts`, `components/Tips.tsx`, `renderer/tips.ts`): about 25 one- or two-sentence tips, each with a group (Sessions, Agents, Board, Assistant, Files, Shortcuts), an optional command (**Try it**, shown when it can run now) and a user guide heading (**Learn more** opens the Docs view there). Shortcuts in a tip's text are `{key:<command>}`, shown with `commandKeybinding()`. A **card** bottom right above the status bar (toasts stack above it), never modal, hidden while a focused terminal lies under it. It and the toasts keep clear of buttons (`CornerPlacement`, `shared/corner.ts`): while the Assistant's panel is open they sit at the bottom right of the main area, left of the panel, and an ended or couldn't-start bar under them lifts them to just above it. The card: the tip, Try it, Learn more, **Next tip**, ✕ and **Don't show tips** (turns *Settings → General → Show a tip when Hive starts* off, with a toast pointing at Help → Tips…). **When**: on start, 4 s after the window loads, at most once a day (any window); and at four **moments**, each once (the first transcript over the size warning, the context over the Compact threshold, a project with a second agent, the first pasted or dropped image). **Which**: in list order, skipping those seen in the current round and those about something the user already does (they have run the tip's command, or its `knownBy` commands: every command run is noted), then a new round. The profile keeps it (`ui.tips`: seen, the day one last showed, moments used, commands run). Each change goes to main as that one change (a tip seen, the day, a moment, a command: `ui:changeTips`), applied to what is saved, every window's copy following by `tips-changed`, so one window's older copy can't undo another's (#266). A window starting up listens before it loads its snapshot (`ui:get`): the `tips-changed` and `ui-pref-changed` events that arrive before the snapshot is applied are handled again after it (`renderer/startupReplay.ts`, #295), so a change another window makes while it loads isn't undone by the older snapshot; `ui:set` doesn't write `tips`. **Help → Tips…**: a dialog with all tips by group, a search box, Try it and Learn more on each, and the setting. New user-visible features add a tip.
- **Failed loads** are never shown as empty results. A list, search or page whose load fails says so in its place, with the error and **Retry** (`LoadFailed`); one whose refresh fails keeps the last results under a note, "Could not refresh …; last updated 14:05", with the error on hover and **Retry** (`StaleNote`). Loading, empty, failed and stale look different. What a view loaded belongs to the scope it was loaded for (the project, the workspace, the search and its query: `useScopedLoad`): after a switch the new scope shows loading, then its own results or its own error, never the last scope's results (as stale or otherwise), and a load that finishes after the view moved on, or after a newer load started, is dropped. This covers the Sessions tab (list and search), the Overviews, the Skills, Attachments, Memory and MCP tabs, the Files tab's filter, the workspace's Skills view and Settings → Workspace's hidden projects; Add Agent says when git couldn't be read rather than that the project isn't a repository. Calls that only add to a view (git colours in the Files tree, live file watching, transcript images, personas) stay quiet when they fail.
- **Keyboard shortcuts** are configurable. Settings → Keyboard Shortcuts lists every command with its shortcut, its category, where it comes from (default, changed, removed), as a data table on one page (#250: sort by a header; filter by Category and by where it is set; the search box narrows it too), conflicts (a warning, and a question before saving one) and keys that go to Claude Code while the terminal has focus. A shortcut is recorded by pressing it; a second combination within 1.2 s makes a chord. Refused: keys without Ctrl or Alt (except F-keys), Ctrl+C/V/X/A/Z/Y and Shift+Tab. Overrides are stored in `settings.keybindings` (`null` removes a default). **Project Settings → Keyboard Shortcuts** overrides the Project and Session commands for that project only, in `.hive/project.json` (`keybindings`), over the global ones. Keys are matched by physical key (`KeyboardEvent.code`), so Shift and AltGr don't change a combination's name. Defaults besides the existing ones: Ctrl+P Go to Project, Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+Shift+Tab next/previous project tab, Ctrl+1…9 focus agent N, Ctrl+Alt+1…5 layouts (of the page on screen), Ctrl+Alt+PageDown / PageUp next/previous agent page, Ctrl+Alt+M permission mode, Ctrl+Alt+C Compact, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N Add Agent, Ctrl+` focus the session terminal, Ctrl+Shift+` external terminal, Ctrl+Alt+U notifications, Ctrl+Alt+P Progress panel, Ctrl+Shift+J Task Board, Ctrl+Shift+O Workspace Overview, Ctrl+Alt+T New Card (Go to Tasks has none, so the project tabs keep Alt+1…0). Help → Keyboard Shortcuts shows the current ones and links to the editor.
- **Providers**: Settings → Providers enables and disables each provider (disabling one with running agents asks whether to stop them now or let them run) and sets the default provider. **Automatic** (`auto`, #474) is the default: new agents (Add Agent, the quick add, New Session, the Board's Start, the Agent API, a new template agent, the Skills tab's provider, the Assistant when it follows the default) use the first provider in `PROVIDERS` order that is turned on and installed, else the first turned on, else Claude Code (`autoProvider`; each process tells `shared/providers.ts` what it found, `setInstalledCheck`: main's providerService, the window's store). A provider chosen in Settings or a project's own default always wins (`chosenDefaultProvider`). Config version 8 turned a saved Claude Code default into Automatic: it was the default saved into every config up to 0.4.x, so it can't be told from a choice, and Automatic still picks Claude Code wherever it is turned on and installed; Codex and Copilot defaults stayed. A project with no agents warns (`defaultProviderWarning`) only about a default the user chose that is turned off or not installed (by name), or, with Automatic, when no provider is turned on or none turned on is installed; a CLI nobody chose is never named. **Add Agent** keeps a chosen default selected even when it is turned off, says so (and whose default it is), and adds nothing until it is turned on or another provider is chosen, as the quick add does. The same rule holds for the providers banner, the status bar and Agent Setup opening by itself (`providerWarnings` with `providersInUse`): they are about providers turned on **and in use** (what new agents use: Settings' default, a project's own, Automatic's pick; and any agent's own provider in the window's workspace, the Assistant's included). When none of those turned on is installed, one banner says so instead (Agent Setup at the first one). A provider only turned on that isn't installed shows plainly in the status bar ("not installed", no warning). Nothing is decided while Hive is still looking for a provider it hasn't found (at start), since Automatic's pick isn't known yet. **Automatic never resolves from partial detection** (#506): detection answers per CLI and in any order, so Automatic is only decided once **every turned-on provider has answered**, found or missing (`autoProviderPending`). Until then `autoProvider` is a guess for display only (the first turned-on provider not known to be missing: never a later one because it answered first). A CLI counts as found as soon as it is located, before its version, models and sign-in are read. What commits to Automatic's pick waits for it: main's `providerService.settle()` (at most 20 s) before a new conversation of an agent following Automatic, an agent added without a provider (Add Agent, the Agent API) and any launch whose CLI hasn't answered (a launch of a known provider waits only for that CLI, never for another); if Hive is still looking after that, it is **refused** ("Hive is still looking for … Try again in a moment."), never decided from the guess, and nothing is saved or started. `hive_list_providers` waits the same way and, still looking, marks no provider `isDefault` unless one was chosen. In the window, the quick add, New Session and Resume wait the same way before deciding (rather than opening Agent Setup for a CLI not answered yet) and, still looking, say so and do nothing; Add Agent shows *Checking which coding agents are installed…* and holds Add until it is known, unless the user picks a provider. Settings shows **Automatic (checking…)** meanwhile. Tests only (unpackaged builds): `HIVE_TEST_SLOW_DETECT="<provider>=<ms>"` holds one provider's detection and `HIVE_TEST_DETECT_WAIT_MS` shortens the wait (`providerdetect`).
- **A resume runs its conversation's own provider** (#506, Darren's decision). Resuming a conversation reopens it in the provider that recorded it (one Hive has no record of, started outside Hive: the provider whose CLI has its transcript), in whichever agent works in the folder it ran in, even if that agent's provider (its own, or a default it follows: the Assistant's, from Settings → Assistant or Assistant Settings) has changed since; new conversations use the agent's provider now. A conversation of another provider than the agent's runs with the project's settings for its provider (the agent's own model, effort, mode and context are its current provider's). If that CLI is turned off or not installed, the refusal names it. While it runs, a change of the agent's provider or a default asks for no restart: it is for the next new conversation. Resume (the agent's last conversation) is still its current provider's last: changing an agent's provider clears it. A conversation still never changes provider (§15, 22): Hand Over to… moves work between providers. `hive_list_providers`' `isDefault` is what new agents use now. Each provider has its own settings page (path, default model, effort and permission mode, dangerous-mode option, extra arguments, update checks, price table). **Allow background sessions** (providers with `capabilities.backgroundSessions`: Claude Code) is off by default: Hive starts Claude Code with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AGENT_VIEW=1`, because ← on an empty prompt opens its agent view and moves the session into Claude Code's background service, where Hive can't see or stop it (Stop only closes the terminal, and resuming fails until `claude stop`). The user's own Claude Code settings are not changed. If a resume exits because the CLI holds the conversation in a background job (it names the job: `claude attach <id>`), Hive shows a notification with **Stop It and Resume**, which runs `claude stop <id>` and resumes the conversation in the agent (`backgroundJobIn()`/`stopBackgroundJob()` on the adapter; nothing else is listed or stopped). Provider icons show on agent tabs, pane headers, the Sessions list, pickers, settings, Agent Setup, About and the status bar. They are **Hive's own badges, never a provider's logo** (#470: the logo owners' terms need their permission): an outline, a glyph and a colour per provider (Claude Code a coral circle with a tilde, Codex a teal rounded square with braces, GitHub Copilot a violet diamond with a double chevron), always with the provider's name or as its tooltip. The status bar draws them in its text colour with the glyph cut out, so the outline tells them apart (`ProviderIcon.tsx`). A banner shows while no provider is enabled, none enabled is installed, or one in use needs setup (#474, above).
- **Status bar**: workspace, branch, session counts, the attention inbox's **n need you** (§14), plan usage per provider (one item each: Claude Code's 5-hour and weekly %, Codex's windows; reset times on hover; darker at 80%, red at 95%), each enabled provider's CLI version. Only app-wide items: an agent's model, effort, permission mode and context are in its pane footer (effort as the running session reports it, else the configured one; model shown as what actually runs (#248): the running session's reported model (Claude Code's status line, else its transcript; Codex's rollout), else what the choice resolves to (an alias by the model the CLI says it stands for: "Opus" shows as "Opus 5.5", the choice in the tooltip, "Opus 5.5 · chosen as Opus"), named as the CLI lists that id, else as the provider formats it; never guessed: an alias the CLI doesn't list shows as named. A project or agent choice shows plainly, an inherited one with "(default)"; Claude Code's own default is named from its settings' `model` (an alias there resolved the same way) or the model last seen in a session started without `--model`, else "Claude Code default"), Agent API state, Hive's own version (opens About; dev builds show "Hive Desktop Dev"), notifications.
- **About** dialog (versions, data folder, update status with Check Now, licence links: Hive's MIT License and the third-party notices open in the Docs view, the Chromium licences file opens from the install folder), **Docs** view (user guide, Agent API reference, release notes, licence, third-party notices, shortcuts), first-run Claude Code setup.

### 13.1 Colour scheme

VS Code's dark neutrals with **honey orange** replacing VS Code blue as the accent.

| Token | Dark | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Accent | `#F59E0B` | focus, selection, primary buttons, active indicators |
| Accent strong | `#D97706` | status bar, pressed states |
| Background | `#1E1E1E` / `#252526` / `#2D2D30` | editor / sidebar / panels |
| Info | `#3B9EFF` | links, informational badges |
| Success / Warning / Error | `#4EC96B` / `#F5C451` / `#F2555A` | status dots, bypass warning |
| Sign-in | `#B48CFF` (light `#8250DF`) | the needs sign-in dot and badge |

A light theme uses the same accent with VS Code Light neutrals.

---

## 14. Tray, notifications and sound

- Close and minimise go to the tray (each configurable). Quit via tray or File → Exit.
- **Quitting** stops all sessions (they stay resumable). Setting *Confirm before quitting*: **When an agent is working** (default; working, waiting on a prompt, or waiting on background tasks), **Whenever sessions are running**, or **Never** (the earlier on/off setting migrates to working/never). The confirmation is an in-app dialog (the window is shown first) listing each session with its status, marking the ones that would be interrupted, with *Don't ask again* (about sessions), *Cancel*, *Quit when agents finish* (only when an agent is working) and *Quit now*. Files with unsaved edits in the Files tab are listed at the top and always make Hive ask, whatever the setting, with the choice to save or discard them (the buttons read *Save and quit…* when saving). No native message boxes.
- **Quit when agents finish**: Hive hides, the tray tooltip and menu show the pending quit (*Quit Now*, *Cancel Pending Quit*), a banner shows it if the window is opened, and Hive quits as soon as no agent is working or waiting on background tasks.
- Quitting without a dialog (or after waiting) shows a notification that the stopped sessions can be resumed. Shutdown waits up to 3 s for each session's final transcript backup.
- **Sleep** (`main/power.ts`, `shared/keepAwake.ts`): while any agent in any window (the Assistant too) is **working** or **waiting on background tasks**, Hive holds `powerSaveBlocker.start('prevent-app-suspension')` (the screen may still turn off and lock) and releases it as soon as none is. **Settings → General → Keep the PC awake while agents work**: *When plugged in* (default; not on battery, `powerMonitor.isOnBatteryPower()`, re-checked on `on-ac` / `on-battery`), *Always, on battery too*, or *Never*. While it's held the status bar shows **Keeping the PC awake: n agents working** (click: Settings → General).
- **Windows shutting down** (shutdown, restart or sign-out): Windows' callbacks (a window's `query-session-end`, then `session-end`; `powerMonitor` `shutdown` elsewhere) don't wait for anything asynchronous, so Hive backs up every running session's transcript before the callback returns: the transcript's bytes only (appended to the backup, or copied whole), no reading of usage or cost. It has 2 s at `query-session-end` and 1 s at `session-end` (which catches up with what was written since), well inside the ~5 s after which Windows offers to end an app, and never cancels the shutdown. It copies 1 MB at a time and starts nothing once its time is up; a step under way can't be cut short, so it can run over by about one disk write. Out of time, a whole copy is dropped (the backup stays as it was) and an append stops where it got to (a shorter backup that is still the transcript's start); sessions after it aren't tried. Every window gets these notices: one save covers them all. A shutdown another app cancels leaves nothing to undo. Best effort: a session whose transcript Hive hasn't found yet keeps its last ordinary backup, and the log counts what was saved, incomplete, not tried or failed. An ordinary backup under way at the time doesn't undo it: appends write each byte at its own offset, and a whole copy is put in place only if the backup hasn't changed since it began, checked and renamed in one synchronous step (retries too), so no rename is left pending across the shutdown copy.
- **Waking up** (`powerMonitor` `resume`): each running session's transcript is read again (usage, background tasks and their time limit, plan usage from it) and its state reported; the workspaces refresh. Logged.
- **Attention inbox**: one list of the window's agents that need you, across its projects and the Assistant: agents **waiting** for input and agents with a **pending question** they work on beside (Codex's async question; *Asks: …*), both until answered, looked at or not (`asksYou()`), and agents **finished, unseen**. An agent is seen once its pane is on screen in a focused window: the selected project's panes on its Session tab (the page shown), or the Assistant's open panel. A finish on screen is seen as it happens; one in another project, on another page or tab, or while the window is unfocused or minimised stays unseen until its pane is shown. Main marks every finish and wait unseen and the window marks what it shows seen (`session:markSeen` with the agents' ids, from `agentsOnScreen()`), so main never needs to know what is on screen. Each agent's state records when it last changed (`statusSince`), and the list is oldest first, saying how long ago.
